344 — Deterministic Replay System from Scratch

Project Number: 344

Project Name: Deterministic Replay System from Scratch

Owner: Mujir Qureshi

Document: FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md

Status: specification — locked

1. Goal

1.1 What Is Built

A deterministic record/replay engine for native Linux x86-64 processes: the dret system captures every source of nondeterminism in a running program (syscall arguments and results, signal delivery, thread scheduling, hardware time-stamp reads, CPUID) into a compact on-disk trace, then re-executes the program offline so it reproduces bit-for-bit. The replay is stable across machines and across time. A gdbserver stub layered on replay enables reverse-step and reverse-continue without re-recording.

1.2 System Boundaries

In scope Out of scope
Linux x86-64 only ARM, x86-32, Windows, macOS
Native ELF processes Interpreted VMs (JVM, V8)
Single-host supervisor Distributed tracing across hosts
User-space tracer via ptrace Kernel-mode or hypervisor recording
Threads, signals, futex, vDSO GPU instructions, DMA, NIC hardware
gdbserver reverse-debug Full GDB front-end / IDE integration
Replay offline (no live network) Live migration of replay sessions
x86 rdtsc/cpuid trapping SSE/AVX nondeterminism beyond timing
Trace forward-compatibility Backward-compat: old recorder on new reader

1.3 Non-Goals

  • Cross-architecture portability layer.
  • Kernel patch or eBPF: this system uses only unprivileged ptrace + perf_event_open.
  • Userspace fault injection / chaos testing (separate concern).
  • Record system calls with > 6 pointer-range memory effects not enumerable in the ABI table.
  • GUI debugger; the deliverable is a headless gdbserver stub.
  • Production-traffic recording at zero overhead; 2–20x slowdown under record is accepted.

1.4 Intended Deployment Model

  • Developer workstation or CI runner (Linux x86-64, kernel ≥ 4.4).
  • Single binary dret (via dret-cli) invoked as supervisor; tracee is any ordinary ELF.
  • Record: dret record -- ./target [args] → emits a trace directory.
  • Replay / debug: dret replay --trace ./trace-dir [--port 1234] → waits for GDB attach.
  • dret dump, dret pack, dret ps for inspection and archival.
  • No daemon, no persistent service, no privileged helper.

1.5 Target Users / Operators

Persona Primary use
Systems / kernel developer Debug nondeterministic crashes, data races
CI infrastructure engineer Capture rare flaky-test failures; share reproducers
Security researcher Deterministic malware analysis; replay on isolated machine
Compiler / runtime author Regression-bisect across compiler versions
Portfolio / learning engineer Understand rr / TTD internals by building them

1.6 Language Rationale (Rust, locked)

Low-level ptrace(2) supervision, perf_event_open hardware-counter reads, seccomp-BPF installation, and direct /proc/pid/mem I/O require C-level control: raw syscall numbers, pointer-sized integer casting, register-file struct overlays, and page-aligned allocation. A recorder that mishandles memory corrupts every trace it writes; a replayer that mishandles memory injects wrong bytes silently. Rust gives the required control with memory safety (no UB in safe code, no use-after-free in the supervisor loop), strong enums for the event taxonomy (EventKind, SyscallRecord, DivergenceReport), and zero-cost FFI to libc syscalls. The type system enforces that TraceWriter is append-only and that ReplaySession never calls a real syscall during injection.

1.7 Target Platform (locked)

Linux x86-64. Required kernel interfaces: ptrace(2), PTRACE_GETREGSET/SETREGSET, PTRACE_SYSEMU, PTRACE_SETOPTIONS (TRACESYSGOOD/TRACECLONE/TRACEFORK/TRACEEXEC/EXITKILL), seccomp(2) SECCOMP_RET_TRACE, perf_event_open(2) PERF_COUNT_HW_BRANCH_INSTRUCTIONS, /proc/pid/mem, /proc/pid/maps, sched_setaffinity(2), signalfd(2). No portability layer is in scope.

1.8 Phase 0 Deliverables — Locked Crates

The following crates/modules are defined at project inception and must compile (even stub) before Phase 1 begins:

  1. dret-core — shared types: EventId, Ticks, TaskId, EventKind, RecordedRegs, MemRange; no I/O; no OS dependencies.
  2. dret-ptrace — safe wrappers: ptrace(2), PTRACE_SETOPTIONS, GETREGSET/SETREGSET, PTRACE_CONT/SYSEMU/SINGLESTEP, waitpid demux.
  3. dret-syscall — x86-64 syscall ABI tables; argument/return marshalling; pointer-arg memory-effect descriptors.
  4. dret-time — deterministic clock via perf_event_open; RCB counting; ticks-to-event mapping; overcount correction.
  5. dret-mem — /proc/pid/mem reads/writes; COW page snapshots; page-diff deltas; AddressSpace map.
  6. dret-sched — deterministic user-space scheduler; one logical CPU; tick-bounded preemption; run-queue; futex ordering.
  7. dret-trace — on-disk trace container: events.log / data.log / mmap.log / checkpoints; framing; CRC; versioned header; TraceWriter/TraceReader.
  8. dret-record — recorder driver: launches tracee; drives supervisor loop; writes trace.
  9. dret-replay — replayer driver: re-executes tracee on one CPU; injects recorded results at exact ticks.
  10. dret-divergence — self-checking replay: per-event register + tick + memory-hash comparison vs recorded; DivergenceReport.
  11. dret-gdb — gdbserver RSP stub: reverse-step / reverse-continue / watchpoints over a replay session.
  12. dret-cli — dret record | replay | dump | pack | ps subcommands; entry point.

1.9 What Later Phases Unlock

Phase Capability unlocked
Phase 1 ptrace supervisor skeleton; single-step harness
Phase 2 Syscall interception; ABI table; passthrough recording
Phase 3 Trace container; disk persistence; TraceReader/Writer
Phase 4 Deterministic RCB clock; addressable tick positions
Phase 5 Memory capture; page snapshots; AddressSpace tracking
Phase 6 Single-threaded end-to-end replay
Phase 7 Deterministic user-space scheduler; preemption
Phase 8 Multi-threaded record + replay; futex ordering
Phase 9 Signal capture; deterministic async delivery
Phase 10 rdtsc/cpuid/vDSO interception
Phase 11 Checkpointing; fast seek; reverse execution substrate
Phase 12 Divergence detection; self-checking corpus
Phase 13 gdbserver stub; reverse-step / reverse-continue
Phase 14 Trace packing; compression; fuzz hardening; conformance

1.10 Deferred Features

  • Kernel-assisted recording (eBPF ring buffer): PhaseDeferred("Phase 14").
  • Multi-machine distributed trace correlation: PhaseDeferred("Phase 14").
  • ARM64 / RISC-V architecture support: PhaseDeferred("Phase 14").
  • Automatic source-line mapping in divergence reports (requires DWARF integration): PhaseDeferred("Phase 13").
  • JIT-compiled language support (V8, JVM): PhaseDeferred("Phase 14").

1.11 Timeline Estimates

Horizon Scope
Phase 0 (workspace, stubs, CI) 1–2 weeks
Phases 1–3 (supervisor + trace I/O) 4–6 weeks
Phases 4–6 (clock, memory, single-thread replay) 6–10 weeks

Full system ~9–15 months

1.12 Required Prerequisites

Area Specific knowledge required
Linux ptrace(2) PTRACE_SYSCALL stop semantics; entry/exit stop distinction; orig_rax; PTRACE_SETOPTIONS bitmask
x86-64 ABI Syscall calling convention (rax/rdi/rsi/rdx/r10/r8/r9/rax-ret); register file layout; fs_base/gs_base TLS
Linux process model fork/clone/exec/waitpid semantics; SIGCHLD; zombie reaping; thread groups; TGID vs TID
seccomp-BPF SECCOMP_RET_TRACE; BPF filter program structure; interaction with ptrace
perf_event_open PERF_COUNT_HW_BRANCH_INSTRUCTIONS; per-task file descriptor; PERF_IOC_RESET/ENABLE; overflow signal SIGIO/SIGUSR1
Memory management /proc/pid/maps parsing; /proc/pid/mem pread; mmap/mprotect/mremap/munmap semantics; COW semantics
Signals Synchronous vs asynchronous signal taxonomy; si_code values; PTRACE_GETSIGINFO/SETSIGINFO; signalfd
Futex FUTEX_WAIT/WAKE; kernel-space vs user-space fast path; recording total-order
vDSO / vsyscall ELF auxiliary vector AT_SYSINFO_EHDR; vDSO symbol resolution; vsyscall page; clock_gettime fast path
gdb RSP Remote serial protocol packet format; 'g'/'G'/'m'/'M'/'s'/'c'/'?'; reverse-s/reverse-c extensions

2. Why Build This

2.1 Systems Knowledge This Teaches

Domain Specific lesson
OS internals ptrace lifecycle end-to-end; supervisor/tracee trust boundary; every syscall stop variant
Hardware counters perf_event_open PMU programming; RCB vs cycle counters; skid measurement and correction
Concurrency Why thread interleavings are nondeterministic; how serialization under one logical CPU eliminates them
Memory systems COW semantics; page-granularity dirty tracking; /proc/pid/mem as a debugging interface
Debugger internals How reverse execution works mechanically (checkpoint + replay-forward); how GDB RSP maps to replay ops
Trace format design Forward-compatible framing; CRC integrity; seekable event streams; delta vs snapshot trade-off
x86 architecture TSC and CPUID as nondeterminism sources; TSD bit; fs_base/gs_base in multi-thread TLS
Security model seccomp-BPF as a syscall filter; why a hostile tracee cannot escape a well-written supervisor

2.2 Historical and Architectural Importance

rr (Mozilla, 2014–present) demonstrated that software-only record/replay is viable in production at ~1.5–2x overhead by exploiting a single insight: retire one logical CPU per replay so thread interleavings need not be replayed, only recorded. Before rr, systems like ReVirt (King & Chen 2002) required hypervisor support. Microsoft TTD/iDNA (Bhansali 2006) required kernel drivers. The rr approach is purely user-space on stock kernels, making it deployable on any developer machine. Building dret from scratch forces understanding of why that insight is sufficient, where it breaks (SMT, async signals, vDSO), and how each break is patched.

Pernosco demonstrated that deterministic replay combined with omniscient debugging changes the economics of bug investigation from "reproduce and guess" to "query the execution history." This is the architectural payoff of the record-once / replay-many model (P2).

2.3 Engineering Muscles Developed

  • Supervisor loop design: event dispatch, state machine per tracee thread, error recovery without UB.
  • Low-level Rust FFI: raw pointer operations in safe wrappers; unsafe scope minimization; libc binding discipline.
  • Data format discipline: append-only logs with integrity checks; versioned schemas; reader/writer separation.
  • Correctness-first engineering: divergence as a first-class signal (P6); self-checking replay corpus.
  • Performance measurement and trade-off: ptrace overhead per event; batching strategies; when not to snapshot.
  • Reverse execution: the checkpoint-seek-forward loop; why O(distance) is acceptable; watchpoint emulation.

2.4 Production Engineering Concepts Exercised

Concept How exercised here
Observability Trace is the structured log; dret dump is the query interface
Reproducibility The entire point: bug is reproducible offline months later
Crash-only design (P7) Recorder leaves replayable prefix on SIGKILL
Forward compatibility (P8) Versioned, tagged frames; unknown tags skipped
Least-privilege (P9) Supervisor is TCB; tracee is untrusted; seccomp limits tracee syscall surface
Graceful degradation Partial trace replays up to last good checkpoint

2.5 Cross-Links to Related Portfolio Projects

Related project Intersection with dret
ptrace-based debugger Phase 1 supervisor skeleton is a debugger harness; register read/write; breakpoints
ELF loader / linker Phase 10 vDSO / vsyscall interception requires ELF auxiliary vector parsing
Syscall sandbox (seccomp) Phase 2 SECCOMP_RET_TRACE filter; BPF program generation
File system / I/O subsystem Phase 5 /proc/pid/mem; Phase 3 trace container I/O
Scheduler / OS kernel Phase 7 deterministic scheduler mirrors kernel run-queue logic in user space
Compiler / code generator Phase 10 CPUID interception; rdtsc timing; instruction-level traps
Database (WAL / append-only log) Phase 3 trace container mirrors WAL design: append, fsync, CRC, reader cursor
Fuzzer / property-based tester Phase 14 fuzz the trace reader; corpus-based conformance testing

2.6 Calibration Value

A developer who builds dret can reason about any user-space debugger, profiler, sandbox, or replay system at the implementation level, not just the API level. The RCB-as-clock insight, once understood by building it, makes hardware-counter-based profiling trivially comprehensible. The checkpoint+replay-forward pattern is the same pattern used in database point-in-time recovery, snapshot isolation, and game-state rewind. The trace container design (framed append log, forward-compatible tags, CRC) applies directly to distributed system event logs, Kafka topics, and WAL files. Building dret is the fastest path to principal-level understanding of the Linux execution model.

2.7 What Mastery Looks Like

After completing this project, the practitioner holds command of the following exact competencies:

  • ptrace supervisor engineering (Phases 1–2): can design and audit any user-space debugger, sandbox, or process-control system; understands every stop variant, the TRACESYSGOOD/SYSEMU distinction, and the trust-boundary implications of each ptrace operation.
  • Hardware-counter clock design (Phase 4): can select, configure, and validate PMU events for deterministic program ordering; understands RCB skid physics, overflow-interrupt timing, and when RCB is and is not a safe proxy for instruction count.
  • Memory-system internals (Phase 5): can implement COW-aware page capture using /proc/pid/mem, pagemap soft-dirty bits, and pread/pwrite injection; understands the TOCTOU constraints that govern when each read is valid.
  • Deterministic multi-thread serialization (Phases 7–8): can reason about why single-CPU serialization is sufficient for atomics and futex correctness; can implement and audit a tick-bounded preemptive scheduler over ptrace without in-tracee instrumentation.
  • Append-only fault-tolerant storage (Phase 3, 11): can design CRC-framed, forward-compatible, crash-safe event logs; the same pattern applies to Kafka topics, LevelDB WALs, and distributed-system journals.
  • Reverse-execution substrate (Phase 11, 13): can implement checkpoint-seek-forward, binary search over replay, and watchpoint scanning; understands why O(distance) replay-forward cost is acceptable and how checkpoint granularity trades storage against seek latency.
  • Security-model reasoning at the TCB level (Phase 2, 9, P9): can evaluate a supervisor's attack surface against a hostile tracee; can reason about seccomp-BPF filter chains, signal delivery races, and the minimal trust boundary required for safe recording.
  • Trace fidelity and divergence diagnosis (Phase 12, 14): can design a self-checking replay corpus, write bisection tooling, and systematically root-cause nondeterminism by event class — skills that transfer directly to any distributed-system linearizability or causal-consistency debugging problem.

3. Architectural Overview

3.1 Canonical Architecture Diagram

                          dret record   (supervisor / tracer process)
  +---------------------------------------------------------------------------+
  |  dret-cli record                                                          |
  |     |                                                                     |
  |     v        ptrace + seccomp-BPF                                         |
  |  dret-record ===============> TRACEE process  [thread 1 .. thread N]      |
  |     |  ^                              |  syscall / signal / rdtsc / cpuid |
  |     |  | trap (PTRACE_SYSCALL,        |  trap                             |
  |     |  |  seccomp SECCOMP_RET_TRACE)  v                                   |
  |     v  |                       dret-sched  (1 logical CPU; preempt at     |
  |  dret-syscall (ABI decode,            tick boundary; serialize threads)   |
  |     |  arg + ret + mem effects)       |                                   |
  |     v                                 v                                   |
  |  dret-time (RCB ticks, perf_event_open) <----- per-task logical clock     |
  |     |                                                                     |
  |     v                                                                     |
  |  dret-mem (COW page snapshot, /proc/pid/mem, page diff)                   |
  |     |                                                                     |
  |     v                                                                     |
  |  dret-trace  ====>  TRACE DIR { events.log | data.log | mmap.log | ckpt/ }|
  +---------------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                     |
                                     v   (offline; deterministic; replay-many)
  +---------------------------------------------------------------------------+
  |  dret replay                                                              |
  |  dret-replay  <====  dret-trace      re-exec TRACEE on ONE logical CPU    |
  |     |   inject recorded syscall ret + mem effects + signals at exact tick |
  |     v                                                                     |
  |  dret-divergence  (per-event reg + tick + mem-hash vs recorded -> abort)  |
  |     |                                                                     |
  |     v                                                                     |
  |  dret-gdb  (gdbserver RSP: reverse-step / reverse-continue / watchpoint)  |
  +---------------------------------------------------------------------------+

3.2 Subsystem Descriptions

dret-cli — entry point; parses subcommand (record, replay, dump, pack, ps); validates arguments; constructs RecordSession or ReplaySession; owns process lifetime; propagates exit codes. Trust boundary: the only component with direct user input; validates paths and permissions before delegating.

dret-record (supervisor loop) — forks tracee under PTRACE_TRACEME; installs seccomp-BPF filter; drives the waitpid(2) event loop; dispatches each stop (syscall entry, syscall exit, signal-delivery, clone, exec, death) to dret-syscall / dret-sched / dret-time / dret-mem for annotation; writes each TraceFrame to dret-trace. Data in: ptrace stops (waitpid status words). Data out: TraceFrame stream to dret-trace. Concurrency boundary: single OS thread in the supervisor; tracee threads are individually stopped and resumed under PTRACE_CONT or PTRACE_SYSEMU. Does not hold any GFDs that the tracee will inherit.

TRACEE process [thread 1..N] — the untrusted program under observation. Constrained by seccomp-BPF to emit SECCOMP_RET_TRACE on a selected syscall subset. Never modified in memory by the recorder except for vsyscall page patching (Phase 10). Each thread has a separate ptrace attachment, a separate Ticks counter, and a separate TraceWriter stream segment.

dret-sched — implements the single-logical-CPU abstraction during recording. Maintains a run-queue of (TaskId, Ticks, state) tuples. At each SchedPoint it picks the next thread deterministically (by TaskId, breaking ties by ticks). Records SchedSwitch events so replay can apply identical switch order. Intercepts futex FUTEX_WAIT/WAKE to record the total ordering of futex operations. On replay, enforces I6: exactly one task runnable at any instant.

dret-syscall — owns the x86-64 syscall ABI table (syscall number → name, arg types, pointer-range effects, return semantics). At syscall-entry stop: reads rax (nr), rdi/rsi/rdx/r10/r8/r9 (args); classifies pointer args; computes MemRange list for args that name memory written by the kernel. At syscall-exit stop: reads rax (ret) and reconstructs errno. Returns SyscallRecord for dret-record to serialize. Pure decode logic; no OS calls beyond ptrace register reads.

dret-time — manages one perf_event_open file descriptor per tracked task, configured for PERF_COUNT_HW_BRANCH_INSTRUCTIONS (retired conditional branches). Reads counter via ioctl PERF_EVENT_IOC_RESET/ioctl at each ptrace stop. Provides read_ticks(tid) → Ticks. Implements overcount/skid correction: after a hardware overflow interrupt, adjusts the Ticks value backward by the measured skid. The Ticks value is the sole addressing mechanism for positions in the trace; it must be monotonically non-decreasing per task (I2).

dret-mem — reads tracee memory via /proc/pid/mem pread(2) after each syscall-exit stop, for ranges named in MemRange list from dret-syscall. Produces MemDelta { range, bytes_ref } written into data.log via DataRef. Manages COW page snapshots for Checkpoint: on checkpoint request, reads all dirty pages since last checkpoint, hashes each page, stores (MemRange, PageRef) list. On replay, provides pwrite(2) injection of recorded page contents.

dret-trace — the persistence layer. events.log: fixed-size framed records (EventId u64, tid u32, ticks u64, kind u8, regs[144 bytes], data_ref u64, crc32 u32). data.log: variable-length payloads at DataRef offsets (memory snapshots, checkpoint dirty pages). mmap.log: address-space change records (mmap/munmap/mremap/brk/exec). ckpt/: one directory per checkpoint, named by EventId. All writes are append-only; fsync at checkpoint boundaries (P7). Forward-compatible: unknown field tags skipped by older readers (P8).

dret-replay — reads TraceReader; re-execs the same binary under ptrace PTRACE_SYSEMU; at each PTRACE_SYSEMU stop, injects the recorded syscall result (write rax = ret; pwrite memory effects into tracee via /proc/pid/mem; call PTRACE_SETSIGINFO for recorded signals). Consumes events in strict EventId order (I3). Enforces I4: injected ret == recorded ret. Enforces I5: no real syscall executes during injection. After each event, delegates to dret-divergence for register/tick/mem-hash comparison.

dret-divergence — compares (RecordedRegs, Ticks, touched-page SHA-256) from replay execution vs stored values in TraceFrame. On first mismatch, emits DivergenceReport and aborts replay (P6). Provides bisect mode: binary search on EventId range to find first divergent event. Used in Phase 12 to validate the entire replay pipeline against a conformance corpus.

dret-gdb — listens on a TCP port for GDB remote serial protocol connections. Maps gdb 'c' (continue) → replay-forward; 's' (step) → replay single event; 'bs' (reverse-step) → seek to (event_id − 1) via checkpoint rewind + replay-forward; 'bc' (reverse-continue) → binary-search backward for watchpoint/breakpoint hit. Exposes register reads ('g'), memory reads ('m'), and breakpoints ('Z0') as views into the current replay position. Has no write-back path to the trace; all mutations are in replay session state only.

4. System Constraints

4.1 Quantified Engineering Targets

Constraint Target Notes
Record overhead 2–20x wall-clock slowdown Acceptable per P1; ptrace round-trip is ~1–3 µs/syscall
Replay overhead 5–30x wall-clock vs native PTRACE_SYSEMU + injection per event
Trace size / memory written ≤ 10× bytes written by program Baseline: 1 MB program memory writes → ≤ 10 MB trace
Trace size / clock ticks ≤ 64 bytes / traced event average Fixed frame 64 B + variable data.log payload
events.log frame size 72 bytes fixed header + DataRef EventId(8) + tid(4) + ticks(8) + kind(1) + regs(144) + data_ref(8) + crc(4) = 177 B → align to 192
Max concurrent tracee threads 256 threads TaskId(u32) supports more; scheduler run-queue O(N)
Max trace duration 2^64 events; 2^64 ticks per task EventId u64; Ticks u64; no wrap handling required
Checkpoint interval (default) Every 10M RCB ticks or 30 s wall-time Configurable; seek latency = replay-forward from checkpoint
Seek latency (reverse-continue) ≤ 2× checkpoint-interval replay time Bounded by distance to nearest checkpoint
Divergence detection overhead ≤ 5% of replay time SHA-256 per touched page, deferred to end of event
Supervisor RSS ceiling ≤ 256 MB Does not grow with tracee heap; page snapshots streamed to disk
Tracee fd budget consumed by supervisor +3 fds per thread perf_event fd, /proc/pid/mem fd, ptrace attachment (no fd)
Trace fsync cadence Every checkpoint boundary Crash leaves replayable prefix (P7)
Startup time (dret record) ≤ 100 ms from exec to first tracee syscall Fork + ptrace setup + seccomp install
Shutdown time (dret record) ≤ 500 ms after tracee exit Flush + fsync + close
Min kernel version Linux 4.4 PTRACE_SYSEMU stable; perf_event RCB; seccomp SECCOMP_RET_TRACE
CPU topology constraint Replay pinned to single core (sched_setaffinity) I6; replay process + tracee on same core
Clock source PERF_COUNT_HW_BRANCH_INSTRUCTIONS Must be available on target CPU (Intel PMU v3+, AMD PerfCtr)
Skid correction budget ≤ 50 RCB ticks of overcount Measured empirically per CPU model; stored in trace header
Max data.log entry size 2^32 bytes (DataRef is u64 file offset) Single MemDelta bounded by tracee address space (≤ 128 TB)
Trace forward-compat Reader skips unknown tag bytes Versioned header: major.minor; major bump = breaking
CRC algorithm CRC-32C (Castagnoli) Hardware-accelerated on x86 SSE4.2
gdbserver TCP port Default 1234; configurable Single connection; no multiplexing
Reverse-continue max search depth Full trace length O(log N) checkpoints; O(distance) replay per segment
Conformance corpus size (Phase 14) ≥ 50 recorded programs Covering: single-thread, multi-thread, signal-heavy, memory-heavy, fork/exec
Fuzz corpus (trace reader) ≥ 10k mutated frames libFuzzer harness on TraceReader::next_frame()

4.2 Failure Budget and Recovery Objectives

Scenario Behavior Recovery
Supervisor killed mid-record (SIGKILL) Trace truncated at last fsynced frame dret replay replays up to last valid EventId
Tracee segfault during record SIGSEGV delivered to tracee; recorded as SignalRecord; tracee exits Trace complete through signal delivery
Divergence detected on replay DivergenceReport emitted; replay aborts (P6) Run dret dump --event <id> to inspect; bisect mode
data.log corruption (bad CRC) TraceReader returns Err on frame read Replay halts at corrupted event; partial replay possible
perf_event_open fails (PMU busy) Record startup fails with diagnostic User must free PMU counters; no fallback clock
sched_setaffinity fails (container) Replay continues on multiple CPUs (degraded) Divergence detection will catch any resulting nondeterminism
gdbserver client disconnect Replay session paused; resumable on reconnect Session held until timeout (30 s default) or explicit kill

4.3 Storage Amplification Model

Amplification = (trace size) / (tracee-written bytes)

Components:
  events.log  : N_events × 192 B        (fixed per event)
  data.log    : sum(mem_effect_bytes)   (1:1 with kernel writes into tracee)
  mmap.log    : N_mmap_events × 128 B  (sparse)
  ckpt/       : N_checkpoints × (dirty_pages × 4096 B)

Typical workload (1M syscalls, 100 MB data written):
  events.log  ≈ 192 MB
  data.log    ≈ 100 MB
  mmap.log    ≈ 1 MB
  ckpt/       ≈ 50 checkpoints × 10 MB avg dirty = 500 MB
  Total       ≈ 793 MB for 100 MB application writes (~8× amplification)

With Phase 14 compression (zstd data.log + ckpt pages):
  Compressed data.log ≈ 20–50 MB (typical 2–5× for memory data)
  Compressed ckpt     ≈ 100–200 MB
  Effective amplification: 2–3× compressed

4.4 Per-Phase Performance Checkpoint

Phase Key metric to pass before advancing
Phase 1 Supervisor handles 100k PTRACE_SYSCALL stops/sec without leak
Phase 4 Ticks monotonic over 10M events; skid ≤ 50 RCB measured on target CPU
Phase 6 Single-thread replay of /bin/ls bit-exact 100 consecutive runs
Phase 8 Multi-thread replay of pthread_mutex benchmark bit-exact 100 runs
Phase 11 Seek to arbitrary tick position in 10M-event trace ≤ 2 s
Phase 12 Zero divergence on full conformance corpus (≥ 50 programs)
Phase 14 Fuzz 10k mutated trace frames; no supervisor panic or UB

6. Design Principles

P1. Determinism over performance

Statement. Every recorded execution must replay bit-for-bit under identical inputs; recording overhead of 2–20x is acceptable, a nondeterministic replay is a correctness failure.

Tradeoff implications.

  • Batch ptrace calls and memory reads are allowed to amortize overhead, but only if they do not alter the observation window.
  • Compression in data.log is deferred to Phase 14 because it is a throughput concern, not a correctness concern.
  • RCB-based preemption incurs a perf_event_open file descriptor per thread; this is acceptable overhead.

Operational implications.

  • CI must run the same workload under record+replay and diff the output byte-for-byte.
  • Any optimization must pass a determinism gate before landing.

Violation example. Reading wall time from clock_gettime inside the supervisor to decide preemption intervals → two recordings of the same workload produce different SchedSwitch sequences → replay diverges at Phase 7 boundary.

P2. Record once, replay many

Statement. The trace directory is an immutable artifact after recording completes. Replay is read-only; multiple simultaneous replay sessions over the same trace are safe.

Tradeoff implications.

  • TraceWriter uses append-only writes with CRC frames; no in-place mutation.
  • Checkpoint restore copies recorded page snapshots; never mutates the stored snapshot.
  • Replay sessions are stateless with respect to the trace; session state lives only in ReplaySession.

Operational implications.

  • dret pack (Phase 14) produces a self-contained archive; original trace remains intact.
  • Parallel dret replay invocations share the same trace directory with read-only opens.

Violation example. A replay session patches mmap.log to cache a derived value → a second concurrent replay session reads a corrupt mmap entry → AddressSpace reconstruction fails at an unrelated event.

P3. Observe, do not perturb

Statement. The recorder must not change the tracee's observable behavior. Instrumentation that injects code into the tracee, modifies its memory mappings, or introduces additional syscalls is forbidden.

Tradeoff implications.

  • Hardware PMU counters (perf_event_open) are used instead of binary instrumentation.
  • Seccomp-BPF is used to intercept vDSO calls at the kernel boundary without patching the vDSO text in the tracee's address space (except the specific vsyscall compatibility patch documented in Phase 10).
  • Heisenbugs caused by observer effect are the primary threat this principle prevents.

Operational implications.

  • PTRACE_PEEKDATA / PTRACE_POKEDATA to tracee memory is permitted only within documented syscall-stop windows, never asynchronously.
  • Memory reads via /proc/pid/mem are bounded to syscall-stop windows to avoid racing with tracee writes.

Violation example. Injecting a trampoline into the tracee's .text to count branches → the tracee's instruction stream changes → RCB counts differ between record and replay → tick-addressed events are unreachable.

P4. Single logical CPU during replay

Statement. During replay, at most one task is scheduled to execute at any point in time. All thread parallelism is serialized; dret-sched enforces this via sched_setaffinity and run-queue exclusion.

Tradeoff implications.

  • Multi-core performance of the replayed workload is not preserved; replay throughput is single-threaded.
  • Atomic operations become deterministic by serialization: there is no concurrent modification.
  • Futex ordering recorded in Phase 8 is sufficient; no memory barrier replay is required beyond serialization.

Operational implications.

  • dret-replay pins itself and all tracee threads to a single CPU via sched_setaffinity(0) at session start.
  • SchedSwitch events gate thread advancement; a thread blocked in replay is held at a PTRACE_SYSCALL stop.
  • I6 is directly enforced by this principle.

Violation example. Replay session allows two threads to be in PTRACE_CONT simultaneously → one thread executes past its recorded preemption tick → RCB counter for that task advances past a recorded SchedSwitch boundary → subsequent syscall injection targets wrong TaskId → mismatched ret injection.

P5. The tick (RCB) is the only clock

Statement. All addresses within the execution trace are expressed as retired-conditional-branch counts (Ticks) per TaskId. Wall time, monotonic clocks, and CPU cycles are neither recorded nor used for ordering.

Tradeoff implications.

  • RCB is TSC-independent; the trace is portable across CPU frequency changes and turbo boost states.
  • RCB is not perfectly stable across microcode updates; the divergence checker (Phase 12) catches any skid-induced deviation.
  • Checkpoints keyed by (EventId, Ticks) are deterministically seekable.

Operational implications.

  • Any code path that reads CLOCK_MONOTONIC or rdtsc inside the supervisor for ordering purposes is a P5 violation.
  • dret dump output shows Ticks next to each event; no wall timestamps in the trace.

Violation example. Preemption triggered by a SIGALRM timer inside the supervisor → the alarm fires at different wall times on different machines → SchedSwitch events differ → replay tick sequence differs → downstream event injection fails.

P6. Fail closed on divergence

Statement. When the divergence checker detects a mismatch between recorded and replay state (registers, ticks, or memory hash), replay halts immediately and emits a DivergenceReport. Silent continuation is never permitted.

Tradeoff implications.

  • A replay that partially progresses past a divergence is worse than one that stops early; partial progress corrupts any debugging session built on top.
  • dret-divergence adds per-event overhead; this is acceptable because replay is already bounded by I/O from trace reads.

Operational implications.

  • DivergenceReport includes first-divergence EventId, kind (RegMismatch / TickMismatch / MemHashMismatch), expected and actual values.
  • The divergence bisection tool (Phase 12) narrows the first-divergent event to isolate the recorder bug.
  • dret replay --check always runs divergence checking; --no-check is an unsafe escape hatch, not a default.

Violation example. A diverged replay session is passed to dret-gdb → the gdb user sets a breakpoint and inspects memory → they observe replay-injected state that never existed in the original run → the debugging session produces a false root cause.

P7. Crash-only recorder

Statement. A recorder process killed at any moment leaves a valid, replayable trace prefix. The trace is append-only; no frame is modified after it is written.

Tradeoff implications.

  • TraceWriter never seeks backward; CRC is appended per frame at commit time.
  • The last frame may be incomplete (partial write); TraceReader stops at the first bad CRC and treats the prefix as the trace.
  • No fsync per frame (too costly); a configurable checkpoint_sync_interval controls durability.

Operational implications.

  • dret replay on a crash-truncated trace replays up to the last intact event without error.
  • dret dump --check-crc validates the entire trace and reports the truncation offset.

Violation example. Recorder rewrites an earlier frame to patch an address-space entry → a SIGKILL between the seek and the rewrite leaves a half-overwritten frame → TraceReader reads corrupt data mid-trace → replay crashes at an unrelated event.

P8. Trace forward-compatibility

Statement. Trace files are versioned and frame-tagged. An older reader skips unknown frame types. A newer recorder never modifies the meaning of existing frame types.

Tradeoff implications.

  • Every EventKind variant must carry a stable wire tag; adding variants is additive.
  • Forward-compatibility means the trace format cannot be a bare struct dump; it must use length-prefixed, tagged fields.
  • Removing a frame type requires a major version bump; readers reject mismatched major versions.

Operational implications.

  • The trace header carries (major, minor) version; dret-trace rejects major mismatches, warns on minor mismatches.
  • Unknown frame type tags are skipped with their length field; no unknown-type error is emitted below WARN level.

Violation example. A new recorder emits EventKind::CgroupChange frames without a length prefix → an older reader has no way to skip the frame → it tries to deserialize a CgroupChange blob as an EventKind::Syscall → corrupted SyscallRecord on every subsequent frame.

P9. Minimal trusted computing base

Statement. The supervisor (tracer) process is the only trusted component. The tracee is untrusted; a buggy or adversarial tracee cannot corrupt the supervisor's state or the trace.

Tradeoff implications.

  • The supervisor never dereferences tracee pointers directly; all tracee memory access goes through /proc/pid/mem or PTRACE_PEEKDATA with explicit bounds.
  • Seccomp-BPF filters are installed in the tracee, not the supervisor.
  • Trace-reader fuzzing (Phase 14) validates that a corrupt trace does not panic the supervisor.

Operational implications.

  • All DataRef offsets read from trace frames are bounds-checked against data.log file size before use.
  • Page snapshot restoration validates page alignment and size before pwrite into tracee.

Violation example. Supervisor trusts a len field from a tracee-writable mmap region to determine how many bytes to copy → tracee writes a large len → supervisor copies out-of-bounds memory into trace → trace data.log is silently corrupted.

P10. Explicit ownership of tracee state

Statement. Every byte written into tracee address space during replay is traceable to a specific recorded TraceFrame. No byte is synthesized, guessed, or derived without a recorded source.

Tradeoff implications.

  • Syscall results that have no memory effects (e.g., getpid) require only ret injection; no memory write is needed.
  • Syscalls with output pointers (e.g., read, recvmsg, getsockname) require full MemDelta replay; partial injection is a correctness failure.
  • I4 and I10 are direct statements of this principle.

Operational implications.

  • dret-syscall's effect descriptor for each syscall must be complete; a missing effect entry causes a MemHashMismatch in Phase 12.
  • dret dump --events shows each MemDelta with its DataRef offset for auditability.

Violation example. read syscall injection omits the last 8 bytes of the output buffer because the MemDelta was computed as len - 8 due to an off-by-one in the recorder → replay tracee reads stale bytes in that region → application logic diverges silently until a later hash check fires.

P11. Task identity is permanent and non-reusable

Statement. Once a TaskId(u32) is allocated to a thread, it is never reassigned to a different thread for the lifetime of the trace, even after the original thread exits. TaskId is a dret-internal identity, distinct from the OS tid, which the kernel does reuse.

Tradeoff implications.

  • TaskIdAllocator (Phase 8) is a monotonic counter, never a free-list; this trades a small amount of address space (u32 range) for unambiguous identity.
  • Every ClonePayload, FutexRecord, and SchedSwitch frame can be attributed to exactly one physical thread across the entire trace, with no cross-referencing against OS pid/tid reuse windows.

Operational implications.

  • dret dump --threads can list every thread that ever existed in a session without needing wall-clock correlation against OS tid recycling.
  • Replay pre-populates ThreadTable from Clone frames at start (Phase 8) precisely because identities are stable and known in advance.

Violation example. A recorder reuses freed TaskIds to save allocator space → two unrelated threads separated by minutes of wall time share one TaskId → dret dump --futex shows a wakeup ordering that appears to violate I2 monotonicity, when in fact two distinct threads' tick sequences were concatenated under one identity.

P12. The trace container is the sole channel between recorder and replayer

Statement. dret record and dret replay never communicate directly (no shared memory, no socket, no IPC). The only interface between them is the on-disk trace directory (or its packed .dret archive).

Tradeoff implications.

  • This enables replay on a different machine, at a different time, by a different operator than the one who recorded — the core "record once, replay many" promise (P2) requires this decoupling to be absolute, not just eventual.
  • It also means every piece of information the replayer needs (binary identity, syscall results, memory deltas, scheduling decisions) must be captured up front; there is no fallback path to ask the original process for missing data after the fact.

Operational implications.

  • CI pipelines can archive a trace as a build artifact and replay it on a completely different worker (Phase 14 dret pack).
  • No dret-replay code path may assume the recording supervisor process, or the original tracee, still exists.

Violation example. A hypothetical "live replay" feature attaches dret-replay directly to a still-running dret-record session via a Unix socket to save disk I/O → the socket-based session works in testing but cannot be archived, cannot be replayed later, and silently couples the two binaries' versions together, defeating P8 (forward-compatibility) and P2 in one step.

7. System Invariants

ID Invariant Statement Why It Matters Enforced By Detection Telemetry Recovery Debugging
I1 EventId increments by exactly 1 per emitted TraceFrame; never reused; no gaps A gap signals corruption or a missing event class; reuse produces replay injection at wrong event dret-trace::TraceWriter — asserts prev_id + 1 == new_id at append() TraceReader::next() checks monotonicity; dret dump --check-ids scans full trace counter: event_id_gap_total increments on first gap detected Truncate trace to last valid EventId; emit P7-compliant prefix Bisect with dret dump --range to find the first gap frame
I2 Ticks(RCB) is monotonic non-decreasing per TaskId across the trace Non-monotone ticks mean the clock wrapped, the PMU was reset, or a task was mis-identified; breaks tick-addressed seeks dret-record — validates tick at each stop before emitting frame; dret-sched — asserts task tick ≥ last on switch-out dret-divergence compares per-task tick sequences; dret dump --ticks plots per-task tick delta gauge: last_task_ticks{tid} per task Replay aborts at first regression; frame is quarantined; operator must re-record Isolate the task and event with dret dump --task <tid> --range
I3 Replay consumes TraceFrames in exact recorded EventId order, never skipping or reordering Out-of-order consumption means a syscall is injected before the corresponding tracee stop reaches it; instant divergence dret-replay::ReplaySession::advance() — asserts next_expected_event_id matches popped frame Sequence counter inside dret-replay; mismatch → DivergenceReport{kind: EventOrderViolation} counter: replay_event_order_violation_total Abort replay; report mismatched IDs Compare dret dump event order with replay session log
I4 Injected syscall result on replay equals recorded result: ret, errno, every MemDelta byte A single differing byte produces a different program state; cascades to arbitrarily distant divergence dret-replay — after injection, reads back each MemDelta range and compares hash dret-divergence MemHashMismatch at the event's DataRef payload counter: mem_delta_inject_mismatch_total{nr} Abort; emit DivergenceReport with first mismatched byte offset dret dump --event <id> --hex shows recorded bytes; compare with tracee read
I5 No real syscall produces externally-visible side effects during replay A real syscall would mutate external state (files, network, kernel objects) making replay non-idempotent dret-ptrace — enforces PTRACE_SYSEMU for all tracee syscall stops during replay Any stop that arrives as a real syscall completion (not SYSEMU) is treated as a fatal error counter: replay_real_syscall_escape_total Abort session; log the syscall nr and event context Verify PTRACE_SETOPTIONS includes correct flags; check kernel version
I6 Exactly one task is runnable (not held at a ptrace stop) at any replay instant Two runnable tasks can diverge from each other's tick counts; destroys single-CPU serialization dret-sched::RunQueue — only one task is in PTRACE_CONT state; all others in PTRACE_SYSCALL stop RunQueue::assert_single_running() called at every scheduler decision point gauge: sched_runnable_task_count — alert if > 1 Immediately stop the second task; DivergenceReport Log task state transitions with dret dump --sched
I7 Every Checkpoint sits exactly on an event boundary: stored (event_id, ticks) matches a recorded TraceFrame A checkpoint between events cannot be restored to a valid state; forward replay from it desynchronizes injection dret-record — checkpoints written only immediately after a frame commit; event_id taken from last committed frame dret-replay validates checkpoint's event_id exists in events.log before restoring counter: checkpoint_boundary_violation_total Skip the malformed checkpoint; use the prior valid one dret dump --checkpoints lists all checkpoint event IDs for cross-reference
I8 Async signal delivery tick on replay equals recorded delivery tick (SignalRecord.delivery_ticks) An early-delivered signal changes register state before the correct tick; a late-delivered signal skips effects that should have occurred dret-replay — single-steps to the exact delivery_ticks boundary, then calls PTRACE_SETSIGINFO dret-divergence compares regs at delivery event; TickMismatch if signal injected off-boundary counter: signal_delivery_tick_delta{signo} histogram Abort; log actual vs. recorded tick at delivery dret dump --signals shows each signal's recorded tick per task
I9 Recorded register set at event E equals replay register set at event E A register mismatch means the execution reached a different point; subsequent control flow is undefined relative to the trace dret-divergence — PTRACE_GETREGSET at each event, hash RecordedRegs, compare to stored hash DivergenceReport{kind: RegMismatch, event_id, expected, actual} emitted immediately counter: divergence_reg_mismatch_total Halt replay; preserve state for bisection Bisect with dret replay --bisect to find first RegMismatch event
I10 Address-space layout at event E is identical in record and replay: mmap provenance, brk, segment permissions A layout difference means pointer arithmetic produces different addresses; silent data corruption dret-replay — replays all mmap/munmap/mremap/brk via recorded AddressSpace events; no speculative mappings dret-divergence compares /proc/pid/maps hash at each Checkpoint event gauge: address_space_hash_mismatch_total Abort; emit AddressSpace diff between recorded and actual dret dump --mmaps shows recorded segment sequence; cat /proc/<pid>/maps in replay for comparison
I11 TaskId(u32) is allocated monotonically per session and never reused, even after the owning thread exits A reused TaskId makes replay unable to tell which physical thread a historical ClonePayload/futex/SchedSwitch record belongs to; two distinct threads collapse into one identity TaskIdAllocator in dret-record (Phase 8) — monotonic u32 counter, never rewound on exit dret-replay asserts no two ThreadRecords ever share a TaskId within one session counter: task_id_reused_total (must stay zero) Abort replay; ReplayError::TaskIdCollision Cross-reference dret dump --threads against the clone/exit event count
I12 Every persisted TraceFrame's crc32 field matches an independent recomputation over its tag + frame_len + payload bytes An undetected corrupt frame silently injects a wrong syscall result or register value, producing a divergence indistinguishable from a genuine recorder bug dret-trace::TraceReader::read_frame() (Phase 3) recomputes CRC-32C before returning any frame to a caller Any mismatch returns Err(CrcMismatch) immediately, before the frame reaches dret-replay or dret-divergence counter: crc_mismatch_total{file} Halt read at the corrupt frame; trace is valid only up to the last good CRC (P7) dret pack --verify recomputes every frame's CRC offline

8. Domain Model

8.1 Core Entities and Relationships

  • RecordSession
  • 1──* TaskRecord { TaskId, Tid, initial_regs, entry_ticks }
  • 1──* TraceFrame { EventId, Ticks, TaskId, EventKind, RecordedRegs, DataRef }
  • 1──1 AddressSpace (per-process snapshot, updated on mmap/munmap/brk)
  • 1──N Checkpoint (periodic; anchored to EventId boundary)
  • TraceFrame
    • ├── Syscall → SyscallRecord { nr, args[6], ret, mem_effects: Vec<MemDelta>, errno }
    • ├── Signal → SignalRecord { signo, si_code, deterministic: bool, delivery_ticks }
    • ├── SchedSwitch → SchedPoint { tid, ticks }
    • ├── RdtscTrap → { recorded_tsc: u64 }
    • ├── CpuidTrap → { leaf, subleaf, eax, ebx, ecx, edx }
    • ├── PatchVsyscall → { page_addr, slot }
    • ├── Exec → { path_ref: DataRef, argv_ref: DataRef, envp_ref: DataRef }
    • ├── Exit → { tid, exit_code }
    • └── Checkpoint → { checkpoint_id: u64, event_id: EventId }
  • Checkpoint
    • ├── regs_per_task: Map<TaskId, RecordedRegs>
    • ├── dirty_pages: Vec<(MemRange, PageRef)>
    • ├── fd_table: Vec<FdRecord>
    • ├── mmaps: Vec<MmapEntry>
    • └── brk: u64

8.2 EventKind Taxonomy

EventKind Source trap Recorder action Replayer action
Syscall PTRACE_SYSCALL stop (exit) record nr, args, ret, mem_effects PTRACE_SYSEMU; inject ret + mem writes
Signal(sync) SIGSEGV/SIGFPE/SIGILL fault record signo, si_code, regs at fault re-raise at same rip; no tick pinning
Signal(async) SIGINT/SIGUSR1/… delivered async pin to (tid, delivery_ticks) step to delivery_ticks, PTRACE setsiginfo
SchedSwitch tick-budget exhausted record (old_tid, new_tid, ticks) force same switch at same ticks
RdtscTrap CR4.TSD=1 or seccomp SIGSEGV record GPR snapshot + tsc value inject recorded eax:edx
CpuidTrap seccomp or UD#; leaf/subleaf record four output regs inject recorded eax/ebx/ecx/edx
PatchVsyscall vsyscall page rip intercept record slot; rewrite to int 0x80 path same patch applied on replay binary
Exec PTRACE_EVENT_EXEC record path + argv + envp verify replayed exec matches
Exit PTRACE_EVENT_EXIT record exit_code; close task entry expect same exit code
Checkpoint periodic / forced flush dirty pages; snapshot fd_table restore for seek/reverse ops

8.3 Ticks (RCB) State Machine per TaskId

States: UNBORN → RUNNING → BLOCKED → RUNNABLE → RUNNING → … → DEAD
Transitions:
  UNBORN     --[clone/exec trap]--> RUNNING    (Ticks reset to 0 at exec)
  RUNNING    --[syscall entry]----> RUNNING    (ticks read at PTRACE stop)
  RUNNING    --[tick budget 0]----> BLOCKED    (SchedSwitch emitted; another task scheduled)
  BLOCKED    --[sched grant]------> RUNNABLE   (new tick budget assigned)
  RUNNABLE   --[PTRACE_CONT]------> RUNNING
  RUNNING    --[exit trap]---------> DEAD
  • Ticks are read via ioctl(PERF_EVENT_IOC_RESET) / read() on the per-task perf fd at every PTRACE stop.
  • Ticks(RCB) is monotonic non-decreasing within a TaskId (I2); wrapping to zero only at exec.
  • The scheduler grants a tick_budget from SchedPoint.ticks to the next SchedSwitch ticks; it uses PERF_EVENT_IOC_RESET + overflow signal to preempt at the exact boundary.

8.4 Trace-on-Disk Layout

TRACE_DIR/
  header.json          — { version, arch, record_pid, start_time_ns, task_count }
  events.log           — append-only; fixed-stride frames or length-prefixed if variable
  data.log             — variable-length blobs; DataRef = byte offset
  mmap.log             — AddressSpace deltas (mmap/munmap/mremap/brk) in order
  checkpoints/
    ckpt_<event_id>/
      regs.bin         — per-task RecordedRegs (fixed layout)
      pages.bin        — dirty pages: (addr:u64, len:u32, data_ref:u64) per entry
      fd_table.bin     — FdRecord list
      mmap_snapshot.bin— full AddressSpace at this checkpoint

events.log frame wire layout (little-endian):

 0       4       8      12      16      24      32      40     48     56     64
[magic:4][len:4][crc:4][eid:8][tid:4][ticks:8][kind:1][pad:3][regs:N][dref:8][tag-fields...]
  • magic = 0x44524554 ("DRET")
  • len = total frame byte count including header
  • crc = CRC-32C over bytes [12..len]
  • eid = EventId (u64, strictly +1 from prior)
  • ticks = Ticks at this event
  • regs = RecordedRegs (17 × u64 = 136 bytes for GPRs + rip + rflags + fs_base + gs_base + orig_rax)
  • dref = DataRef into data.log (0 if no payload)
  • tag-fields = kind-specific payload if small enough to inline (max 32 bytes); else DataRef

data.log blob entry:

[len:4][crc:4][payload:len]
  • Blobs are referenced only by DataRef offset; no reverse index in data.log itself.
  • TraceReader builds an in-memory EventId→offset index on first open; checkpointed to events.idx after pack.

mmap.log entry:

[eid:8][kind:1 (Map|Unmap|Remap|Brk)][addr:8][len:8][prot:4][flags:4][offset:8][path_ref:8]

8.5 RecordedRegs Layout

Field Width Notes
rax u64 return value at syscall exit
rbx–r15 u64×13 general-purpose
rip u64 instruction pointer at stop
rflags u64 including TF (trap flag) state
fs_base u64 TLS base; captured via PTRACE_GETREGSET
gs_base u64 alternate TLS / per-cpu (user-space only)
orig_rax u64 syscall number at entry stop

Total: 17 × 8 = 136 bytes. Stored verbatim in each TraceFrame.

8.6 SyscallRecord Semantics

SyscallRecord {
  nr:          u32,                  // syscall number (x86-64 ABI)
  args:        [u64; 6],             // rdi, rsi, rdx, r10, r8, r9 at entry
  ret:         i64,                  // rax at exit (negative = -errno)
  errno:       i32,                  // decoded errno or 0
  mem_effects: Vec<MemDelta>,        // each region kernel wrote into tracee
}
MemDelta { range: MemRange, bytes_ref: DataRef }
  • Pointer args classified by dret-syscall ABI table: IN (read by kernel), OUT (written by kernel), INOUT, or SCALAR.
  • Only OUT/INOUT ranges with non-zero len produce MemDelta entries.
  • mem_effects captured by reading /proc/pid/mem at the PTRACE syscall-exit stop, before PTRACE_CONT.
  • On replay: write each MemDelta.bytes into tracee address range.addr via PTRACE_POKEDATA or /proc/pid/mem pwrite.

8.7 Signal Determinism Model

Signal class Examples Determinism strategy
Synchronous SIGSEGV, SIGFPE, SIGILL Caused by exact rip/instruction; replays without pinning
Async hardware SIGALRM, SIGIO Pinned to (tid, delivery_ticks); replayer steps to that tick
Async software kill(2), tkill, tgkill Same; sender tid + ticks also recorded (I8)
SIGCHLD child state change Pinned; note: pid remapping required on replay
  • SignalRecord.deterministic == true for sync signals (no tick injection needed).
  • SignalRecord.deterministic == false requires replayer to single-step tracee until Ticks == delivery_ticks, then inject via PTRACE_SETSIGINFO + PTRACE_CONT.
  • Nested signals: recorded in event order; replayer re-queues in same order.

8.8 Scheduler Serialization Semantics

RunQueue (replay):
  - ordered list of (TaskId, tick_budget)
  - head task = currently running
  - tail tasks = blocked at PTRACE stop

SchedSwitch event:
  { prev_tid, next_tid, prev_ticks, next_ticks_start }
  • All threads held at PTRACE stops except the running one (I6).
  • Tick budget = next_switch.ticks - current.ticks; perf overflow at budget end causes SIGTRAP.
  • Futex record: futex(2) exit recorded with {uaddr, op, ret}; replayer injects at same position, unblocking waiter at the recorded ticks.
  • sched_setaffinity called in tracee's namespace to pin to CPU 0; supervisor ignores the call during replay (injects ret=0).

8.9 Checkpoint Model

Checkpoint creation triggers:
  - Every K events (default K=50,000; tunable)
  - Before exec, exit, and dlopen (mandatory)
  - Forced by CLI: `dret pack --checkpoint-interval`

Seek semantics:
  target_event_id E:
    1. Find largest Checkpoint C where C.event_id <= E
    2. Restore: write C.regs_per_task, C.dirty_pages, C.mmaps, C.fd_table into replay process
    3. Replay-forward from C.event_id to E consuming events in order
    4. On reverse-step: seek(E-1); on reverse-continue: seek(last watchpoint hit before E)
  • I7: Checkpoint is always placed on an event boundary; C.event_id points to the TraceFrame emitted immediately before the snapshot.
  • Dirty pages: union of all pages written since prior Checkpoint; stored as (addr, PageRef→data.log).
  • FdRecord: { fd, kind: File|Pipe|Socket|Epoll|…, path_or_peer, flags, offset }. Sockets recorded as closed/unrestorable on replay if not AF_UNIX.

8.10 Address-Space Model

AddressSpace {
  segments: Vec<Segment { addr, len, prot, flags, offset, MmapProvenance }>,
  brk:      u64,
}
MmapProvenance: { path: Option<DataRef>, build_id: Option<[u8;20]>, inode: u64, dev: u64 }
  • Updated on every mmap/mmap2/munmap/mremap/brk/mprotect syscall exit.
  • On replay: mmap calls with MAP_FIXED to reproduce layout (I10); file-backed mappings resolved from packed trace or host filesystem by build-id.
  • ASLR disabled in tracee via personality(ADDR_NO_RANDOMIZE) at record and replay start.
  • [vvar]/[vdso] pages: vsyscall patch applied at exec (PatchVsyscall event); vDSO remapped to supervisor-controlled page on replay.

8.11 Replay Injection Semantics

Sequence per Syscall event during replay:

1. PTRACE_SYSCALL → stop at syscall entry
2. Read syscall nr from orig_rax; verify == recorded nr (else DivergenceReport)
3. PTRACE_SYSEMU → tracee does not execute syscall; stops again at entry
4. Set rax = SyscallRecord.ret in tracee regs via PTRACE_SETREGS
5. For each MemDelta: pwrite /proc/pid/mem at range.addr with bytes from data.log
6. Verify RecordedRegs (I9): read regs; hash vs stored; on mismatch → DivergenceReport
7. PTRACE_CONT
  • PTRACE_SYSEMU replaces both entry and exit stops with a single combined stop; replayer uses the exit-equivalent position.
  • errno encoded in rax as -(errno) per Linux x86-64 ABI; replayer sets rax directly.
  • write(2), read(2), pread(2): inject ret + any OUT buffers; actual file I/O suppressed.

8.12 RdtscTrap and CpuidTrap Injection

Trap Mechanism Record Replay injection
RDTSC / RDTSCP CR4.TSD=1 via seccomp-BPF SIGSEGV or ptrace UD read eax:edx + rcx (rdtscp) PTRACE_SETREGS eax:edx[:rcx]; skip instr
CPUID seccomp BPF traps to supervisor record leaf, subleaf, 4 outputs PTRACE_SETREGS; advance rip by 2
gettimeofday(vDSO) vsyscall page rewrite + PatchVsyscall event recorded as Syscall nr=96 injected as normal SyscallRecord
clock_gettime(vDSO) same recorded as Syscall nr=228 injected as normal SyscallRecord
  • On replay: rip advanced past the faulting instruction length (RDTSC=2, CPUID=2 bytes).
  • CPUID masking: replayer applies a feature mask table to prevent tracee from detecting host CPU differences; recorded leaf/subleaf values win.

8.13 Divergence Detection State Machine

States: CLEAN → DIVERGED → ABORTED
Transitions:
  CLEAN    --[event E: regs match + ticks match + mem_hash match]--> CLEAN
  CLEAN    --[event E: any mismatch]-----> DIVERGED (emit DivergenceReport)
  DIVERGED --[--strict mode]--> ABORTED  (replay process killed)
  DIVERGED --[--lenient mode]--> CLEAN   (resync regs; continue; increment mismatch_count)
  • Default mode: --strict (P6: fail closed).
  • DivergenceReport { event_id, kind: RegMismatch|TickMismatch|MemHashMismatch, expected, actual, task_id }.
  • mem_hash: xxhash3 of pages touched by syscall mem_effects at exit stop (record side) vs identical pages post-injection (replay side).

8.14 gdbserver RSP Interaction Model

GDB client <──RSP over TCP/Unix──> dret-gdb stub <──> ReplaySession
  • Supported packets: ?, g/G, m/M, c/s, vCont, qXfer:features, Z0/Z1 (sw/hw breakpoint), Z2/Z3/Z4 (watchpoints via mem-delta scan).
  • Reverse packets: bc (reverse-continue), bs (reverse-step) — mapped to checkpoint_seek(event_id - 1) + replay-forward.
  • Thread list: qfThreadInfo returns all recorded TaskIds; Hg<tid> selects focus task.
  • Watchpoints on replay: no hardware DR registers; replayer re-runs from nearest checkpoint scanning for writes to watched address range; first hit = watchpoint event.
  • No side effects: M (memory write) during replay session updates only the replayer's tracee; does not persist to trace (P2).

8.15 Worked Example — Normal Syscall Record/Replay

Record side:

tracee calls: read(fd=3, buf=0x7fff1000, count=4096)
PTRACE_SYSCALL entry stop: orig_rax=0, rdi=3, rsi=0x7fff1000, rdx=4096
  → record args; continue tracee
PTRACE_SYSCALL exit stop:  rax=512 (bytes read)
  → record SyscallRecord{ nr=0, args=[3,0x7fff1000,4096,0,0,0], ret=512, errno=0 }
  → read /proc/pid/mem at [0x7fff1000, 512): capture 512 bytes → data.log offset D1
  → MemDelta{ range={0x7fff1000,512}, bytes_ref=D1 }
  → emit TraceFrame{ eid=42, tid=T1, ticks=8103, kind=Syscall, dref=DataRef(D0) }
     where D0 = serialized SyscallRecord pointing to D1

Replay side:

event 42: PTRACE_SYSEMU stop; orig_rax=0 ✓
  → set rax=512 via PTRACE_SETREGS
  → pwrite /proc/pid/mem at 0x7fff1000 ← 512 bytes from data.log[D1]
  → read regs; compare vs RecordedRegs stored in frame 42 → match (I9)
  → PTRACE_CONT
  → ticks after event = 8103 ✓ (I2 monotonic)

8.16 Malformed/Failure-Path Example — EventId Gap

Scenario: recorder killed between writing frame 99 and frame 100; data.log has partial blob.

TraceReader open:
  scan events.log frames: frames 0–99 valid CRC; frame 100 truncated (len field reads 0xFFFFFFFF)
  → detect: eid[99]+1 != eid[100] — gap detected
  → mark trace as TRUNCATED_AT(99)
  → TraceReader.last_valid_event_id() = 99

Replay attempt to event 150:
  → ReplaySession::seek(150) → error: TraceError::TruncatedTrace { last_valid: 99 }
  → CLI prints: "trace truncated at event 99; replay available up to event 99"

P7 (crash-only recorder): prefix 0..99 is valid and replayable.

8.17 Edge Cases

Edge Case Handling
fork + exec in child PTRACE_EVENT_FORK creates new TaskId; Exec event resets Ticks to 0 for that TaskId
vfork with parent blocked Parent PTRACE stop held until child exec/exit; recorded as vfork event pair
Large mmap anonymous region AddressSpace updated; dirty-page tracking deferred until first write fault (COW)
mmap(MAP_FIXED) over existing mapping Old segments removed from AddressSpace; new segment inserted
Signal during syscall (EINTR) Signal recorded before interrupted-syscall retry; replay delivers signal first
ptrace(2) by tracee Supervisor intercepts; returns EPERM (tracee cannot have its own sub-tracee)
seccomp already installed by tracee Supervisor detects on exec; emits warning; compatibility depends on seccomp filter chain
Ticks skid (hardware overcount) dret-time applies skid correction: step backward by 1–4 ticks, re-measure
Nested clone (thread → thread) Each clone creates new TaskId; tick counter initialized from perf_event_open at clone
execve with O_CLOEXEC fds FdRecord marks cloexec fds as closed at exec boundary in Checkpoint
CPUID with unknown leaf All four output registers recorded verbatim; no semantic interpretation needed
Very long mem_effects (>1 MB write) Chunked into 4 KB MemDelta entries; DataRef chain; no single blob exceeds 1 MB

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
ADR-01 Implementation language Rust (stable toolchain, edition 2021) C, C++, Go, Zig Memory safety without GC; zero-cost FFI to Linux syscalls; ownership model maps to tracee-state ownership (P10) C wins when the target team already owns a ptrace codebase in C (rr) and the priority is diff-minimal contributions to that codebase; C++ wins if the project must interoperate with an existing C++ debugger plugin ecosystem (LLDB).
ADR-02 Build system Cargo workspace (Cargo.toml at root; per-crate Cargo.toml) Meson, CMake, Bazel Native Rust ecosystem; reproducible builds via Cargo.lock; no extra toolchain Bazel wins when the repo is a large polyglot monorepo requiring cross-language dependency tracking and remote build caching across dozens of teams; Meson wins for a primarily C/C++ project where Rust is a small embedded component.
ADR-03 Workspace structure 12 crates (dret-core … dret-cli) in crates/ subdirectory Single monolithic crate, flat layout Compile-time isolation; minimal dependency surfaces; parallel crate compilation A single monolithic crate wins in a solo-developer prototype where incremental compile times do not matter and the priority is minimizing boilerplate; flat layout wins for projects that will never be published as separate library crates.
ADR-04 Dependency policy Minimal: pin exact versions; no * or open ranges; audit via cargo audit Liberal semver Reproducibility; supply-chain safety; deterministic builds Liberal semver ranges win in an application library that must compose cleanly with downstream crates that also pin shared dependencies (e.g., a public API crate used by many consumers who need flexibility on transitive versions).
ADR-05 Trace on-disk format Custom binary framing: events.log + data.log + mmap.log + checkpoints/ SQLite, RocksDB, Cap'n Proto files, FlatBuffers files Append-only; crash-safe (P7); no external runtime; CRC-32C per frame; forward-compatible tag fields (P8) SQLite wins when the primary consumer is ad-hoc SQL queries over event data (e.g., a data-science team correlating traces); RocksDB wins when random-write + point-lookup semantics dominate (e.g., a streaming replay that must update intermediate state).
ADR-06 Serialization (trace frames) Hand-written little-endian binary (fixed-stride header + DataRef for variable payload) serde + bincode, postcard, protobuf, flatbuffers Zero allocations on hot path; exact layout control for CRC; no schema compiler dependency Protobuf wins when the trace format must be consumed by tools in many languages (Go, Python, Java) without a Rust toolchain; FlatBuffers wins when zero-copy in-place reading of large message fields (e.g., 64 KB MemDelta) is the primary access pattern.
ADR-07 Serialization (config/metadata) serde + serde_json for header.json and CLI config TOML, YAML, custom Human-readable; standard ecosystem; config is cold path TOML wins for user-facing configuration files where comment support and multi-line string syntax matter (e.g., a CI pipeline config authors edit frequently); YAML wins in environments where the config must be generated by existing YAML tooling (Kubernetes operators, Ansible).
ADR-08 Async runtime None (synchronous; std::thread only) tokio, async-std, smol Supervisor is inherently synchronous (ptrace waitpid loop); async overhead adds nondeterminism risk; I6 enforces single active task Tokio wins when the supervisor must simultaneously manage a large number of I/O-bound sessions (e.g., a multi-tenant replay service with hundreds of concurrent gdbserver clients); async-std wins for a lighter-weight single-binary service without Tokio's macro complexity.
ADR-09 ptrace abstraction dret-ptrace safe wrapper over raw libc / nix crate bindings nix crate used directly everywhere, pete crate Encapsulates PTRACE_SETOPTIONS, waitpid demux, GETREGSET/SETREGSET in one auditable module; nix used as thin FFI layer only The pete crate wins for a rapid prototype where minimizing boilerplate is the primary goal and the project will not need to extend ptrace semantics beyond what pete already wraps; nix direct wins in a single-crate tool that never exposes ptrace as a library API.
ADR-10 Performance counter access perf_event_open(2) PERF_COUNT_HW_BRANCH_INSTRUCTIONS per-task fd; overflow SIGIO RDPMC direct, BPF perf, Intel PT rr-proven mechanism; per-task isolation; overflow-based preemption; no ring-0 requirement Intel PT wins when instruction-level granularity is required for a replay system that must capture every control-flow transfer (not just syscalls), and the overhead budget allows 2–4× slower recording; RDPMC wins in a hypervisor context where perf_event_open is unavailable and the guest has direct PMU access.
ADR-11 RCB skid correction Subtract skid_constant (empirically measured per CPU model at startup); validate with micro-benchmark No correction, post-hoc statistical correction Hardware overcounts by 1–4 branches on overflow; correction is mandatory for I2 monotonicity No correction wins when the replay system uses a threshold looser than 1-tick precision (e.g., a coarse-grained event-log replay where ±50-branch uncertainty is acceptable and the performance cost of single-step backward probing is prohibitive).
ADR-12 Syscall interception mechanism PTRACE_SYSCALL + PTRACE_SYSEMU; seccomp-BPF SECCOMP_RET_TRACE for hot-path syscalls Full seccomp-BPF only, LD_PRELOAD interposition PTRACE_SYSEMU for replay (no real syscall executed); seccomp for low-latency pre-entry filter Full seccomp-BPF wins when the target is a sandboxing tool (not a replay engine) where preventing execution matters more than capturing return values; LD_PRELOAD wins for user-space library interposition in environments where ptrace is disallowed (e.g., Android apps, restricted containers).
ADR-13 CPUID / RDTSC interception seccomp-BPF → SIGSEGV → supervisor handles; CR4.TSD via seccomp where available Binary rewriting, IR instrumentation, hardware PMU virtualization No binary modification; works on unstripped and stripped binaries; P3 (observe, do not perturb) Binary rewriting wins when the target process runs in a VM where ptrace is unavailable and the binary is owned by the recorder team (e.g., a proprietary runtime instrumented at deploy time); hardware PMU virtualization wins inside a hypervisor where the VMM can intercept CPUID at ring-0.
ADR-14 Memory capture /proc/pid/mem pread/pwrite at PTRACE stops; COW page diff for checkpoints process_vm_readv, PTRACE_PEEKDATA word loop pread on /proc/pid/mem bulk-copies regions; fastest non-kernel path; process_vm_readv as fallback process_vm_readv wins when the supervisor is in a container where /proc/pid/mem is blocked by a seccomp policy but cross-process vector I/O is permitted; PTRACE_PEEKDATA wins for a single-word inspection tool where bulk transfer performance is irrelevant.
ADR-15 Page hashing (divergence) xxHash3 (128-bit) per 4 KB page SHA-256, CRC-32, MurmurHash Fastest non-cryptographic hash; 128-bit reduces collision probability to negligible; no security requirement SHA-256 wins when the hash doubles as a content-addressable storage key that must be collision-resistant against a malicious trace author (e.g., a shared replay archive where adversarial content injection is a threat model).
ADR-16 Concurrency model (supervisor) Single supervisor thread; waitpid(-1, WNOHANG) poll loop; one tracee thread active at a time (I6) Multi-threaded supervisor, async supervisor Eliminates supervisor data races; ptrace semantics require tracer thread to be the same OS thread per PTRACE_ATTACH A multi-threaded supervisor wins when the system records many independent single-threaded processes simultaneously (e.g., a batch fuzzing infrastructure recording hundreds of concurrent single-threaded fuzz targets), where per-process supervisor threads can run in parallel without ptrace cross-thread conflicts.
ADR-17 Logging tracing crate (structured spans + events); tracing-subscriber with JSON formatter for machine-readable output log + env_logger, slog Structured key-value fields; compatible with OpenTelemetry export; log facade bridged via tracing-log log + env_logger wins for a single-binary CLI tool with no observability requirements where the only consumer is a human reading stderr; slog wins when the project predates tracing and migration cost outweighs the benefit.
ADR-18 Metrics metrics crate façade; metrics-exporter-prometheus for Prometheus scraping in long-running dret record sessions Custom counters, statsd, no metrics Standard façade allows swapping exporter; record latency per event kind; checkpoint size histograms statsd wins in a legacy infrastructure where Prometheus is not deployed but a statsd pipeline already exists; no metrics wins for a short-lived developer tool where the session completes in seconds and aggregation overhead is not justified.
ADR-19 Error handling thiserror for library error enums; anyhow at CLI boundary only std::error::Error manual impl, eyre thiserror keeps library errors typed and composable; anyhow acceptable at binary entry points eyre wins when the project requires color-coded, contextual error reports with backtrace capture by default in a developer-facing CLI where ergonomic error display is a first-class feature; manual std::error::Error wins for a no-std embedded context.
ADR-20 Testing framework Cargo #[test] + cargo nextest; integration tests in tests/ per crate; proptest for property-based tests gtest via FFI, criterion-only nextest parallel execution; proptest for ABI tables and serialization round-trips gtest via FFI wins in a polyglot codebase that already has thousands of gtest suites for C++ components and needs Rust tests to appear in the same test report infrastructure; criterion-only wins for a pure performance-characterization project with no correctness test suite.
ADR-21 Fuzzing cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) targeting TraceReader frame parsing and SyscallRecord deserialization AFL++, honggfuzz, manual corpus First-class Rust integration; sanitizer support; Phase 14 conformance corpus feeds fuzz seeds AFL++ wins when coverage-guided mutation must run across a polyglot boundary (e.g., fuzzing a C parser invoked by a Rust harness) where libFuzzer's fork-server model is insufficient; honggfuzz wins for persistent-mode fuzzing of a tight loop with sub-microsecond execution time.
ADR-22 Benchmarking criterion crate; micro-benchmarks for tick read, event write, page hash; macro-benchmark: dret record + dret replay on standard corpus programs ad-hoc timing, perf stat only Criterion statistical analysis; CI regression detection on p50/p99 latency perf stat only wins for a one-time performance investigation on known bottlenecks where statistical rigor is not needed and setup time must be minimized; ad-hoc timing wins in a CI environment where the overhead of criterion's warm-up cycles would consume more wall-time than the allowable job budget.
ADR-23 CI/CD GitHub Actions; matrix: [ubuntu-22.04, ubuntu-24.04] × [stable, beta]; jobs: build, test, fuzz (short), audit, clippy GitLab CI, Jenkins, CircleCI Standard open-source; Linux-only (ptrace is Linux-specific); no macOS/Windows jobs GitLab CI wins when the project is hosted on a self-managed GitLab instance with access to bare-metal runners that expose PMU counters (GitHub-hosted runners are VMs that often block perf_event_open); Jenkins wins in an enterprise environment with existing build infrastructure and custom hardware provisioning.
ADR-24 Linux kernel version requirement Minimum Linux 4.14 (perf_event_open PERF_COUNT_HW_BRANCH_INSTRUCTIONS stable; seccomp-BPF GA) 5.x+ only, 3.x support rr targets similar floor; 4.14 is LTS and widely available in CI images 5.x+ wins when the project wants to use io_uring for async trace I/O (stable in 5.1+) or PTRACE_GET_SYSCALL_INFO (5.3+) for cleaner syscall interception without manual register decoding; 3.x support wins only for embedded Linux distributions frozen on older kernels where the tool is the only available debugger.
ADR-25 Packaging / distribution Single dret binary (all subcommands via dret-cli); cargo install from crates.io; optional Docker image on ghcr.io Shared libraries, split binaries Self-contained; no runtime dependency except Linux kernel; trace portability independent of host tools Shared libraries win when the project exposes dret-core and dret-replay as APIs consumed by multiple downstream tools that must avoid binary size duplication (e.g., an IDE plugin, a CI runner, and a command-line tool all linking the same replay engine).
ADR-26 Trace compression zstd (via zstd crate) applied to data.log during dret pack; events.log left uncompressed for random-access lz4, brotli, gzip zstd best ratio/speed trade-off; events.log must remain seekable by EventId without decompression lz4 wins when compression speed is the bottleneck (e.g., compressing during record on a CPU-constrained CI runner where zstd encoding is the limiting factor); brotli wins for a web-served trace archive where download size is more important than decompression speed.
ADR-27 Reproducibility of the build Cargo.lock checked in; pinned toolchain via rust-toolchain.toml; SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH set in CI Floating toolchain, no lockfile Reproducible binaries; bisectable; supply-chain audit A floating toolchain wins for a library crate published to crates.io where locking the toolchain prevents users from compiling with their own newer stable Rust; no lockfile wins for a library (not a binary) where downstream consumers determine their own lock.
ADR-28 License Apache-2.0 OR MIT (dual) GPL, LGPL, BSL Compatible with embedding in proprietary toolchains; consistent with rr (MPL) for reference; no copyleft constraint on users GPL wins when the project's primary distribution model is OS package repositories (Debian/Fedora) where GPL is the norm and copyleft enforcement is desired; LGPL wins for a shared library where proprietary applications may link without copyleft propagation but modifications to the library must remain open.
ADR-29 gdb RSP transport Unix-domain socket (/tmp/dret-<pid>.sock) default; TCP (--port N) optional Pipe, shared memory, custom protocol gdb target remote /path and target remote :N both supported; Unix socket avoids port conflicts in CI Pipe wins in a tight GDB integration where the debugger is always launched as a child of the replay tool and the user never needs to connect a second client; shared memory wins in a high-frequency single-step scenario where RSP packet framing overhead is measurable (e.g., stepping millions of instructions per second).
ADR-30 ASLR / randomization suppression personality(ADDR_NO_RANDOMIZE) + MAP_FIXED on replay; sysctl kernel.randomize_va_space=0 documented but not required Binary patching, LD_PRELOAD stub personality() is per-process; no system-wide change required; MAP_FIXED reproduces exact layout (I10) sysctl kernel.randomize_va_space=0 wins in a controlled lab where all replays run on a dedicated machine and the operator prefers a single global setting over per-process personality calls; binary patching wins for a position-independent executable where MAP_FIXED cannot be applied without modifying load addresses in the ELF header.

11. Core Schema and Core Types

11.1 dret-core — Primitive Identity Types

Type Signature Notes
EventId struct EventId(u64); Monotonic, per-trace, never reused. I1 enforced at write time.
Ticks struct Ticks(u64); RCB count. Monotonic non-decreasing per TaskId (I2).
TaskId struct TaskId(u32); Stable across record+replay; not OS pid.
Tid type Tid = u32; OS-level thread id; used only in ptrace calls; not persisted as identity.
DataRef struct DataRef(u64); Byte offset into data.log; 0 means no payload.
PageRef struct PageRef(u64); Byte offset into checkpoint page store.

Ownership: all are Copy + Clone + Eq + Ord + Hash. Serialization: little-endian u32/u64 on wire. No interior mutability.

11.2 dret-core — EventKind and TraceFrame

pub enum EventKind {
    Syscall,
    Signal { synchronous: bool },
    SchedSwitch { from: TaskId, to: TaskId },
    RdtscTrap,
    CpuidTrap,
    PatchVsyscall,
    Exec,
    Exit { code: i32 },
    Checkpoint,
}

pub struct TraceFrame {
    pub event_id: EventId,
    pub tid:      TaskId,
    pub ticks:    Ticks,
    pub kind:     EventKind,
    pub regs:     RecordedRegs,
    pub data_ref: DataRef,
}
  • Ownership: TraceFrame is Send + Sync; regs is inline (no heap).
  • Mutation: immutable after construction; only TraceWriter creates frames.
  • Persistence: framed in events.log as length-prefix + CRC32 + field-tagged payload.
  • Compatibility: unknown EventKind variant → reader skips via length prefix (P8).
  • data_ref == DataRef(0) signals no variable payload.

11.3 dret-core — RecordedRegs

pub struct RecordedRegs {
    pub rax: u64, pub rbx: u64, pub rcx: u64, pub rdx: u64,
    pub rsi: u64, pub rdi: u64, pub rsp: u64, pub rbp: u64,
