    pub r8:  u64, pub r9:  u64, pub r10: u64, pub r11: u64,
    pub r12: u64, pub r13: u64, pub r14: u64, pub r15: u64,
    pub rip: u64, pub rflags: u64,
    pub orig_rax: u64,
    pub fs_base: u64, pub gs_base: u64,
    pub cs: u16, pub ss: u16, pub ds: u16, pub es: u16, pub fs: u16, pub gs: u16,
}
  • Invariant I9: RecordedRegs at EventId E in record == same in replay; mismatch → DivergenceReport.
  • orig_rax: kernel's pre-intercept syscall number; required for restart semantics.
  • fs_base/gs_base: captured via PTRACE_GETREGSET(NT_PRSTATUS) + arch_prctl; needed for TLS (Phase 8).
  • Serialization: 22 × u64 + 6 × u16 = 188 bytes; fixed-layout little-endian; no padding.
  • Mutation: replaced atomically via PTRACE_SETREGSET on injection; never partially updated.

11.4 dret-core — Memory Types

pub struct MemRange { pub addr: u64, pub len: usize }

pub struct MemDelta { pub range: MemRange, pub bytes_ref: DataRef }

pub struct MappedSegment {
    pub range:   MemRange,
    pub perms:   MapPerms,    // r/w/x/p/s flags
    pub offset:  u64,
    pub provenance: MmapProvenance,
}

pub enum MmapProvenance { Anonymous, File { path: PathBuf, inode: u64 }, Vdso, Vsyscall }

pub struct MapPerms { pub read: bool, pub write: bool, pub exec: bool, pub shared: bool }

pub struct AddressSpace {
    pub segments: Vec<MappedSegment>,  // sorted by addr; non-overlapping
    pub brk: u64,
}
  • MemDelta: bytes live in data.log at bytes_ref; range.len must equal stored byte count.
  • AddressSpace: rebuilt incrementally from mmap.log events; snapshot on Checkpoint.
  • I10: AddressSpace at event E identical in record + replay; enforced by deterministic mmap replay.
  • Ownership: MemDelta.bytes_ref is a borrow against TraceReader; no in-memory copy retained after injection.

11.5 dret-core — SyscallRecord and SignalRecord

pub struct SyscallRecord {
    pub nr:          u32,
    pub args:        [u64; 6],
    pub ret:         i64,
    pub errno:       i32,
    pub mem_effects: Vec<MemDelta>,
}

pub struct SignalRecord {
    pub signo:            i32,
    pub si_code:          i32,
    pub si_info_bytes:    [u8; 128],   // raw siginfo_t bytes; platform-specific fields preserved
    pub synchronous:      bool,
    pub delivery_ticks:   Ticks,
}
  • SyscallRecord.mem_effects: every byte the kernel wrote into tracee address space; I4 requires exact reproduction on replay.
  • SignalRecord.delivery_ticks: pinned at record; I8 requires replay delivery at same tick.
  • si_info_bytes: raw 128-byte copy; avoids platform struct layout assumptions; decoded by consumer on demand.
  • Both types serialize into data.log via DataRef; TraceFrame.data_ref points to the encoded payload.

11.6 dret-core — Checkpoint

pub struct Checkpoint {
    pub event_id:     EventId,
    pub ticks:        Ticks,
    pub regs_per_task: HashMap<TaskId, RecordedRegs>,
    pub dirty_pages:  Vec<(MemRange, PageRef)>,
    pub fd_table:     Vec<FdRecord>,
    pub brk:          u64,
    pub mmaps:        AddressSpace,
}

pub struct FdRecord {
    pub fd:     i32,
    pub flags:  i32,
    pub kind:   FdKind,
}

pub enum FdKind { File { path: PathBuf, offset: u64 }, Pipe { id: u64 }, Socket { kind: SocketKind }, Epoll, Other }
  • I7: Checkpoint.event_id and ticks sit exactly on an event boundary; written by TraceWriter after frame flush.
  • dirty_pages: pages that differ from the previous checkpoint; PageRef indexes the checkpoint page store.
  • Phase 11 restore: PTRACE_SETREGSET all tasks + /proc/pid/mem pwrite dirty pages + re-open fds per FdRecord.
  • HashMap<TaskId, RecordedRegs>: one entry per live task at checkpoint; serialized as sorted vec for determinism.

11.7 dret-core — Scheduler Types

pub struct SchedPoint { pub tid: TaskId, pub ticks: Ticks }

pub struct SchedSwitch {
    pub event_id:    EventId,
    pub from:        TaskId,
    pub to:          TaskId,
    pub at_ticks:    Ticks,
}
  • SchedPoint: the deterministic preemption boundary; I6 guarantees exactly one task runnable at any replay instant.
  • SchedSwitch written as EventKind::SchedSwitch frame; replay re-applies in exact EventId order (I3).
  • Tick budget: dret-sched arms a perf overflow interrupt at current_ticks + budget; when fired → emit SchedSwitch.

11.8 dret-core — DivergenceReport

pub struct DivergenceReport {
    pub event_id:  EventId,
    pub tid:       TaskId,
    pub kind:      DivergenceKind,
    pub expected:  DivergenceValue,
    pub actual:    DivergenceValue,
}

pub enum DivergenceKind { RegMismatch, TickMismatch, MemHashMismatch }

pub enum DivergenceValue {
    Regs(RecordedRegs),
    Ticks(Ticks),
    MemHash { range: MemRange, hash: u64 },
}
  • P6 / I9: any DivergenceReport causes ReplaySession to abort with non-zero exit; no silent continuation.
  • DivergenceKind::MemHashMismatch: hash is xxHash3 of touched page bytes at event boundary.
  • expected sourced from TraceReader; actual from live tracee state via dret-ptrace.
  • Phase 12 bisect: binary-search EventId range; each bisect step loads nearest checkpoint + replays forward.

11.9 dret-trace — TraceWriter and TraceReader

pub struct TraceWriter { /* append-only; owns file handles for events.log, data.log, mmap.log */ }
impl TraceWriter {
    pub fn new(trace_dir: &Path) -> Result<Self, TraceError>;
    pub fn write_frame(&mut self, frame: &TraceFrame, payload: Option<&[u8]>) -> Result<DataRef, TraceError>;
    pub fn write_checkpoint(&mut self, ckpt: &Checkpoint) -> Result<(), TraceError>;
    pub fn write_mmap_event(&mut self, event: &MmapEvent) -> Result<(), TraceError>;
    pub fn flush(&mut self) -> Result<(), TraceError>;
    pub fn finish(self) -> Result<TraceHeader, TraceError>;
}

pub struct TraceReader { /* seekable; owns read-only file handles */ }
impl TraceReader {
    pub fn open(trace_dir: &Path) -> Result<Self, TraceError>;
    pub fn next_frame(&mut self) -> Result<Option<TraceFrame>, TraceError>;
    pub fn seek_to_event(&mut self, id: EventId) -> Result<(), TraceError>;
    pub fn seek_to_ticks(&mut self, tid: TaskId, ticks: Ticks) -> Result<Option<EventId>, TraceError>;
    pub fn read_payload(&self, data_ref: DataRef, len: usize) -> Result<Vec<u8>, TraceError>;
    pub fn nearest_checkpoint_before(&self, id: EventId) -> Result<Option<Checkpoint>, TraceError>;
}
  • TraceWriter: single-writer; not Sync; owned by RecordSession; flush on every checkpoint (P7 crash-recovery).
  • TraceReader: multiple independent readers allowed (replay + gdb + dump); each holds own file position.
  • Frame format: [u32 length][u8 tag][payload bytes][u32 CRC32]; length excludes CRC field.
  • Seek by Ticks: uses index built from Checkpoint table; O(checkpoints) + O(events since checkpoint).
  • Forward-compatibility: unknown tag → skip length bytes; no parse error (P8).

11.10 dret-record / dret-replay — Session Handles

pub struct RecordSession {
    pub trace_dir: PathBuf,
    // owns: TraceWriter, ptrace supervisor state, per-task tick counters
}
impl RecordSession {
    pub fn launch(argv: &[&str], trace_dir: &Path, opts: RecordOpts) -> Result<Self, RecordError>;
    pub fn run_to_exit(self) -> Result<RecordSummary, RecordError>;
}

pub struct ReplaySession {
    pub trace_dir: PathBuf,
    // owns: TraceReader, re-exec'd tracee, DivergenceChecker
}
impl ReplaySession {
    pub fn open(trace_dir: &Path, opts: ReplayOpts) -> Result<Self, ReplayError>;
    pub fn run_to_exit(self) -> Result<ReplaySummary, ReplayError>;
    pub fn run_to_event(self, target: EventId) -> Result<ReplayState, ReplayError>;
    pub fn attach_gdb(&mut self, port: u16) -> Result<GdbSession, ReplayError>;
}
  • RecordSession::run_to_exit: blocks until tracee exits; writes TraceHeader::finish; returns RecordSummary { event_count, tick_range, trace_bytes }.
  • ReplaySession::run_to_event: stop at target EventId; used by Phase 11 checkpoint-seek and Phase 13 reverse ops.
  • Both session types are !Send; they own raw OS resources (ptrace fds, perf fds, /proc/pid/mem).

11.11 Type Compatibility and Stability Guarantees

Guarantee Scope Enforcement
Stable binary layout RecordedRegs, MemRange, EventId, Ticks, TaskId, DataRef Fixed-width LE; no reordering; semver breaking change required to alter
Forward-compatible frames TraceFrame + EventKind Unknown variant tag skipped by length; new variants only added, never renumbered
Checkpoint index stability Checkpoint.event_id Always on event boundary (I7); reader may safely restore without re-scanning
No implicit clone of payload bytes MemDelta.bytes_ref DataRef is a lazy reference; TraceReader::read_payload allocates on demand
Divergence → abort DivergenceReport dret-divergence calls std::process::abort after reporting; no unwinding past divergence (P6)
Monotonicity asserted at write EventId, Ticks TraceWriter::write_frame panics in debug, returns TraceError::Monotonicity in release if violated
Deterministic serialization HashMap<TaskId, RecordedRegs> in Checkpoint Serialized as Vec sorted by TaskId; no hash-map order dependence

Phase 1 — ptrace supervisor skeleton

1.1 Objectives

  • Establish dret-ptrace as the safe, low-level ptrace(2) boundary; expose typed Rust wrappers, no raw libc beyond this crate.
  • Implement fork/exec of tracee under PTRACE_TRACEME; supervisor attaches at first SIGSTOP.
  • Set PTRACE_SETOPTIONS: PTRACE_O_EXITKILL | PTRACE_O_TRACESYSGOOD | PTRACE_O_TRACECLONE | PTRACE_O_TRACEFORK | PTRACE_O_TRACEEXEC | PTRACE_O_TRACEVFORK.
  • Build waitpid demultiplex loop mapping raw (pid, status) to typed StopEvent; handle all ptrace-stop flavors.
  • Provide single-step harness (PTRACE_SINGLESTEP); clean detach (PTRACE_DETACH) and EXITKILL safety.
  • No syscall recording yet; no trace I/O; just the supervisor event loop skeleton.

1.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

[P3: observe-do-not-perturb constrains every ptrace call to windows where tracee is stopped. P9: supervisor is the TCB; tracee is untrusted. Ref: ptrace(2) man page; Kerrisk Ch. 26; mozilla/rr src/Task.cc.]

dret-ptrace/src/lib.rs — module tree: attach, regs, stop, options, step.

stop module — typed stop discriminant [P9; Kerrisk Ch. 26 (waitpid semantics)]:

StopEvent = SyscallEntry(Tid) | SyscallExit(Tid) | Signal(Tid,signo)
          | Clone(Tid,new_tid) | Fork(Tid,new_pid) | Exec(Tid)
          | Exit(Tid,code) | GroupStop(Tid,signo) | SingleStep(Tid)

attach module [ADR-09: dret-ptrace as safe wrapper boundary]:

  • fn spawn_tracee(argv: &[&str], envp: &[&str]) -> Result<RawPid> — forks; child calls ptrace(PTRACE_TRACEME,0,0,0) then execve; parent waits for initial SIGSTOP.
  • fn set_options(pid: RawPid) -> Result<()> — wraps ptrace(PTRACE_SETOPTIONS, …) with all PTRACE_O_* flags above.
  • fn detach(pid: RawPid, sig: Option<Signal>) -> Result<()> — PTRACE_DETACH; if EXITKILL is set and supervisor dies, kernel kills tracee automatically [P7 crash-only recorder].

regs module [I9: RecordedRegs must match at every event boundary; ELF-64 ABI §3.2]:

  • fn get_regs(pid: RawPid) -> Result<RecordedRegs> — PTRACE_GETREGSET NT_PRSTATUS.
  • fn set_regs(pid: RawPid, regs: &RecordedRegs) -> Result<()> — PTRACE_SETREGSET NT_PRSTATUS.
  • RecordedRegs declared in dret-core; marshalled from user_regs_struct.

stop demux loop in dret-record/src/supervisor.rs [ADR-16: single supervisor thread; Leblanc & Mellor-Crummey 1987 nondeterminism taxonomy]:

loop {
  (pid, status) = waitpid(-1, WNOHANG | __WALL)
  event = decode_status(pid, status)  // -> StopEvent
  dispatch(event, &mut session)
}
  • decode_status: inspects WIFEXITED, WIFSTOPPED, WSTOPSIG, (status >> 8) & 0xff for ptrace-event byte.
  • dispatch routes SyscallEntry/SyscallExit to stub (Phase 2); Clone/Fork registers new TaskId; Exit finalizes task.

Single-step harness [I2: Ticks monotone; used in Phase 4 skid correction]: fn single_step(pid: RawPid) -> Result<()> — PTRACE_SINGLESTEP, deliver zero signal, await SingleStep stop.

EXITKILL safety [P7: crash-only recorder]: PTRACE_O_EXITKILL set before any PTRACE_CONT; supervisor panics must not leave orphaned tracee.

1.3 Deliverable

  • dret-ptrace compiles with all wrappers; dret-record binary can spawn /bin/true, /bin/ls, a simple loop program.
  • Supervisor loop runs to tracee exit without hang or panic.
  • dret ps subcommand prints TaskId → (pid, status) for all tracked threads at any stop.

1.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Pass condition
test_spawn_exit /bin/true traces to Exit(0); no orphan process
test_setoptions_flags PTRACE_GETEVENTMSG returns expected clone/fork child pids
test_single_step_rip rip advances by 1 instruction per step for 100 steps on a nop-sled binary
test_exitkill_on_supervisor_crash Kill supervisor mid-trace; tracee exits within 500 ms
test_detach_clean Detach while tracee sleeping; tracee resumes and exits normally

1.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
fixtures/tracee/exit0.c Minimal _exit(0) Exit(tid=1, code=0) event
fixtures/tracee/fork_child.c Single fork() Fork(parent,child) + 2× Exit
fixtures/tracee/clone_thread.c pthread_create Clone(parent,child) stop
fixtures/tracee/exec_chain.c execve after start Exec(tid) stop, new binary
fixtures/malformed/attach_dead_pid.sh Attach to nonexistent pid ESRCH error, no crash

1.6 Pitfalls

  • Double-stop on exec: kernel sends SIGTRAP | (PTRACE_EVENT_EXEC << 8) AND a SIGSTOP; demux must consume both or supervisor deadlocks.
  • __WALL missing: waitpid without __WALL misses thread stops; manifests as supervisory loop stall.
  • PTRACE_SETOPTIONS timing: must be called before first PTRACE_CONT after SIGSTOP; if called late, clone/fork events are missed.
  • Signal injection confusion: on a signal-delivery stop, resuming with PTRACE_CONT, 0 suppresses the signal; resuming with the real signo delivers it. Wrong choice silently changes tracee behavior (P3 violation).
  • Group-stop vs signal-delivery stop: both arrive as WIFSTOPPED; distinguish by (status >> 16) and by checking PTRACE_GETSIGINFO for si_code == SI_TKILL.
  • EXITKILL scope: only kills the thread group leader by default; surviving threads from pthread_create may escape if the option is set after clone events.

1.7 Performance Concerns

  • Each waitpid round-trip is a kernel context switch; minimize by using -1 (any child) not per-pid polling.
  • Demux match on stop type is hot path; keep allocation-free with stack-only StopEvent.
  • PTRACE_GETREGSET at every stop: ~1–3 µs each; acceptable in Phase 1; batched in Phase 14.

1.8 Edge Cases

  • Tracee calls exec from a non-leader thread: new Exec(tid) replaces thread group; all other tids vanish; supervisor must purge stale TaskId entries.
  • vfork semantics: parent is blocked until child execs/exits; supervisor must not issue PTRACE_CONT to parent until child Exec/Exit received.
  • Signal sent to tracee while it is already in ptrace-stop: queued, delivered on next resume.
  • SIGKILL to tracee bypasses ptrace: tracee may exit without SyscallExit matching a prior SyscallEntry; supervisor must handle unmatched entry.
  • Tracee with prctl(PR_SET_PTRACER, …) or seccomp already applied before spawn: unexpected stops; document as unsupported configuration.

1.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Containment Recovery
Supervisor panic (bug) Rust panic hook EXITKILL kills tracee Restart from scratch
waitpid EINTR errno == EINTR Retry loop Transparent
Orphan tracee after supervisor exit Process table scan EXITKILL Kernel cleans up
PTRACE_SETOPTIONS ESRCH Result::Err Abort record session Surface error to CLI

1.10 Operational Notes

  • DRET_LOG=trace enables per-stop logging: [stop] tid={} event={:?}.
  • dret ps reads live stop state from supervisor's HashMap<TaskId, TaskState>.
  • Metric counters (exported via dret-cli --stats): stops_total, clone_events, exec_events, exit_events.
  • Supervisor thread must be pinned to a dedicated OS thread (std::thread::Builder::spawn with affinity); never share with tracee threads.

Phase 2 — syscall interception + ABI tables

2.1 Objectives

  • Intercept every syscall entry/exit via PTRACE_SYSCALL stop pairs; no injection yet.
  • Build dret-syscall: x86-64 syscall number → SyscallSig table; argument/return marshalling from RecordedRegs.
  • Classify each syscall argument as scalar, pointer-in, pointer-out, or pointer-inout; derive MemDelta descriptors for every pointer-out/inout arg and for kernel writes into tracee memory.
  • record-passthrough: at entry, note args; let real syscall execute; at exit, read ret + errno + all mem_effects; construct SyscallRecord.
  • I1 (EventId), I2 (Ticks) not yet enforced; done in Phase 3–4.

2.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

[ADR-12: PTRACE_SYSCALL + PTRACE_SYSEMU as interception mechanism. ADR-06: hand-written binary serialization. P3: capture bytes, never alter tracee execution. P10: every byte traceable to a recorded frame. Ref: strace/strace syscallent.h; O'Callahan et al. 2017 (syscall recording).]

dret-syscall/src/table.rs — static array indexed by syscall nr (0–450+) [ADR-06: no schema compiler; O'Callahan 2017 per-syscall effect tables from rr/src/record_syscall.cc]:

SyscallSig { nr: u32, name: &'static str, args: [ArgKind; 6] /* Scalar|PtrIn(size_expr)|PtrOut(size_expr)|PtrInOut(size_expr) */, ret_kind: RetKind /* Scalar|FdOrNeg1|VoidOrNeg1|Ptr */ }

Table generated from a build-time script reading <sys/syscall.h> + manual annotations for ~200 most common Linux x86-64 syscalls. Remaining unknown syscalls tagged ArgKind::Unknown; recorder logs warning, captures no mem effects.

dret-syscall/src/marshal.rs:

  • fn read_args(regs: &RecordedRegs) -> [u64; 6] — reads rdi, rsi, rdx, r10, r8, r9.
  • fn read_ret(regs: &RecordedRegs) -> (i64, u32) — (rax as i64, -rax as u32 if rax > u64::MAX - 4095).
  • fn read_mem_effects(pid, sig, args, ret) -> Result<Vec<MemDelta>> — for each PtrOut/PtrInOut arg: read len from appropriate register/retval per size_expr, pread pid's /proc/pid/mem at arg address, store bytes.

size_expr encoding (static per arg slot):

Code Meaning
Ret size = syscall return value (e.g., read)
Arg(n) size = value of nth arg register
Fixed(n) size = n bytes (e.g., gettimeofday → 16)
Socklen read via socklen_t pointer arg
StatStruct fixed 144 bytes (struct stat)

dret-record/src/record_loop.rs — per-task state machine enum SyscallState { Idle, AtEntry { nr, args, entry_regs } }. On SyscallEntry(tid): read regs → decode nr + args → store AtEntry. On SyscallExit(tid): read regs → read ret → read mem effects → emit SyscallRecord to pending queue (Phase 3 writes it).

SyscallRecord shape (in dret-core): SyscallRecord { nr: u32, args: [u64;6], ret: i64, errno: u32, mem_effects: Vec<MemDelta>, entry_regs: RecordedRegs }.

Special syscalls requiring custom handling:

Syscall Custom logic
mmap/mmap2 Record full AddressSpace delta; update supervisor mmap table
munmap Remove range from AddressSpace
mremap Update range in AddressSpace
brk Record new brk value
clone/clone3 New TaskId allocation; handled before mem effects
exit/exit_group Finalize task
read/pread64 PtrOut(Ret): captures actual bytes read
write/pwrite64 PtrIn(Arg(2)): no capture needed
getdents64 PtrOut(Ret)
recv/recvfrom/recvmsg Socket mem effects per msg structure

2.3 Deliverable

  • dret record /path/to/binary runs; supervisor prints per-syscall log lines with nr, args, ret.
  • dret dump (no trace file yet) prints decoded SyscallRecord structs to stdout.
  • All syscalls for /bin/ls, /bin/cat, a threaded hello binary are decoded without panics.

2.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Pass condition
test_abi_read read(3, buf, 100) → args [3,ptr,100,0,0,0], ret=bytes, mem_effects len = ret
test_abi_stat stat(path, &s) → mem_effects[0].bytes.len() == 144
test_abi_mmap mmap(0,4096,PROT_RW,MAP_ANON,-1,0) → ret = mapped addr, AddressSpace updated
test_unknown_syscall nr=999 → ArgKind::Unknown, warning logged, no crash
test_failed_syscall open("nonexistent",0) → ret=-1, errno=ENOENT, no mem effects
test_passthrough_idempotent Traced /bin/true produces same exit code as untraced

2.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose
fixtures/tracee/syscall_read.c read(0, buf, 64) from a pipe; verify mem_effect bytes match piped data
fixtures/tracee/syscall_mmap.c mmap + mprotect + munmap sequence; verify AddressSpace transitions
fixtures/tracee/syscall_clone.c `clone(CLONE_VM
fixtures/abi/golden_table.txt 50 syscall signatures as text; CI diff checks no regressions
fixtures/malformed/mid_exec_syscall.sh Kill tracee between entry/exit; supervisor handles unmatched entry

2.6 Pitfalls

  • Restarted syscalls (ERESTART*): kernel returns ERESTARTSYS/ERESTARTNOINTR as ret at exit stop; supervisor must re-enter state machine, not record a false failure; detect by ret < 0 && -ret in {512..516}.
  • clone arg layout: clone3 uses a clone_args struct (PtrIn, not registers); table must special-case it; naively reading rdi..r9 gives wrong childstack.
  • recvmsg scattered buffers: msghdr.msg_iov is an array of iovec; pread must walk each iov segment; single PtrOut(Ret) is insufficient.
  • mmap with file backing: must record the fd + offset + path so replay can recreate the mapping; pure bytes capture not sufficient; AddressSpace entry stores MmapProvenance::File { dev, ino, offset }.
  • TOCTOU on /proc/pid/mem: read mem effects at syscall exit, after kernel wrote them; read before PTRACE_CONT for next stop; never read after PTRACE_CONT.
  • Large write pointer arg: do not capture PtrIn bytes for writes — they are tracee input, not nondeterminism; capturing them inflates trace and is wrong.

2.7 Performance Concerns

  • /proc/pid/mem pread: 1 fd open per tracee at attach; keep fd cached in TaskState; pread is ~2–5 µs per call.
  • Vec<MemDelta> allocation per syscall: pool allocator or arena; hot path for read-heavy workloads.
  • ABI table lookup: static array indexed by nr; O(1); no hash map.
  • size_expr evaluation: resolved at exit stop, not entry; avoids storing evaluated sizes in AtEntry.

2.8 Edge Cases

  • Syscall nr > table length: default SyscallSig::unknown(nr).
  • Tracee uses int 0x80 (32-bit ABI on 64-bit kernel): different syscall nr space; detect via cs == 0x23; log warning; do not record mem effects.
  • Zero-length read return (EOF or EAGAIN): PtrOut(Ret) → 0 bytes; mem_effects empty, not missing.
  • writev / readv: multiple iovecs; must iterate all segments for PtrOut.
  • Syscall at entry, then tracee receives SIGKILL before exit stop: entry logged without matching exit; supervisor must discard AtEntry on Exit(tid).

2.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Containment
/proc/pid/mem ESRCH (tracee exited between stops) Result::Err(ESRCH) Discard current SyscallRecord; finalize task
Table produces wrong size_expr (silent data loss) Golden fixture diff in CI Reject PR until fixture passes
Restarted syscall mis-recorded Replay divergence (Phase 12) Restart detector added to record loop
clone3 wrong arg decode test_abi_clone3 failure Fix table entry

2.10 Operational Notes

  • DRET_LOG=debug logs each SyscallRecord as [syscall] nr={} name={} ret={} effects={}.
  • dret dump --syscalls <trace> (Phase 3+) prints full decoded table.
  • Metric: syscall_records_total{nr} counter; mem_effect_bytes_total histogram.
  • Unknown syscalls emit WARN unknown_syscall nr={} log line; counted in unknown_syscalls_total.

Phase 3 — trace container format

3.1 Objectives

  • Define and implement the on-disk trace format: events.log, data.log, mmap.log, ckpt/ inside a trace directory.
  • Implement TraceWriter (append-only, framed, CRC-protected) and TraceReader (seekable by EventId and Ticks).
  • Enforce I1 (EventId strictly increasing), I7 (checkpoints on event boundaries).
  • Forward-compatible: unknown frame tags skipped by older readers; newer writers never break a trace prefix.
  • No computation here; pure I/O and framing contracts.

3.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

[P7: append-only, fsync at checkpoint, crash-safe prefix. P8: forward-compatible tags — unknown kinds skipped via length field. I1: EventId strictly monotone enforced at write. ADR-05: custom binary framing rationale. Ref: mozilla/rr TraceStream.cc; Xu et al. 2003 (checkpoint design).]

Trace directory layout [ADR-05: custom framing wins over SQLite for append-only crash-safe semantics]: <trace-dir>/{header.toml (versioned metadata), events.log (framed TraceFrame records, append-only), data.log (raw variable-length payloads: MemDelta bytes etc.), mmap.log (AddressSpace events), ckpt/<event_id>.ckpt (serialized Checkpoint, Phase 11), index/ticks.idx (sparse (TaskId,Ticks)→byte offset), index/event.idx (dense EventId→byte offset, 8 B/entry)}.

header.toml fields:

Field Type Notes
version u32 Format version; currently 1
created_at_ns u64 wall clock, informational only
host_arch str "x86_64"
tracee_argv [str] for display
tracee_exe_sha256 str binary identity
first_event_id u64 always 1

events.log frame wire format (little-endian): [magic: u16=0xDE73][tag: u16][frame_len: u32][payload: bytes][crc32: u32]. tag identifies frame type; unknown tag → skip frame_len bytes. payload is bincode-serialized TraceFrame (or sub-type per tag). crc32 covers tag + frame_len + payload. On write: always append; fsync at checkpoint boundary (I7).

Frame tag registry:

Tag Type Introduced
0x0001 TraceFrame (Syscall/Signal/SchedSwitch) v1
0x0002 TraceFrame (RdtscTrap/CpuidTrap) v1
0x0003 TraceFrame (Exec/Exit) v1
0x0010 CheckpointRef { event_id, ckpt_path } v1
0x00FF ExtensionFrame { key: str, bytes } v1 (skip-unknown)

data.log: raw byte stream; DataRef(u64) is byte offset; length encoded inline in TraceFrame.data_ref as (offset, len) pair packed in 2×u32. Append-only; no deletion.

mmap.log: sequence of MmapEvent { event_id, kind: Map|Unmap|Remap, range, prot, flags, provenance } in same framing as events.log.

TraceWriter (dret-trace/src/writer.rs): fn new(dir: &Path) -> Result<TraceWriter>; fn append_frame(&mut self, frame: &TraceFrame) -> Result<EventId>; fn append_data(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<DataRef>; fn append_mmap(&mut self, ev: &MmapEvent) -> Result<()>; fn flush_checkpoint(&mut self) -> Result<()> (fsync + update index); fn close(self) -> Result<()>. Maintains in-memory next_event_id: u64, incremented atomically (single-writer); writes event.idx entry at each append_frame; flush_checkpoint flushes both events.log and data.log and updates ticks.idx sparse entries.

TraceReader (dret-trace/src/reader.rs): fn open(dir: &Path) -> Result<TraceReader>; fn read_frame(&mut self) -> Result<Option<TraceFrame>> (sequential); fn seek_event(&mut self, id: EventId) -> Result<()> (via event.idx); fn seek_ticks(&mut self, tid: TaskId, t: Ticks) -> Result<()> (via ticks.idx; linear scan within checkpoint window); fn read_data(&self, r: DataRef) -> Result<Vec<u8>>; fn read_mmap_at(&self, event_id: EventId) -> Result<AddressSpace>. Index loads on open, giving O(1) seek by EventId; seek_ticks binary-searches ticks.idx for the nearest entry ≤ target, then scans forward.

Bincode schema for TraceFrame: all fields little-endian fixed-width; Vec prefixed with u32 length; RecordedRegs is 18×u64 (rax..r15, rip, rflags, fs_base, gs_base, orig_rax); unknown trailing fields ignored if frame_len > decoded size (forward compat).

3.3 Deliverable

  • dret-trace crate: TraceWriter and TraceReader compile; round-trip test passes.
  • dret record /bin/ls writes a valid trace dir; dret dump <trace> prints all TraceFrames with decoded fields.
  • CRC failures on truncated file detected and reported.

3.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Pass condition
test_roundtrip_1000_frames Write 1000 frames; read back all; fields identical; EventIds 1..=1000
test_seek_event seek_event(500) returns frame with event_id==500 in O(1) index lookup
test_crc_corruption Flip byte in frame payload; reader returns Err(CrcMismatch)
test_unknown_tag_skip Insert tag=0xBEEF frame; reader skips it; subsequent frames read correctly
test_data_roundtrip Append 64 KB payload; read_data returns identical bytes
test_ticks_seek seek_ticks(tid=1, ticks=5000) lands within 100 ticks of target
test_concurrent_open Two TraceReaders open same dir simultaneously; no interference

3.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose
fixtures/trace/v1_golden.trace/ Golden trace dir; reader must decode all frames without error
fixtures/trace/truncated_frame.trace/ Frame cut mid-payload; reader returns Err(UnexpectedEof)
fixtures/trace/wrong_magic.trace/ Magic bytes 0x0000; reader returns Err(BadMagic)
fixtures/trace/future_version.trace/ version=99; reader logs warning, reads known tags, skips unknown
fixtures/trace/large_data.trace/ data.log with 100 MB payload; DataRef seek correct

3.6 Pitfalls

  • Non-atomic append on crash: partial frame write at end; reader must detect truncated CRC and treat as "replayable prefix" (P7); close() must write a sentinel end-of-log frame.
  • Index drift: if event.idx is written lazily, a crash leaves index incomplete; replay must rebuild index by scanning events.log linearly if index is missing or shorter than log.
  • bincode field order: adding a field to TraceFrame without a version bump silently misaligns all subsequent fields; always bump version on any schema change; use tagged/length-prefixed encoding for extension.
  • data.log offset overflow: DataRef(u64) supports 16 EB; but (offset, len) packed in 2×u32 limits payload to 4 GB each; use explicit u64 pairs in v1 schema.
  • ticks.idx sparse granularity: if too sparse (e.g., one entry per 100 K events), seek_ticks forward-scan window is large; target ≤1000-event window; emit ticks index entry at every checkpoint.

3.7 Performance Concerns

  • append_frame: sequential write; BufWriter 64 KB buffer; flush only at checkpoint.
  • event.idx: 8-byte entries; 1 M events → 8 MB in memory; acceptable.
  • CRC32 (hardware CRC32C via crc32fast): ~10 GB/s; negligible overhead per frame.
  • read_data for large mem effects: zero-copy via mmap of data.log in TraceReader; avoid per-call Vec allocation.
  • Index rebuild on crash: ~50 MB/s scan; 1 GB log → 20 s; acceptable recovery path.

3.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty trace dir (record killed before first event): TraceReader::open returns Err(EmptyTrace).
  • data.log and events.log out of sync (partial append_data before append_frame): DataRef in frame points past end of data.log; reader returns Err(DataRefOutOfBounds).
  • Multiple TraceWriters to same dir: unsupported; detect via lock file trace.lock; second open returns Err(AlreadyLocked).
  • Frame frame_len larger than file: reader returns Err(FrameTooLarge) without reading past EOF.
  • EventId wrap-around at u64::MAX: impossible in practice (2^64 events); assert in writer.

3.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
Disk full mid-write write returns ENOSPC Current frame lost Trace prefix valid to last fsynced frame; reopen trace, report truncation
CRC mismatch read_frame returns Err(CrcMismatch) Single frame unreadable Skip to next magic sync point; log corrupt event count
Index file missing/truncated open detects index shorter than log Full seek-by-id unavailable Rebuild index; log WARN rebuilding_index
data.log truncated read_data returns Err(DataRefOutOfBounds) Frames referencing lost payloads unreadable Report first affected EventId; frames before it usable

3.10 Operational Notes

  • dret dump <trace> prints: EventId | TaskId | Ticks | Kind | rip | ret per frame; --data flag includes hex dump of mem effects.
  • dret pack <trace> <out.dtpk> (Phase 14) produces self-contained archive.
  • Metric trace_bytes_written_total{log} (events/data/mmap) exported via --stats.
  • header.toml is human-readable; operators can inspect without tooling.
  • Trace dir is the unit of archival; whole dir must be preserved for replay; partial dirs are corrupt.

Phase 4 — Deterministic Clock (RCB Ticks)

4.1 Objectives

  • Assign every event an addressable position in the execution via Ticks(u64) — retired-conditional-branch count, per-task, monotonic.
  • Open one perf_event_open fd per tracee task configured for PERF_COUNT_HW_BRANCH_INSTRUCTIONS (or PERF_COUNT_HW_BRANCH_MISSES fallback) in user-only mode, interrupt-on-overflow disabled.
  • Read current tick value at each ptrace stop and embed it in every TraceFrame.
  • Apply overcount/skid correction so that the recorded tick at a syscall entry equals the true RCB count the tracee had when the syscall instruction executed.
  • Expose dret-time as the single source of clock truth; prohibit wall-clock usage in any event record.
  • Constraint: Ticks are per-TaskId; TaskId must be stable across record and replay (not the OS tid). I2 enforced here.

4.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

[P5: RCB is the sole clock — wall time never used for ordering. I2: Ticks monotonic non-decreasing per TaskId. ADR-10: perf_event_open chosen over RDPMC/Intel PT. Ref: O'Callahan et al. 2017 (RCB clock); Gregg Systems Performance Ch. 13; Choi & Srinivasan 1998 (JVM RCB analog); Intel SDM Vol. 3B Ch. 18.]

dret-time crate — public surface [ADR-10: per-task perf_event_open fd; O'Callahan 2017 RCB mechanism proven in rr]:

pub struct PerfCounter { fd: OwnedFd, task: TaskId }
impl PerfCounter { fn open(pid: Pid, cpu: i32) -> Result<Self>; fn read(&self) -> Result<Ticks>; fn reset(&self) -> Result<()>; fn enable(&self) -> Result<()>; fn disable(&self) -> Result<()>; }
pub fn skid_correction(raw: Ticks, kind: EventKind, regs: &RecordedRegs) -> Ticks;

perf_event_open configuration:

Field Value
type PERF_TYPE_HARDWARE
config PERF_COUNT_HW_BRANCH_INSTRUCTIONS
exclude_kernel 1
exclude_hv 1
inherit 0
pinned 1
disabled 1 (enable after exec)
pid arg tracee tid
cpu arg -1 (any CPU, but tracee is pinned to one CPU in Phase 7)
  • ioctl(fd, PERF_EVENT_IOC_RESET) at exec entry; ENABLE before PTRACE_CONT; DISABLE before reading to avoid counter drift. Read via read(fd, &mut u64_buf, 8); no mmap ring buffer needed.
  • Fallback path: if PERF_COUNT_HW_BRANCH_INSTRUCTIONS is unavailable (some VMs), try PERF_COUNT_HW_BUS_CYCLES then fail with UnsupportedHardware.

Skid correction algorithm (description, not code):

  • At a PTRACE_SYSCALL stop, the kernel delivers the stop slightly after the syscall instruction; the counter may have advanced by 0-15 RCBs past the actual syscall site. Correct by single-stepping backward from the stop's rip to the syscall instruction boundary (1-3 steps max), subtracting the measured delta from the raw reading.
  • Cache correction deltas per (nr, rip) pair in a HashMap<u64, i8> inside SkidTable; amortize cost after warmup. For signals (async): correction is zero (signal stops are ptrace stops, not instruction stops).

Tick embedding: RecordSession::on_stop() calls PerfCounter::read() immediately after waitpid returns, before any other work; result stored as TraceFrame.ticks, also in SyscallRecord, SignalRecord, SchedSwitch.

Per-task counter map: HashMap<TaskId, PerfCounter> in RecordSession; new entry on PTRACE_EVENT_CLONE/FORK, counter reset at thread start. On TaskExit: read final ticks, emit EventKind::Exit with that tick, then close fd.

dret-core type enforcement: Ticks is #[repr(transparent)] over u64; comparison only via PartialOrd; no arithmetic except saturating_sub in skid correction.

4.3 Deliverable

  • dret-time compiles; PerfCounter::open/read/reset round-trips against a live tracee.
  • dret record embeds non-zero, strictly increasing Ticks in every TraceFrame for a single-threaded C program (e.g., md5sum /dev/urandom).
  • dret dump prints EventId, TaskId, Ticks, EventKind columns; operator can visually verify monotonicity; dret-divergence tick comparison passes on a replayed single-threaded trace (Phase 6 prerequisite).

4.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Pass Condition
Monotonicity ticks[n+1] >= ticks[n] for all consecutive same-TaskId frames; test against 10k-event trace
Skid bound Recorded ticks at syscall entry within ±20 RCB of independently-measured value via /proc/self/fdinfo
Counter isolation Tick readings for TaskA unaffected by TaskB running concurrently (verify in Phase 7/8; placeholder assertion here)
Fallback detection UnsupportedHardware returned and logged when PERF_COUNT_HW_BRANCH_INSTRUCTIONS unavailable in CI VM
I2 enforcement Any trace where ticks decreases within a task fails TraceReader::validate_invariants()

4.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected Output
tests/fixtures/rcb_monotonic.trace 500-event single-thread trace; tick must increase every event validate_invariants() passes
tests/fixtures/rcb_skid_spike.trace Artificially injected +50 tick spike at event 100 I2 violation detected at event 100
tests/fixtures/rcb_zero_ticks.trace All ticks = 0 (broken counter) ZeroTicksError on open
tests/fixtures/perf_unavailable.json Mock perf_event_open returning ENODEV UnsupportedHardware variant
benches/rcb_read_latency PerfCounter::read() in a tight loop, 1M reads p99 < 500 ns

4.6 Pitfalls

  • Counter multiplexing: if pinned=1 is not set, the kernel may time-multiplex the counter across multiple events, returning a scaled value. Symptom: ticks jump by thousands at random stops. Fix: assert PERF_FORMAT_ID scale == 1.0 or fail.
  • rdtsc vs RCB confusion: using rdtsc (wall-ish) as backup silently breaks P5. Enforce at type level — Ticks is only ever populated from PerfCounter::read().
  • clone() counter inheritance: inherit=0 ensures child does NOT inherit parent's counter; if set to 1, child tick starts at parent value, breaking monotonicity across tasks. Verify inherit=0 in every PerfCounter::open call.
  • Counter not enabled after exec: if PTRACE_EVENT_EXEC fires before PERF_EVENT_IOC_ENABLE, first events have tick=0. Fix: enable in on_exec_stop(), not on task creation.
  • Kernel vs user branch counting: if exclude_kernel=0, kernel branch instructions pollute the count. Symptom: tick values far exceed expected for simple programs. Fix: always exclude_kernel=1.
  • VM/container environments: KVM guests may not expose PERF_COUNT_HW_BRANCH_INSTRUCTIONS; manifest as ENOENT or EINVAL. Detection: explicit probe at recorder startup; emit DRET_WARN_NO_RCB.

4.7 Performance Concerns

  • PerfCounter::read() is a read(2) syscall — ~200-500 ns each; called once per ptrace stop; at 100k stops/s this is ~50 ms/s overhead.
  • Batch-disable/read/enable sequence (three ioctl calls) is more expensive than a single read; profile to determine if disable before read is necessary or if single-read suffices (counter drift during read window is < 1 RCB).
  • SkidTable HashMap lookup per stop: use FxHashMap (non-cryptographic) to reduce hash cost; key is u64 rip so no heap alloc.
  • perf_event_open fd count grows with thread count; Linux default limit fs.file-max applies; pre-check and log.

4.8 Edge Cases

  • Task with zero RCBs: a thread that does only clone + immediate exit may produce 0 RCBs; valid, not a counter error.
  • Tick overflow: u64 at 10^9 RCBs/s would overflow after ~584 years; ignore but document.
  • Counter reset race: if PERF_EVENT_IOC_RESET races with PTRACE_CONT, a stale count may be read at next stop; serialize reset inside on_exec_stop() before any PTRACE_CONT.
  • Multiple CPUs before Phase 7: until sched_setaffinity is applied, the tracee may migrate CPUs; perf_event_open with cpu=-1 and pid=tid tracks across migration, but the counter is not pinned and may multiplex.
  • Seccomp interaction: seccomp stops fire before ptrace syscall-entry stops; tick must be read at ptrace stop, not seccomp stop (different stop points).

4.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
perf_event_open returns EACCES Startup probe; log EPERM Record aborted Run as root or grant CAP_PERFMON / perf_event_paranoid=1
Counter multiplexed (not pinned) Scale factor != 1 in PERF_FORMAT_LOST Tick values unreliable; divergence at replay Set pinned=1; fail if not satisfied
FD leak (no close on TaskExit) /proc/self/fd count grows Eventual EMFILE crash Drop PerfCounter on TaskId removal
Skid table grows unbounded (many unique rip values) Heap monitor metric dret.skid_table_entries OOM Cap at 4096 entries; evict LRU

4.10 Operational Notes

  • Metric: dret.ticks_per_event_avg (running mean) exposed via --stats flag; sharp drop indicates counter reset or task exit.
  • Log line at every PerfCounter::open: [dret-time] pid={} tid={} fd={} hw=BRANCH_INSTRUCTIONS.
  • dret dump --ticks prints per-event tick deltas; useful to identify long-running loops between events (large deltas) vs rapid syscalls (small deltas).
  • Deployment: perf_event_paranoid must be <= 1 (/proc/sys/kernel/perf_event_paranoid); document as system prerequisite.
  • Rollback: if RCB counter proves unreliable on a specific CPU microarchitecture, record a DRET_WARN_RCB_UNRELIABLE event in the trace header; replay will abort with a clear message rather than silently diverge.

Phase 5 — Memory Capture

5.1 Objectives

  • Record every byte a syscall writes into tracee address space as a MemDelta attached to the SyscallRecord.
  • Capture full COW page snapshots at Checkpoint events for seekable replay (Phase 11 dependency introduced here).
  • Track the tracee's AddressSpace continuously via mmap/munmap/mremap/brk/mprotect interception; emit mmap.log events.
  • Provide dret-mem as the abstraction layer over /proc/pid/mem (no other crate reads tracee memory directly); constraint I10 — address-space layout at event E is identical in record and replay.

5.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

[I4: injected MemDelta must match recorded byte-for-byte. I10: AddressSpace layout identical in record and replay. P3: read at syscall-exit stop only. ADR-14: /proc/pid/mem pread chosen. Ref: King & Chen 2002 (memory-replay fidelity); Narayanasamy et al. 2005 (page-diff delta); proc(5) man page.]

dret-mem crate — public surface [ADR-14: /proc/pid/mem pread fastest non-kernel path; Narayanasamy 2005 page-diff delta strategy]:

pub struct MemAccess { pid: Pid }
impl MemAccess { fn open(pid: Pid) -> Result<Self>; fn read(&self, addr: u64, buf: &mut [u8]) -> Result<()>; fn write(&self, addr: u64, buf: &[u8]) -> Result<()>; }
pub struct AddressSpace { segments: BTreeMap<u64, Segment> }
pub struct Segment { start: u64, end: u64, perms: Perms, kind: SegmentKind }
pub fn snapshot_pages(mem: &MemAccess, ranges: &[MemRange]) -> Result<Vec<(MemRange, Vec<u8>)>>;
pub fn diff_pages(before: &[(MemRange, Vec<u8>)], after: &MemAccess) -> Result<Vec<MemDelta>>;

Syscall memory-effect capture (dret-syscall integration): each syscall nr in dret-syscall's ABI table carries a MemEffectDescriptor — a list of (ArgIndex, Direction, LenSource) tuples; at syscall exit stop, for each OUT/INOUT arg, call MemAccess::read(ptr, len) to capture the written bytes and store as MemDelta { range, bytes_ref: DataRef }.

  • DataRef is an offset into data.log written by TraceWriter::append_data(). Special cases requiring explicit descriptors:
Syscall group Memory effect source
read, pread64 buf[0..ret]
readv, preadv each iovec
recvmsg, recvfrom msg_iov + msg_control
mmap no data; but AddressSpace updated
getdents64 buf[0..ret]
clock_gettime timespec struct
getsockname, getpeername addr buf

AddressSpace tracking: on PTRACE_EVENT_EXEC, re-parse /proc/pid/maps into AddressSpace as baseline. On mmap2/mmap exit: parse ret (new addr) + args (len, prot, flags, fd, offset); insert/update Segment. On munmap exit: remove or split overlapping segments; on mremap exit: move/resize segment, updating the BTreeMap key.

  • On brk exit: update brk pointer, inserting an anonymous segment if grown; on mprotect exit: update Segment.perms. Emit an mmap.log frame for each change: { event_id, tid, kind: MmapEvent, addr, len, prot, flags, fd_backing }.

COW page snapshot (for Checkpoint): snapshot_pages(mem, &[MemRange]) reads each range via MemAccess::read in 4 KB page-aligned chunks, storing raw bytes in data.log via TraceWriter; Checkpoint holds Vec<(MemRange, PageRef)> where PageRef is a DataRef into data.log.

  • Only writable (W) segments are snapshotted — read-only mapped files are remapped from the original file on replay. Delta compression (optional Phase 5, required Phase 14): diff_pages compares the current snapshot against the previous checkpoint's, storing only changed pages.

Memory write injection (replay, used in Phase 6): MemAccess::write(addr, buf) over /proc/pid/mem via pwrite64; used by ReplaySession to apply MemDelta at each syscall stop.

Segment kind tagging: SegmentKind: File(PathBuf, offset) | Anonymous | Stack | Vdso | Vsyscall | Heap, tag derived from the /proc/pid/maps pathname column.

5.3 Deliverable

  • dret-mem compiles with MemAccess::read/write and snapshot_pages passing unit tests against /proc/self/mem.
  • dret record captures non-empty mem_effects for read(2) and recvmsg(2) syscalls; dret dump --mem prints DataRef offsets and byte lengths for each event's MemDeltas.
  • AddressSpace correctly reflects a 50-syscall trace of a program that calls mmap/munmap, verified by diffing against /proc/pid/maps snapshots taken at each stop; snapshot_pages produces a byte-identical re-read of all writable segments.

5.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Pass Condition
MemDelta completeness For read(buf, 4096) returning 512: mem_effect[0].range.len == 512; bytes match actual bytes read
AddressSpace fidelity After 100 mmap/munmap ops: AddressSpace BTreeMap matches /proc/pid/maps parse; zero missing/extra segments
Snapshot integrity snapshot_pages then re-read via MemAccess::read produces identical bytes (SHA-256 check)
I10 enforcement Any AddressSpace divergence (missing segment at event E) is flagged by dret-divergence
No cross-task reads MemAccess holds Pid; unit test verifies wrong-pid access returns ESRCH

5.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
tests/fixtures/mem_read_effect.trace Single read(2) syscall; 1 MemDelta range.len == ret_val; bytes match
tests/fixtures/mmap_lifecycle.trace 20 mmap + 10 munmap events; AddressSpace log Final AddressSpace == parsed /proc/pid/maps
tests/fixtures/snapshot_4k_page.bin 4 KB page snapshot reference SHA-256 pinned in fixture manifest
tests/fixtures/mem_zero_len_effect.trace Syscall returns 0 bytes (EOF) MemDelta with len=0; no data.log bytes written
tests/fixtures/large_iov.trace readv with 16 iovecs, 64 KB total 16 MemDeltas; total bytes == ret

5.6 Pitfalls

  • TOCTOU on pointer args: reading arg pointer at syscall exit instead of entry means the tracee may have already overwritten the buffer. Fix: for IN args (data sent to kernel), read at entry; for OUT args (kernel wrote result), read at exit.
  • Lazy mmap vs eager snapshot: mmapping a file with MAP_PRIVATE adds a segment but the pages are not yet resident; snapshot of unpopulated pages returns zeros, not file content. Fix: for file-backed read-only segments, record the file path + offset, not the page content. Reconstruct on replay by re-mmap'ing the same file.
  • Shared memory segments: MAP_SHARED segments are visible to other processes; reading them at syscall exit captures a snapshot that may already be stale. Document as a known limitation for Phase 5; full shared-memory replay is deferred.
  • /proc/pid/mem vs PTRACE_PEEKDATA: PTRACE_PEEKDATA reads 8 bytes per call (200k+ calls for a 64 KB iov); /proc/pid/mem pread is O(1) overhead. Always use MemAccess (pread path).
  • Segment split on partial munmap: munmap(addr+4096, 4096) in the middle of a 3-page segment splits it into two; BTreeMap must handle range-split correctly.
  • vDSO/vsyscall regions: these are kernel-mapped pages; attempting to snapshot them via /proc/pid/mem returns zeros or EFAULT; skip them in snapshot_pages based on SegmentKind::Vdso.

5.7 Performance Concerns

  • /proc/pid/mem pread: single call per contiguous range; batch by merging adjacent MemDelta ranges before read.
  • Large socket buffers (e.g., recv returning 1 MB): full byte copy into data.log dominates record overhead; use DataRef deduplication (content-hash cache) in Phase 14.
  • BTreeMap segment lookup on every mmap event: O(log n) with n typically < 200; negligible.
  • snapshot_pages at checkpoint: checkpoint every ~10k events; typical dirty set < 10 MB; 10 MB/snapshot at 1 GB/s pread = 10 ms; acceptable.
  • Iov iteration for readv/writev: copy each iovec descriptor from tracee space first (one MemAccess::read call), then loop; avoid N separate pread calls for N iovecs by coalescing adjacent iovecs.

5.8 Edge Cases

  • mmap with MAP_FIXED over an existing segment: old segment must be evicted from AddressSpace before inserting the new one.
  • mremap that moves a segment to a new address: BTreeMap key changes; must remove old key and insert new.
  • Zero-length read (EOF or O_NONBLOCK EAGAIN): mem_effects must be empty Vec; MemDelta with len=0 must not write to data.log.
  • Tracee calls execve mid-trace: address space is completely replaced; re-parse /proc/pid/maps on PTRACE_EVENT_EXEC.
  • Multi-page write partially fails (e.g., write returns short): capture only ret bytes, not the full buffer length.
  • brk(0) (query current brk): no address space change; ret != arg; do not modify AddressSpace.

5.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
pread /proc/pid/mem returns EIO MemAccess::read error; log with addr/len That event's MemDeltas are incomplete; trace corrupted Abort record; emit partial-trace marker
Missed mmap event (bug in ABI table) AddressSpace mismatch at next parse I10 violation on replay Periodic full reparse of /proc/pid/maps as cross-check
data.log disk full mid-snapshot TraceWriter::append_data returns Err(ENOSPC) Trace truncated at last good frame P7: trace recovers to last good frame; emit TRACE_TRUNCATED header flag
Wrong MemDelta len (off-by-one in arg decode) Divergence at replay (wrong bytes injected) Replay corrupted from that event Instrument with DRET_TRACE_MEM env var for byte-level logging

5.10 Operational Notes

  • DRET_TRACE_MEM=1 env var enables per-MemDelta hex dump to stderr; for debugging memory-effect mismatches.
  • Metric: dret.mem_effects_bytes_per_event histogram; spikes indicate large syscall payloads.
  • dret dump --data-ref <id> prints raw bytes at a DataRef offset in data.log; dret dump --maps <event_id> prints AddressSpace snapshot at that event.
  • Rollback/compatibility: mmap.log format is versioned; older replayer skips unknown MmapEvent kinds (P8 via forward-compatible tags).
  • Deployment prerequisite: tracee process must be accessible via /proc/pid/mem; requires ptrace capability or same-uid + PTRACE_ATTACH already held.

Phase 6 — Single-Threaded Replay Engine

6.1 Objectives

  • Re-execute the recorded binary under dret-replay without any real syscall side effects; use PTRACE_SYSEMU to intercept every syscall before kernel execution.
  • At each syscall stop: inject the recorded ret/errno into tracee registers and write all recorded MemDelta bytes into tracee address space.
  • Validate registers and ticks at each event against the TraceFrame; emit DivergenceReport on any mismatch (I9 enforced here).
  • Replace non-deterministic mmap calls with deterministic remaps that reproduce the recorded AddressSpace layout.
  • Constraint: single-threaded trace only (Phase 7/8 extend to multi-thread); I6, I3, I4, I5 fully enforced.

6.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

[I3: events consumed in exact EventId order. I4: injected ret + MemDelta exact match. I5: no real syscall executes. I6: one runnable task. P4: single logical CPU via sched_setaffinity. ADR-12: PTRACE_SYSEMU suppresses real syscall. Ref: O'Callahan et al. 2017 (replay injection); ptrace(2) PTRACE_SYSEMU semantics.]

dret-replay crate — ReplaySession [P4: sched_setaffinity enforced at session start; I5: PTRACE_SYSEMU is the exclusive replay mechanism]:

pub struct ReplaySession { tracee: Pid, reader: TraceReader, mem: MemAccess, current_event: EventId, addr_space: AddressSpace }
impl ReplaySession { fn new(trace_dir: &Path, binary: &Path) -> Result<Self>; fn run_to_end(&mut self) -> Result<ReplayOutcome>; fn step_event(&mut self) -> Result<StepResult>; }

Replay launch sequence: 1. Fork tracee with PTRACE_TRACEME; exec binary with -- separator for args. 2. Wait for initial SIGTRAP (exec stop); apply PTRACE_SETOPTIONS: PTRACE_O_TRACESYSGOOD | PTRACE_O_EXITKILL. 3. Apply sched_setaffinity to pin tracee to CPU 0 (single CPU for I6). 4. Open TraceReader; seek to EventId(0). 5. Enter ReplayLoop.

PTRACE_SYSEMU — supervisor loop: send PTRACE_SYSEMU instead of PTRACE_SYSCALL so the tracee stops at syscall entry without executing it; on stop, waitpid classifies the stop kind (SYSCALL_STOP = (status >> 8) == (SIGTRAP | 0x80)).

  • Read registers via PTRACE_GETREGSET(NT_PRSTATUS); extract orig_rax (syscall nr) and args from rdi/rsi/rdx/r10/r8/r9. Fetch the next TraceFrame from TraceReader; assert frame.kind == EventKind::Syscall and frame.tid == current_task (I3).

Syscall result injection: set rax = frame.syscall_record.ret (or errno-encoded negative for error) via PTRACE_SETREGSET; if frame.syscall_record.errno != 0, set rax = -(errno as i64) per Linux ABI with no mem effects written.

  • For each MemDelta in frame.syscall_record.mem_effects, call MemAccess::write(delta.range.addr, &bytes); advance current_event by 1, enforcing I4.

Register validation (dret-divergence integration): after injection, before PTRACE_SYSEMU resume, call divergence::check_regs(frame, &live_regs), which computes equality on all RecordedRegs fields; on mismatch it constructs DivergenceReport { event_id, kind: RegMismatch, expected: frame.regs, actual: live_regs } and invokes DivergenceHandler (abort by default, configurable).

Tick validation: read PerfCounter::read() for the tracee at each stop and compare to frame.ticks; exact match required (overcount correction applied same as in record); on mismatch emit DivergenceReport { kind: TickMismatch }.

Deterministic mmap replacement: on mmap/mmap2 syscall stop, instead of injecting recorded ret directly, use MemAccess::write/PTRACE_SETSIGINFO to arrange the exact recorded address. File-backed segments open the same file path from the trace's embedded file-table and mmap at MAP_FIXED | recorded_addr; anonymous segments mmap anonymous at the same fixed address, then write any dirty MemDelta bytes. ReplaySession.AddressSpace is kept a mirror of the recorded AddressSpace.

Signal delivery on replay (stub for Phase 9): Phase 6 defers async signals; if a SignalRecord frame appears in a single-threaded trace, inject via PTRACE_SETSIGINFO + PTRACE_CONT (not PTRACE_SYSEMU).

TraceReader integration: TraceReader::next_frame() → Option<TraceFrame>, advancing the internal cursor by one EventId; on EventKind::Exit, terminate the replay loop and verify the final EventId matches trace length.

6.3 Deliverable

  • dret replay <trace_dir> -- <binary> re-executes a single-threaded program deterministically.
  • A 1000-event trace of sha256sum /dev/urandom replays without divergence from the recorder; dret dump --divergence shows zero divergences on a correctly recorded trace.
  • DRET_REPLAY_VERBOSE=1 prints per-event: EventId, syscall nr, injected ret, tick comparison result.
  • Any manually corrupted ret field in events.log produces a DivergenceReport at that exact EventId.

6.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Pass Condition
Full replay equivalence 50-program corpus: 0 divergences; verified by dret-divergence hash check
I4 enforcement Injected rax == frame.ret for 100% of syscall events
I5 enforcement No real syscall escapes to kernel: strace -p <replay_pid> shows only PTRACE-related calls in supervisor
I9 enforcement RecordedRegs at event E matches live regs at event E; test with injected single-bit flip
Tick match Tick at event E matches recorded Tick within ±2 RCB (skid tolerance) for >99.9% of events
Exit match Replay exits with same exit code as recorded trace

6.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
tests/fixtures/replay_singlethread_basic.trace 100-event trace of true(1) 0 divergences; exit 0
tests/fixtures/replay_ret_corrupted.trace event 42: ret value flipped by 1 DivergenceReport at EventId(42), kind=RegMismatch
tests/fixtures/replay_mem_corrupted.trace event 17: 1 MemDelta byte flipped MemHashMismatch at EventId(17)
tests/fixtures/replay_mmap_fixed.trace Program uses mmap(MAP_FIXED); specific addr Replay reproduces same addr; AddressSpace matches
tests/fixtures/replay_exit_code.trace Program exits with code 42 Replay exit code == 42
tests/fixtures/replay_large_read.trace read(2) returns 65536 bytes All 65536 bytes injected; no truncation

6.6 Pitfalls

  • PTRACE_SYSEMU vs PTRACE_SYSCALL confusion: PTRACE_SYSCALL executes the real syscall then stops at exit; PTRACE_SYSEMU stops before execution. Mixing them causes real syscalls to escape. Symptom: network packets sent, files created. Fix: assert PTRACE_SYSEMU is used exclusively in replay; gated by a compile-time mode enum.
  • rax sign extension for errno: Linux ABI returns -errno as a negative i64 in rax; if stored/retrieved as u64, sign extension breaks. Store ret as i64 in SyscallRecord; cast carefully.
  • mmap MAP_FIXED collision: recorded mmap address may already be mapped in the replayed process (e.g., vDSO placed by kernel at replay exec). Fix: at replay launch, after exec stop, parse /proc/pid/maps, unmap any segment that conflicts with a to-be-replayed MAP_FIXED region.
  • Replay binary exec ASLR: kernel ASLR places stack, vDSO, loader at random addresses even if binary is non-PIE. The replay exec must set ADDR_NO_RANDOMIZE (via personality(ADDR_NO_RANDOMIZE) in child before exec) to match recorded layout. Omitting this causes immediate address-space divergence.
  • orig_rax vs rax: after a syscall stop, rax is overwritten by the kernel with the return value; orig_rax holds the syscall nr. On replay with PTRACE_SYSEMU, the kernel never executes the syscall, so rax == orig_rax at entry. Validation must read orig_rax for syscall nr, not rax.
  • Signal during replay syscall stop: if an asynchronous signal arrives while the supervisor is processing a PTRACE_SYSEMU stop, waitpid delivers it separately; replay loop must handle WIFSIGNALED and WSTOPSIG non-syscall stops without consuming a TraceFrame.

6.7 Performance Concerns

  • MemAccess::write per MemDelta: if a syscall has 64 MemDeltas (large readv), this is 64 pwrite64 calls; coalesce adjacent ranges into a single write.
  • PTRACE_GETREGSET/SETREGSET: two calls per event; at 200k events/s = 400k ptrace syscalls/s from supervisor. This is the primary overhead of replay. Batch future work (Phase 14).
  • TraceReader::next_frame: disk read (likely cached after first pass); use mmap-backed reader with MADV_SEQUENTIAL for events.log.
  • divergence::check_regs: 16-field struct comparison; SIMD not needed; branch-free equality check with early exit on first mismatch.
  • PerfCounter::read at every stop: see Phase 4 notes; ~300 ns per stop; at 200k stops/s adds 60 ms/s overhead.

6.8 Edge Cases

  • Replay of a program that calls clone: single-threaded replay engine must abort or skip; detect clone/clone3 syscall nr in TraceFrame and return UnsupportedMultiThread error (Phase 8 handles it).
  • Syscall restart (ERESTARTSYS): some syscalls are restarted by the kernel on signal delivery; recorded trace will show two consecutive Syscall events for the same nr; replay must inject both, in order.
  • Empty trace (0 events): run_to_end returns ReplayOutcome::EmptyTrace; no divergence check.
  • Trace version mismatch: TraceReader checks header version; unknown future version returns IncompatibleTrace.
  • Replay tracee segfaults: if replay diverges before a divergence check fires, tracee may SIGSEGV; supervisor must catch WIFSIGNALED(SIGSEGV) and emit a DivergenceReport with kind UnexpectedSignal.
  • Partial MemDelta write (pwrite64 returns short): must loop until all bytes written; EINTR requires retry.

6.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Replay exits early (tracee crash) WIFSIGNALED before trace Exit event Replay incomplete DivergenceReport(UnexpectedSignal) + coredump path logged
Injected mmap at wrong address AddressSpace divergence at next map-dependent event All subsequent addresses wrong Abort at first I10 violation; recheck ADDR_NO_RANDOMIZE
MemDelta write fails (EFAULT) MemAccess::write returns Err; log addr That event's replay state corrupted Fail closed; emit ReplayError::MemWriteFailed; abort
TraceReader CRC mismatch TraceReader::next_frame returns CrcError Trace corrupt from that frame Abort replay; operator inspects dret dump --verify
Divergence not detected (bug) End-to-end hash of tracee stdout differs from record Silent wrong replay Mandatory stdout hash comparison in conformance fixtures

6.10 Operational Notes

  • DRET_REPLAY_VERBOSE=1: per-event log to stderr: [replay] E={event_id} nr={syscall_nr} ret={ret} ticks_rec={} ticks_live={}.
  • DRET_REPLAY_ABORT_ON_DIVERGE=0: continue replay past first divergence (collect all divergences); default is abort-on-first (P6).
  • Metric: dret.replay_events_per_sec, dret.divergences_total (should be 0 on clean replay); dret dump --verify <trace_dir> offline-checks CRC of all frames without replay.
  • Operator runbook on divergence: (1) capture DivergenceReport event_id; (2) run dret dump --event <id> to compare expected vs actual regs; (3) check if same binary/kernel version; (4) check ADDR_NO_RANDOMIZE and CPU microarchitecture.
  • Compatibility: replay binary must match recorded binary (build-id check in trace header; Phase 14 enforces this; Phase 6 logs a warning).

Phase 7 — Deterministic User-Space Scheduler

7.1 Objectives

  • Force all tracee threads onto a single logical CPU via sched_setaffinity(2); ensure no two threads run simultaneously at any point during record or replay.
  • Implement tick-bounded cooperative preemption: supervisor reads RCB tick count at each ptrace stop, compares to per-task budget, and decides the next thread to run.
  • Produce a deterministic, fully-ordered run-queue sequence captured as SchedSwitch frames in events.log; replay re-applies identical switch points by consuming those frames.
  • Define SchedPoint { tid: TaskId, ticks: Ticks } as the canonical preemption boundary; encode in EventKind::SchedSwitch.
  • Constraints: scheduler state lives entirely in the supervisor, no in-process synchronization primitives are used, and the tracee is never aware of preemption. Scope: dret-sched owns run-queue logic; dret-record calls into it at every ptrace stop; dret-replay drives replay-side switch injection.

7.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

[P4: single logical CPU eliminates SMP nondeterminism. I6: exactly one task runnable. P5: tick-budget preemption driven by RCB counter, not wall clock. ADR-16: single supervisor thread. Ref: Dunlap et al. 2008 (logical-clock SMP serialization); Leblanc & Mellor-Crummey 1987 (formal nondeterminism sources); O'Callahan 2017 (scheduler design).]

dret-sched crate — core types [I6 enforcement; Dunlap 2008 logical-clock serialization applied to SchedSwitch design]:

Symbol Kind Shape / Contract
RunQueue struct VecDeque<TaskId>; front = next scheduled; O(1) push/pop
SchedState struct { task_id, tick_budget: Ticks, ticks_used: Ticks, blocked: Option<BlockReason> }
BlockReason enum { Futex(u64 /*uaddr*/), SyscallBlocking, ExitPending, Stopped }
SchedDecision enum { Continue { remaining: Ticks }, Preempt { next_tid: TaskId } }
SchedPolicy trait fn next_to_run(&self, queue: &RunQueue, states: &[SchedState]) -> TaskId
RoundRobin struct default SchedPolicy impl; rotates on each preemption
tick_budget_for fn (config: &SchedConfig, task: TaskId) -> Ticks; default 1000 RCB

sched_setaffinity enforcement: called by the supervisor for the tracee immediately after the fork/clone stop with cpuset = {cpu_0}, re-applied in the PTRACE_EVENT_CLONE handler for each new thread before its first PTRACE_CONT, verified post-call via /proc/{tid}/status field Cpus_allowed_list == "0". Supervisor loop (dret-record): each iteration, waitpid_any() yields (tid, stop_reason); ticks = dret_time::read_ticks(task_fd[tid]); the stop is classified and dispatched, then sched.decide(ticks, budget[tid]) yields a decision, dispatched per:

Stop kind / Decision Action
SyscallStop record_syscall(); update ticks_used[tid]
SignalStop record_signal() (Phase 9)
CloneStop register_thread(); enqueue RunQueue tail
ExitStop dequeue; mark Exited
SchedPreempt emit SchedSwitch frame; rotate RunQueue
Continue(rem) PTRACE_CONT(tid, 0)
Preempt(next) PTRACE_CONT(tid, SIGSTOP); await SIGSTOP stop; PTRACE_CONT(next, 0)
  • SchedSwitch TraceFrame layout: populates the base TraceFrame (§11 Core Schema & Types) with event_id=next EventId, tid=outgoing TaskId, ticks=RCB count at preemption, kind=EventKind::SchedSwitch, regs=outgoing thread's RecordedRegs snapshot, data_ref→data.log payload { next_tid: TaskId, tick_budget_used: Ticks }.
  • Run-queue ordering: new/preempted threads appended to RunQueue tail (round-robin); a futex-blocked thread is removed with its position not preserved; on unblock (FUTEX_WAKE, Phase 8) it is re-inserted at tail in deterministic TaskId-ascending order among unblocked threads.
  • Replay-side scheduler (dret-replay): reads SchedSwitch frames from TraceReader in EventId order; at each SchedSwitch, PTRACE_CONTs the current thread into SIGSTOP, validates outgoing regs vs recorded, then PTRACE_CONTs next_tid; tick budget: target_ticks = next_SchedSwitch.ticks, single-step/PTRACE_SYSCALL until ticks match; I6 asserts runnable_tasks.len() == 1 at each replay step.
  • dret-trace schema: EventKind::SchedSwitch is serialized as a variant tag + next_tid (fixed-size) + data_ref (variable); forward-compatible per P8 — older readers skip the unknown variant frame.

7.3 Deliverable

  • dret record ./mt_pingpong produces events.log with interleaved SchedSwitch + Syscall frames from two threads.
  • dret replay ./mt_pingpong exits with identical return code; 0 DivergenceReports; dret dump --switches prints event_id | from_tid | to_tid | ticks_used.
  • /proc/{tid}/status confirms Cpus_allowed_list: 0 during record for all threads; SchedPolicy::RoundRobin exercised, with tick budget log showing no thread exceeded budget + skid.

7.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Threshold
CPU affinity all threads: Cpus_allowed_list = 0 throughout record
SchedSwitch count record vs replay: exact match (diff = 0)
Determinism two independent records of same binary → identical switch event sequence
Replay divergence 0 DivergenceReport on 2-thread mutex-ping-pong, 1 M iterations
Tick budget no thread runs more than budget + 200 RCB skid between stops
Record overhead ≤ 20x wall-clock vs native on pthread_mutex microbenchmark
Preempt latency ptrace SIGSTOP → next PTRACE_CONT ≤ 100 µs p99

7.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture file Purpose Expected output
fixtures/sched/ping_pong.c 2-thread mutex alternation, 100k iterations Alternating SchedSwitch; 0 divergence
fixtures/sched/busy_3threads.c 3 CPU-bound threads, no blocking All 3 threads scheduled; deterministic rotation
fixtures/sched/spawn_exit_flood.c 50 threads each running 1 µs then exiting No RunQueue corruption; TaskIds monotonic; no double-dequeue
fixtures/sched/futex_stall.c thread blocks on futex forever RunQueue drains to 0 active; SchedulerStall error emitted
fixtures/sched/vfork_child.c vfork + execve in child Parent parked until child exec; switch sequence correct
fixtures/sched/bad_switch_frame next_tid field = 0xFFFFFFFF (invalid) TraceError::InvalidTaskId on reader
fixtures/sched/tick_overflow synthesized frame with ticks = u64::MAX - 1 Monotonicity check fires; TraceError::TickOverflow

7.6 Pitfalls

  • Affinity inheritance gap: threads born via clone before supervisor installs affinity inherit parent's CPU mask; fix: apply sched_setaffinity in PTRACE_EVENT_CLONE handler before first PTRACE_CONT of child.
  • SIGSTOP delivery latency: issuing PTRACE_CONT + SIGSTOP is not atomic; tracee executes additional instructions before the stop arrives; correct by re-reading ticks at the resulting stop, not at the signal-send time; log delta as preempt_overshoot.
  • Spurious external SIGSTOP: a debugger or external kill -STOP may deliver SIGSTOP to tracee; differentiate by tagging each in-flight supervisor SIGSTOP with a bool pending_sched_stop flag per TaskId; unexpected stops are logged and treated as asynchronous events.
  • Futex-blocked thread blocking the scheduler: a thread sleeping in futex(FUTEX_WAIT) never reaches a ptrace stop; it must be removed from the active RunQueue and tracked in SchedState.blocked = Some(BlockReason::Futex(uaddr)); failure to do so causes the scheduler to attempt PTRACE_CONT on a stopped thread → ESRCH.
  • Empty active queue deadlock: if all tasks are Futex-blocked and no FUTEX_WAKE is pending, the supervisor's waitpid blocks forever; detect by checking RunQueue.is_empty() && all_states == Blocked; emit SchedulerStall error and flush trace to last good checkpoint.
  • Tick read overhead in hot path: read(perf_fd) per ptrace stop adds ~2–5 µs; batch with PTRACE_GETREGSET in the same trap window to amortize kernel transitions.

7.7 Performance Concerns

  • ptrace round-trip per switch: each context switch = PTRACE_CONT(SIGSTOP) + waitpid + PTRACE_CONT(next) ≈ 40–80 µs; tick budget of 1000 RCB ≈ few hundred instructions → budget should be raised to reduce switch rate on CPU-bound workloads.
  • RunQueue as VecDeque: push/pop O(1); linear scan for TaskId removal (futex block) O(N threads); acceptable for ≤ 256 threads.
  • SchedState as Vec<SchedState> indexed by TaskId as usize: avoids HashMap overhead in hot scheduler loop; pre-allocate capacity 64.
  • sched_setaffinity per new thread: one extra syscall per clone; negligible vs per-stop overhead.
  • Tick budget tuning: lower budget = more determinism, more ptrace round-trips; higher budget = less overhead, coarser preemption granularity; expose --tick-budget CLI flag.
  • PTRACE_CONT vs PTRACE_SYSCALL mode: in scheduler mode, use PTRACE_SYSCALL to stop at syscall boundaries for recording; combine with tick-based preemption by checking ticks at every syscall stop.

7.8 Edge Cases

  • Single-threaded tracee: RunQueue always length ≤ 1; no SchedSwitch frames emitted; Phase 7 is transparent.
  • Thread exits while holding mutex: mutex remains locked; FUTEX_WAKE may never arrive; record exit frame; replay reproduces identical deadlock (which is the correct behavior).
  • Clone with CLONE_VFORK: child must exec/exit before parent is PTRACE_CONTed; supervisor must hold parent in BlockReason::VforkWait until PTRACE_EVENT_VFORK_DONE.
  • Signal arriving between PTRACE_CONT and SIGSTOP: signal queued by ptrace; delivered at next stop; handle in Phase 9 signal queue.
  • Replay task count mismatch: trace references TaskId not present in replay (e.g., trace truncated mid-clone); abort with ReplayError::TaskNotFound { task_id }.
  • Tick counter reset on perf_event_open fd recycle: if PERF_EVENT_IOC_RESET used after thread exit, new thread's ticks start from 0; must verify monotonicity per TaskId, not globally.
  • sched_setaffinity fails with EPERM: supervisor lacks CAP_SYS_NICE; fallback: use sched_setattr with affinity struct if available; document requirement in CLI error.

7.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection signal Blast radius Containment / Recovery
Affinity not enforced → parallel threads Tick ordering violation in replay; I2 broken per TaskId Full trace nondeterministic Assert affinity in clone handler; add /proc check in smoke test
Supervisor deadlock (empty active queue) waitpid never returns; SchedulerStall timeout fires Record hangs; trace may be incomplete Flush trace to last valid frame; emit error; allow replay of prefix
SchedSwitch frame CRC fail TraceReader returns TraceError::CrcMismatch Replay aborts at corrupt frame Seek to nearest Checkpoint; replay forward from there
RunQueue double-insert (clone retry) Assertion !queue.contains(tid) in enqueue() I6 violation; two threads active Deduplicate by TaskId on insert; log as WARN duplicate_enqueue
Tick budget 0 (misconfiguration) Infinite preemption loop; excessive ptrace stops Record extremely slow Validate budget > 100 at startup; default to 1000

7.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics exported: dret_sched_switches_total, dret_sched_active_tasks (gauge), dret_tick_budget_hits_total, dret_preempt_overshoot_ticks (histogram p50/p99).
  • Log DEBUG: each SchedSwitch: [sched] switch from={tid} to={tid} ticks_used={} budget={}; log WARN: [sched] scheduler_stall all_tasks=blocked.
  • dret dump --switches output: tabular event_id | from_tid | to_tid | ticks_used | budget; dret dump --thread <tid> filters events to a single thread's subsequence.
  • Deployment: Phase 7 is backward-compatible with Phase 6 single-thread traces (SchedSwitch frames simply absent); rollback disables multi-thread sched, reverting to the Phase 6 supervisor loop with the single-thread trace format still valid; --tick-budget N CLI option (documented in dret record --help) enforces a minimum of 100.

Phase 8 — Multi-Threaded Record + Replay

8.1 Objectives

  • Intercept clone(2)/clone3(2) via PTRACE_EVENT_CLONE; register each new thread as a distinct TaskId with its own tick counter, regs, ThreadRecord.
  • Capture per-thread TLS base (fs_base/gs_base) at thread creation and at every arch_prctl(ARCH_SET_FS) call.
  • Record futex ops (FUTEX_WAIT, FUTEX_WAKE, FUTEX_REQUEUE, FUTEX_WAKE_BITSET) with full woken_tids[] so replay reproduces identical wakeup order.
  • Atomics determinism by serialization (I6): one thread runs at a time, so CAS/fetch-add are sequentially consistent, no instrumentation needed. Constraint: state captured only via ptrace//proc; no code injection, no compiler instrumentation.

8.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

[I6: serialization makes atomics deterministic without instrumentation. I8: async signals pinned to (tid, delivery_ticks). ADR-08: synchronous supervisor, no tokio. Ref: Dunlap et al. 2008 (SMP futex ordering); Choi & Srinivasan 1998 (threading model); clone(2)/clone3(2) kernel.org.]

struct ThreadRecord { task_id: TaskId, os_tid: i32, ticks: Ticks, regs: RecordedRegs, fs_base: u64, gs_base: u64, stack_range: MemRange, state: ThreadState, perf_fd: RawFd }
enum ThreadState { Running, Blocked(BlockReason), Exited(i32) }
struct FutexRecord { op: FutexOp, uaddr: u64, val: u32, timeout_abs: Option<u64>, woken_tids: Vec<TaskId>, ret: i64, errno: i32 }
enum FutexOp { Wait, Wake, Requeue, WakeBitset(u32), LockPi, UnlockPi }
  • Clone interception (dret-record + dret-ptrace): 1. PTRACE_SETOPTIONS includes TRACECLONE|TRACEFORK|TRACEVFORK|TRACEVFORKDONE. 2. PTRACE_EVENT_CLONE stop reads child os_tid via PTRACE_GETEVENTMSG. 3. Allocate TaskId from monotonic u32 TaskIdAllocator (never reused). 4. PTRACE_GETREGSET(child, NT_PRSTATUS) → initial RecordedRegs. 5. Read fs_base/gs_base from regs struct (offsets 0xE8/0xF0). 6. Open perf_event_open for child tid → ThreadRecord.perf_fd. 7. sched_setaffinity(child, {cpu_0}) before first PTRACE_CONT. 8. Emit TraceFrame{kind: SchedSwitch, data_ref: ClonePayload{parent_tid, child_tid, clone_flags, stack_ptr}}. 9. Insert ThreadRecord into supervisor ThreadTable: Vec<ThreadRecord> indexed by TaskId.
  • clone_flags capture: read from rdi at syscall-entry. Recorded: CLONE_THREAD, CLONE_VM, CLONE_FILES, CLONE_SIGHAND, CLONE_SETTLS, CLONE_PARENT_SETTID, CLONE_CHILD_CLEARTID. CLONE_SETTLS TLS base arrives in r8; captured as initial fs_base before child runs.
  • Per-thread tick accounting: perf_event_open(HARDWARE, HW_BRANCH_INSTRUCTIONS, pid=child_tid, cpu=-1); ticks read at every ptrace stop via read(perf_fd). On PTRACE_EVENT_EXIT: read final ticks, close perf_fd, emit EventKind::Exit{final_ticks}.
TLS event Action
Clone with CLONE_SETTLS Capture r8 from clone-entry regs as initial fs_base
arch_prctl(ARCH_SET_FS) exit Re-read fs_base via PTRACE_GETREGSET
arch_prctl(ARCH_SET_GS) exit Re-read gs_base similarly
Every TraceFrame Include current fs_base/gs_base in RecordedRegs
Futex stop Recorded fields Replay action
FUTEX_WAIT entry uaddr, val, timeout_abs, tid Mark BlockReason::Futex(uaddr); skip real syscall
FUTEX_WAIT exit errno (0/EINTR/ETIMEDOUT), ticks Inject recorded errno; unblock at recorded tick
FUTEX_WAKE entry uaddr, n_requested Record op params
FUTEX_WAKE exit n_woken, woken_tids[] (FIFO) Wake exactly woken_tids in order; re-insert to RunQueue
FUTEX_REQUEUE exit n_woken, n_requeued, woken_tids[], requeued_tids[] Re-apply same splits
FUTEX_LOCK_PI exit result, owner_tid Inject result; record ownership
  • woken_tids[] stored as Vec<TaskId> in data.log via DataRef. Supervisor enumerates all Futex(uaddr)-blocked tasks at FUTEX_WAKE exit; picks up to n_woken in TaskId-ascending order (stable tie-break); records the chosen list.
  • Atomics determinism: hardware atomics (lock cmpxchg, xadd, xchg) execute inside the serialized single-thread window; memory barriers execute normally with no replay side effects; single-CPU serialization is the complete guarantee, no instrumentation needed.
  • dret-trace schema: clone uses EventKind::SchedSwitch with ClonePayload in data_ref (or a named EventKind::Clone{child_tid, clone_flags} variant — either choice must stay consistent).
  • Replay ThreadTable init: read all Clone/initial-task frames to pre-populate ThreadTable at replay start; never allocate ThreadRecord lazily, so missing thread frames are detected early.

8.3 Deliverable

  • dret record on a pthreads producer/consumer (4 threads, 10k iterations) emits per-thread frames in events.log.
  • dret replay reproduces final output identically; per-thread tick counts match at every event.
  • dret dump --thread <tid> filters to single thread; dret dump --futex shows FUTEX_WAIT/WAKE pairs with woken_tids.
  • fs_base values in replay match recorded values at every thread's first event.

8.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Threshold
Clone coverage every pthread_create = one ClonePayload frame; no untracked thread
fs_base fidelity RecordedRegs.fs_base at replay == recorded for all threads; memcmp passes
Futex wake order woken_tids[] on replay == recorded list for every FUTEX_WAKE event
Multi-thread divergence 0 DivergenceReport on 4-thread mutex workload, 10k iterations
Atomic counter correctness __atomic_fetch_add final value on replay == recorded
Tick monotonicity (I2) per-TaskId ticks non-decreasing across all frames
perf_fd leak 0 open perf_fds after all threads exit (/proc/self/fd count)

8.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
fixtures/mt/producer_consumer.c 2-thread queue via pthread_mutex+pthread_cond Ordered wake sequence; 0 divergence
fixtures/mt/tls_heavy.c 8 threads, distinct TLS var via __thread fs_base captured per thread; replay TLS reads identical
fixtures/mt/futex_pi.c FUTEX_LOCK_PI priority inheritance PI futex fields recorded; no panic; replay inherits same owner
fixtures/mt/clone3_flags.c clone3(2) with CLONE_INTO_CGROUP, CLONE_NEWUSER All clone_flags captured; TaskId allocated; unsupported flags logged
fixtures/mt/atomic_counter.c 4 threads, __atomic_fetch_add shared counter Final count identical on replay
fixtures/mt/arch_prctl_reset.c Thread calls arch_prctl(ARCH_SET_FS) mid-run Updated fs_base in subsequent frames
fixtures/mt/bad_woken_tid woken_tids contains unknown TaskId ReplayError::FutexWakeMismatch{unknown_tid}
fixtures/mt/double_clone Same os_tid recycled (exit + new clone) New TaskId allocated; old ThreadRecord marked Exited before reuse

8.6 Pitfalls

  • perf_event_open fd leak on short-lived threads: each thread opens one fd; mass thread spawn exhausts fd limit; fix: close perf_fd in PTRACE_EVENT_EXIT handler; assert 0 open fds after all threads exit.
  • CLONE_THREAD vs fork: CLONE_THREAD shares address space/fd table; fork creates a new AddressSpace; abort loudly on CLONE_NEWNS/CLONE_NEWPID (unsupported namespaces) via RecordError::UnsupportedCloneFlags.
