  • fs_base stale after set_thread_area/inline wrmsr: glibc uses arch_prctl exclusively on x86-64; raw wrmsr from kernel modules is unreachable from user space; no workaround needed.
  • Futex uaddr in shared memory (MAP_SHARED): two tasks may hold different virtual addresses mapping the same page; record physical page offset (/proc/pid/pagemap) alongside uaddr for divergence-check verification.
  • Kernel wakeup ordering non-determinism: kernel futex-waiter wake order may differ between record and replay kernels; record woken_tids[] explicitly; replay enforces the recorded list regardless of kernel behavior.
  • TaskId exhaustion: u32 counter; 4B threads/trace before overflow, unreachable in practice; debug-assert counter != u32::MAX.
  • vfork child not yet exec'd when parent re-enters ptrace: PTRACE_EVENT_VFORK_DONE not yet received; supervisor must not PTRACE_CONT parent until it arrives; add BlockReason::VforkWait.

8.7 Performance Concerns

  • perf_event_open fd count: 1 fd/live thread; 256-thread program = 256 extra fds vs default 1024 limit; raise via setrlimit(RLIMIT_NOFILE) at startup.
  • PTRACE_GETREGSET per stop: reads 216 bytes (NT_PRSTATUS), unavoidable; skip NT_PRFPREG unless an FP syscall is detected.
  • Futex frame payload size: woken_tids[] variable; FUTEX_WAKE_OP to 1000 waiters = 4000-byte payload via DataRef in data.log; no inline limit.
  • ThreadTable lookup: Vec<ThreadRecord> indexed by task_id as usize, O(1); pre-allocate capacity 256.
  • Clone stop latency: TaskId alloc + perf_event_open + sched_setaffinity ≈ 50 µs/clone; acceptable.
  • HashMap avoidance: never HashMap<TaskId,_> in the hot supervisor loop; use Vec indexed by TaskId.

8.8 Edge Cases

  • vfork: child must exec/exit before parent resumes; parent BlockReason::VforkWait; unblock on PTRACE_EVENT_VFORK_DONE.
  • Thread exit during futex wait: kernel cancels the wait (EINTR); PTRACE_EVENT_EXIT arrives before the syscall-exit stop; emit exit frame first, then the cancelled syscall frame.
  • pthread_cancel (SIGCANCEL=SIGRTMIN): treated as async signal, recorded in Phase 9; Phase 8 handles only the clone/futex side.
  • clone with CLONE_SETTLS+CLONE_PARENT_SETTID: parent-tid write is a memory effect; record as MemDelta on the parent-tid pointer.
  • Replay under a different libc TLS layout: fs_base address differs; replay must inject the recorded fs_base (never recompute) via PTRACE_SETREGSET.
  • FUTEX_CMP_REQUEUE: extra val3 arg; dret-syscall ABI table must cover this opcode.
  • clone3 struct argument: pointer to clone_args; dret-syscall reads it from tracee memory for flags/stack/tls fields.

8.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
Untracked thread (clone stop missed) Replay hits unknown TaskId; ReplayError::TaskNotFound Replay aborts Assert PTRACE_O_TRACECLONE set; integration test counting TaskIds
fd exhaustion perf_event_open returns EMFILE Record aborts Raise RLIMIT_NOFILE at startup; document in CLI help
Futex deadlock on replay Replay blocks in FUTEX_WAIT indefinitely Replay hangs Configurable --replay-timeout; emit DivergenceReport{FutexDeadlock}
fs_base mismatch DivergenceReport{RegMismatch} on fs_base Divergence abort at that event Re-read fs_base after every arch_prctl stop
woken_tids replay mismatch ReplayError::FutexWakeMismatch Replay aborts at that futex event Inspect dret dump --futex for recorded vs. attempted list

8.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: dret_threads_recorded_total, dret_live_threads (gauge), dret_futex_wait_total, dret_futex_wake_total, dret_clone_flags_unsupported_total.
  • DEBUG log per clone: [clone] parent={tid} child={tid} os_tid={} flags=0x{:x} fs_base={:#x}; per futex wake: [futex] wake uaddr={:#x} requested={} woken=[{tid...}].
  • dret dump --futex: event_id|tid|op|uaddr|woken_tids|ticks. dret dump --threads: task_id|os_tid|state|final_ticks.
  • Compatibility: Phase 8 adds ClonePayload frames and futex payloads; single-thread traces have neither; replayer skips absent sections.
  • Migration: pre-Phase-8 traces lack woken_tids; replayer treats absent payload as empty list (safe for single-thread traces).

Phase 9 — Signal Capture + Deterministic Delivery

9.1 Objectives

  • Classify every signal as synchronous (fault-generated: SIGSEGV, SIGBUS, SIGFPE, SIGILL, SIGTRAP with fault si_code) or asynchronous (externally-sent: SIGINT, SIGTERM, SIGCHLD, SIGRTMIN+N, etc.).
  • Record the full siginfo_t (128 bytes on x86-64) for every signal in data.log via DataRef.
  • Pin async signal delivery to exact (tid, ticks) at interception, injecting on replay via single-step-to-tick then ptrace(PTRACE_SETSIGINFO) + PTRACE_CONT(signo); record sigaction disposition changes so replay can install identical handlers before the delivery tick.
  • Constraint: supervisor blocks all signals to itself via sigprocmask(SIG_BLOCK, all); tracee signals arrive only through ptrace interception.

9.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

[I8: async signal delivery_ticks must match exactly. P3: supervisor blocks all signals to itself via sigprocmask. ADR-12: PTRACE_SETSIGINFO for signal injection. Ref: Ronsse & De Bosschere 1999 (signal interception); Kerrisk Ch. 22 signal taxonomy; Linux Signal Frame arch/x86/kernel/signal.c.]

Classification (dret-record) [I8: delivery_ticks pinning — Ronsse & De Bosschere 1999 (async signal pitfall inventory)]: fn classify_signal(signo: i32, si_code: i32) -> SignalKind (enum SignalKind { Synchronous, Asynchronous }), decided by si_code:

si_code Kind
SI_KERNEL(0x80), BUS_ADRALN(1)..SEGV_ACCERR(2) Synchronous
SI_USER(0), SI_QUEUE(-1), SI_TIMER(-2), SI_ASYNCIO(-4) Asynchronous
SIGTRAP+TRAP_BRKPT Synchronous
SIGTRAP+SI_KERNEL may be Asynchronous

Synchronous signal path: waitpid returns a signal-delivery-stop (WSTOPSIG(status)==SIGSEGV etc., and (status>>8) != (SIGTRAP|0x80)); ptrace(PTRACE_GETSIGINFO, tid) captures siginfo_t, classified via si_code; emit TraceFrame { kind: Signal(Sync), tid, ticks: current_ticks, data_ref: siginfo_payload }; PTRACE_CONT(tid, signo) delivers the signal to the tracee. On replay, at the matching EventId: PTRACE_SETSIGINFO then PTRACE_CONT(signo).

Asynchronous signal path: any ptrace signal-delivery-stop on a waitpid call triggers ptrace(PTRACE_GETSIGINFO) to capture siginfo_t (classified Asynchronous) and delivery_ticks = dret_time::read_ticks(task_fd[tid]) (recipient task's ticks at interception); emit TraceFrame { kind: Signal(Async), tid, ticks: delivery_ticks, data_ref: siginfo_payload }, enqueued in PendingSignals: HashMap<TaskId, VecDeque<SignalRecord>> if tid is not currently active. On the task's next PTRACE_CONT: if delivery_ticks doesn't match current ticks, suppress and re-enqueue; if it matches, inject via PTRACE_SETSIGINFO + PTRACE_CONT(signo).

Replay async delivery — step-to-boundary: while current ticks are below the next Signal(Async) frame's target tick, single-step (PTRACE_SINGLESTEP) and re-read ticks (waitpid + read_ticks(perf_fd)); if ticks overshoot the target by more than SKID_THRESHOLD (default 50 RCB, --skid-threshold), emit DivergenceReport{kind: TickMismatch, expected: target_tick, actual: current_ticks}; once at the boundary, inject via ptrace(PTRACE_SETSIGINFO, tid, recorded_siginfo) then ptrace(PTRACE_CONT, tid, signo).

  • siginfo_t serialization: the raw 128-byte siginfo_t is stored in data.log as a DataRef payload; on replay it is reconstructed from raw bytes and passed directly to PTRACE_SETSIGINFO — no portable parsing, the kernel x86-64 siginfo_t layout is used verbatim.
  • Signal disposition recording (dret-syscall): rt_sigaction(2) is intercepted at syscall-exit and (signo, sa_handler, sa_sigaction, sa_mask, sa_flags) recorded as a SigactionRecord frame; on replay, rt_sigaction is replayed as a real syscall with identical arguments before the delivery tick.
  • Signal masking/unmasking: rt_sigprocmask(2) is intercepted at syscall-exit, recording (how, newset, oldset); the effective mask is tracked per thread in ThreadRecord.sigmask: u64; masked signals are queued by the kernel and not delivered until unblocked, and ptrace intercepts at delivery (after unblock).
  • rt_sigreturn handling: after a signal handler executes, the tracee calls rt_sigreturn(2) to restore pre-signal register state from the sigframe on the stack; at that syscall-exit stop, regs are NOT snapshotted as a new RecordedRegs baseline (the restored regs are simply the next normal regs); rt_sigreturn is recorded as a SyscallRecord with no mem_effects, and the divergence check is skipped at this specific stop.
  • Signal queue ordering (POSIX real-time): standard signals (1–31) may be coalesced by the kernel, so each delivery is recorded separately; real-time signals (SIGRTMIN–SIGRTMAX) are POSIX-guaranteed to deliver in order with si_value — all are recorded and replayed in exact order.
  • dret-core additions: struct SignalRecord { signo: i32, kind: SignalKind, delivery_ticks: Ticks, si_info_ref: DataRef }, where si_info_ref is the 128-byte siginfo_t in data.log.
  • Supervisor sigprocmask at startup: sigprocmask(SIG_BLOCK, &full_mask, NULL) runs in the supervisor process before fork/exec of the tracee, ensuring no signal reaches the supervisor's own handlers — all child signals are intercepted via ptrace.

9.3 Deliverable

  • dret record on a program that installs SIGINT handler and receives ^C: trace contains Signal(Async) frame with delivery_ticks and full siginfo_t.
  • dret replay delivers SIGINT at the recorded tick; program handler executes identically; register state at handler entry matches.
  • Synchronous SIGSEGV in a fault-recovery program (using sigsetjmp/siglongjmp): recorded + replayed; handler reruns identically; dret dump --signals prints event_id | tid | signo | si_code | ticks | kind.

9.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Threshold
Async delivery tick fidelity `
siginfo_t byte fidelity 128-byte si_info on replay == recorded (memcmp) for all signal events
Sync signal replay SIGSEGV handler: rip, rsp, regs at handler entry match recorded
Signal count every Signal frame in trace has exactly one corresponding replay delivery
Signal mask fidelity ThreadRecord.sigmask at replay == recorded at each event boundary
Self-checking DivergenceReport emitted on any tick skid > threshold; replay aborts
rt_sigreturn regs No false RegMismatch at rt_sigreturn stops

9.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
fixtures/signal/sigint_handler.c Catches SIGINT; prints tick at handler entry delivery_ticks match ± threshold
fixtures/signal/sigsegv_recover.c SIGSEGV + sigsetjmp/siglongjmp recovery Sync signal recorded; handler reruns identically; 0 divergence
fixtures/signal/sigrt_queue.c SIGRTMIN+1 sent 10 times with distinct si_value All 10 si_value payloads preserved in recorded order
fixtures/signal/sigchld_wait.c SIGCHLD from child exit(0) si_pid, si_status in siginfo match on replay
fixtures/signal/nested_signal.c Signal arrives inside a signal handler (re-entrant) Both delivery ticks recorded; nested delivery replayed
fixtures/signal/masked_signal.c Signal blocked via sigprocmask; later unblocked Delivery deferred; delivery tick at unblock recorded
fixtures/signal/sigaltstack.c Handler on alternate stack Altstack base/size recorded via sigaltstack(2) interception
fixtures/signal/sigkill.c SIGKILL sent to tracee EventKind::Exit { signo: 9 } emitted; replay terminates at same event
fixtures/signal/bad_siginfo Truncated si_info_ref in data.log TraceError::TruncatedPayload from TraceReader

9.6 Pitfalls

  • Signal-delivery-stop vs syscall-stop confusion: ptrace overloads WSTOPSIG; syscall-stop has (status >> 8) == (SIGTRAP | 0x80) when PTRACE_O_TRACESYSGOOD set; a signal-delivery-stop has WSTOPSIG == signo without the 0x80 modifier; misclassification causes a signal to be treated as a syscall or vice versa.
  • Async signal arriving during PTRACE_CONT gap: the signal is queued by the kernel until tracee runs; ptrace intercepts at delivery-stop before the tracee executes the handler; tick read immediately after stop is the correct delivery tick; no race exists here.
  • Scheduler SIGSTOP vs user SIGSTOP: Phase 7 uses SIGSTOP for preemption; an external kill -STOP is also SIGSTOP; differentiate by the pending_sched_stop: bool flag per TaskId; unexpected SIGSTOP = external signal, log as WARN unexpected_sigstop.
  • Skid exceeding threshold on replay: single-step mode is slower than normal execution; RCB counter may overshoot target tick by more than 50 RCB; common causes: branch-heavy signal-delivery paths; tune SKID_THRESHOLD to 100–200 if needed; expose --skid-threshold CLI flag.
  • rt_sigreturn register misread: after signal handler, rt_sigreturn restores the pre-signal RecordedRegs from the signal frame; if supervisor snapshots regs at this stop, it will record the restored values as if they were a new event baseline; must mark this stop as NoRegSnapshot; resume normal snapshotting at subsequent stops.
  • Real-time signal ordering: POSIX guarantees SIGRTMIN+N delivered in send order with si_value; if replay delivers them out of order (e.g., due to queue reordering on a different kernel), divergence will occur; enforce delivery from the recorded PendingSignals queue in exact recorded order.
  • Signals sent to the process group (negative pid in kill): kernel delivers to one thread arbitrarily; record the receiving tid explicitly; replay must deliver to the same tid.

9.7 Performance Concerns

  • Single-step loop to delivery tick: O(instructions) per signal delivery; at 1000-tick budget this is ≤ a few thousand instructions; each single-step = one ptrace round-trip ≈ 10–30 µs; for 50-tick delivery latency = ≤ 50 steps = ~1 ms; acceptable.
  • siginfo_t storage: 128 bytes per signal in data.log; for 10k signals = 1.25 MB; negligible.
  • PTRACE_SETSIGINFO + PTRACE_CONT: one extra syscall pair per signal delivery on replay; negligible vs overall ptrace overhead.
  • Signal-dense workloads: SIGPROF from setitimer (used by profilers) arrives at 100 Hz or faster; 10k SIGPROF events in a 100-second recording; each adds one Signal(Async) frame; manageable; provide --suppress-signal SIGPROF to suppress profiling signals.
  • PendingSignals queue: VecDeque per task; allocation only on signal arrival; typical size ≤ 5 entries; no hot-path allocation concern.
  • rt_sigaction interception: every sigaction call adds a SigactionRecord frame; high-frequency sigaction (unusual) could inflate trace; no mitigation needed for standard programs.

9.8 Edge Cases

  • Signal delivered while thread is inside a blocking syscall (EINTR): orig_rax holds the syscall number; kernel returns EINTR; ptrace fires syscall-exit stop with rax = -EINTR; emit SyscallRecord { ret: -EINTR } then SignalRecord; replay reproduces identical EINTR.
  • SA_RESTART semantics: after EINTR, kernel transparently restarts certain syscalls; record the restart as a second syscall-entry stop with same nr; replay sees the same two entry stops.
  • sigaltstack: handler executes on alternate stack; intercept sigaltstack(2) at syscall-exit; record (ss_sp, ss_size, ss_flags); on replay, inject identical sigaltstack call before delivery tick.
  • SA_NODEFER: handler is re-entrant for its own signal; nested delivery possible; replay must handle the nested signal frame.
  • SIGKILL: uncatchable; tracee terminates immediately; supervisor sees PTRACE_EVENT_EXIT with signo = 9; emit EventKind::Exit { signal: 9 }; replay terminates at same event; no further frames.
  • Signal arrives at EventId = 0 (before first recorded event): record at ticks = 0; replay delivers at tick 0 = before any instruction executes.
  • Thread receives signal during rt_sigreturn: extremely rare (another async signal arriving while restoring from first); record both; replay in order.
  • SIGSTOP cannot be caught or blocked: unlike SIGKILL, SIGSTOP stops the process; supervisor intercepts via ptrace before delivery; must distinguish from scheduler SIGSTOP by pending_sched_stop flag.

9.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery / Postmortem
Signal not recorded (missed delivery stop) Replay handler fires at unreferenced ticks; DivergenceReport: RegMismatch at handler entry Replay diverges silently Add counter: signals_intercepted == signal_frames_emitted; assert equality at trace close
Tick skid > threshold DivergenceReport { TickMismatch } Replay aborts Increase SKID_THRESHOLD; investigate RCB counter behavior near signal delivery; check PMU skid docs for CPU model
siginfo_t ABI mismatch (kernel upgrade) si_code or si_addr fields incorrect on replay Wrong handler behavior Version-gate si_info layout; store raw bytes; add kernel version to trace header (Phase 14)
rt_sigreturn false RegMismatch DivergenceReport at rt_sigreturn syscall stop Replay aborts spuriously Skip reg snapshot at rt_sigreturn; add a SkipRegCheck flag for this stop kind
PTRACE_SETSIGINFO fails (EINVAL) Syscall returns error; supervisor log shows EINVAL Signal not delivered on replay Validate siginfo_t format before call; ensure si_code is valid for the signo
PendingSignals queue grows unbounded Memory growth in supervisor; heap pressure Supervisor OOM Cap queue depth at 1024 per task; emit RecordError::SignalQueueOverflow

9.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: dret_signals_recorded_total{signo,kind}, dret_signal_skid_ticks (histogram, p50/p99/p999), dret_signal_replay_deliveries_total, dret_signal_queue_depth (gauge).
  • Log DEBUG: each signal stop: [signal] tid={} signo={} si_code={} ticks={} kind={sync|async}; log WARN on skid > threshold: [signal] skid tid={} expected={} actual={} skid={}; dret dump --signals columns: event_id | tid | signo | si_code | ticks | kind | deterministic.
  • --suppress-signal <signo> CLI option suppresses recording of the named signal (useful for SIGPROF, SIGWINCH); --skid-threshold N overrides the default 50 (minimum 1); both documented in dret record --help/dret replay --help.
  • Compatibility: Signal(Async)/Signal(Sync) frames are new in Phase 9; older replayers that don't recognize the variant skip it per P8 — this may cause divergence in signal-dependent programs but never corrupts the trace. Deployment: no kernel module required; ptrace signal interception is standard since Linux 2.6; no capability beyond CAP_SYS_PTRACE needed.

Phase 10 — Nondeterministic Instruction Handling

10.1 Objectives

  • Trap rdtsc/rdtscp/cpuid instructions executed by the tracee; record returned values; inject recorded values on replay.
  • Intercept gettimeofday/clock_gettime via vsyscall page patching and vDSO mapping replacement.
  • Mask CPUID feature bits on replay to the recorded feature set, ensuring portability across CPU generations.
  • Constraints: interception must not perturb RCB ticks; seccomp-BPF path preferred over single-step for throughput.

10.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

[P3: no binary modification — seccomp-BPF trap is preferred. ADR-13: seccomp-BPF over binary rewriting. I4: recorded TSC/CPUID values injected verbatim. Ref: O'Callahan et al. 2017 (vDSO/rdtsc); Intel SDM Vol. 3B Sec. 17.17 TSC invariance; seccomp(2) man page; Payer & Gross 2013 (vsyscall patch).]

dret-ptrace / dret-record — rdtsc trap strategy [ADR-13: seccomp-BPF chosen; P3: no instruction rewriting; Intel SDM Sec. 17.17 CR4.TSD semantics]:

  • Method A (preferred): set CR4.TSD via PTRACE_SETOPTIONS → causes rdtsc/rdtscp to raise SIGSEGV in ring-3 → captured as EventKind::RdtscTrap.
  • Method B (fallback): seccomp-BPF SECCOMP_RET_TRACE on x86_64::SYS_rdtsc (not a real syscall; inject via BPF hook that patches the ret instruction); only applicable if the kernel builds support it.
  • At trap entry: read RecordedRegs via PTRACE_GETREGSET NT_PRSTATUS; synthesize deterministic return value from a per-task monotonic counter or fixed seed; write edx:eax via PTRACE_SETREGSET; advance rip past the 2-byte 0F 31 opcode.
  • Emit TraceFrame { kind: RdtscTrap, ticks: current_ticks, data_ref: DataRef(recorded_edx_eax) }.

dret-syscall — cpuid trap:

  • cpuid is unprivileged; cannot be trapped by CR4 alone. Use single-step (PTRACE_SINGLESTEP) with opcode scanning at rip OR patch the instruction in-process to ud2 and handle SIGILL.
  • On entry: record eax (leaf), ecx (subleaf), and full {eax,ebx,ecx,edx} output.
  • On replay: at matching EventKind::CpuidTrap, overwrite {eax,ebx,ecx,edx} with recorded values before PTRACE_CONT.
  • Feature-masking table in dret-syscall: per-leaf bitmask applied on replay when target host differs from record host.

dret-record — vsyscall/vDSO interception:

  • vsyscall page (0xffffffffff600000): on record, patch gettimeofday / time / getcpu entries to syscall; ret via PTRACE_POKETEXT; these then surface as regular PTRACE_SYSCALL traps recorded in SyscallRecord.
  • vDSO: locate via AT_SYSINFO_EHDR in auxiliary vector; remap with PROT_READ|PROT_WRITE; overwrite gettimeofday/clock_gettime stubs with syscall; ret; record patching event EventKind::PatchVsyscall.
  • On replay: same patch applied from checkpoint; vDSO content recorded in mmap.log as MemDelta.

dret-syscall — ABI table additions:

  • Entries: rdtsc, rdtscp, cpuid (pseudo-nr entries distinct from Linux syscall numbers); gettimeofday, clock_gettime (real syscall nrs).
  • CpuidLeafMask table: struct CpuidLeafMask { leaf: u32, subleaf: Option<u32>, mask_eax: u32, mask_ebx: u32, mask_ecx: u32, mask_edx: u32 }.

dret-replay — injection:

  • On RdtscTrap: set edx:eax from DataRef; advance rip by 2.
  • On CpuidTrap: set {eax,ebx,ecx,edx} from DataRef; advance rip by 2.
  • PTRACE_CONT after injection; do not allow real rdtsc/cpuid to execute.

10.3 Deliverable

  • Tracee calling rdtsc, rdtscp, cpuid, gettimeofday, clock_gettime all produce deterministic values on replay matching recorded output.
  • dret dump shows RdtscTrap and CpuidTrap events with leaf/value annotations.
  • CpuidLeafMask applied on replay when --mask-cpu flag set.

10.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Pass condition
test_rdtsc_replay_exact replay edx:eax == recorded for all events
test_cpuid_leaf_0_replay replay {eax..edx} == recorded for leaf 0, 1, 7
test_vdso_clock_gettime vDSO-patched call surfaces as SyscallRecord; replay matches
test_cpuid_feature_mask masked bits absent on replay host
test_rdtscp_ecx_replay ecx (IA32_TSC_AUX) injected correctly
RCB tick impact rdtsc trap adds < 2 ticks overhead per event

10.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
fixtures/rdtsc_loop.bin tight loop calling rdtsc 1000x 1000 RdtscTrap events; replay values deterministic
fixtures/cpuid_all_leaves.bin calls all standard CPUID leaves per-leaf recorded values injected on replay
fixtures/vdso_clock.bin clock_gettime(CLOCK_REALTIME) via vDSO PatchVsyscall + SyscallRecord entries
fixtures/rdtsc_malformed_rip.trace corrupted rip after injection divergence detected at next event
fixtures/cpuid_cross_host.trace trace recorded on AVX-512 host replay on non-AVX-512 host with mask passes

10.6 Pitfalls

  • rdtscp vs rdtsc: rdtscp is 3 bytes (0F 01 F9); rdtsc is 2 bytes (0F 31). Advancing rip by wrong amount causes decode cascade. Fix: detect opcode bytes at trap site.
  • vDSO remapped by kernel on exec: re-patch vDSO after every execve event; record new PatchVsyscall. Failure manifests as untrapped clock_gettime returning wall time.
  • cpuid serializing behavior: some code uses cpuid as a memory fence. Recording it correctly is mandatory; skipping it breaks memory ordering assumptions and causes divergence at next mfence-dependent read.
  • seccomp-BPF interaction: if tracee installs its own seccomp filter, the BPF chain must not shadow supervisor hooks. Enforce SECCOMP_FILTER_FLAG_NEW_LISTENER or detect conflict and abort.
  • TSC invariance assumption: tracee may assume TSC increments at fixed rate and use rdtsc difference for busywait. Injecting constant values may break the wait; inject linearly increasing values at a plausible rate.
  • AT_SYSINFO_EHDR absent: some kernels omit it in auxv. Fall back to scanning /proc/pid/maps for [vdso] entry.

10.7 Performance Concerns

  • rdtsc in hot loops (spin-waits, PRNG seeds): trapping every call via CR4.TSD adds one ptrace stop per rdtsc. Batch adjacent stops where tick boundary is shared.
  • cpuid opcode scan at every single-step stop is expensive; cache the opcode at last known rip and invalidate on rip change.
  • vDSO patch is a one-time cost; verify patching does not trigger copy-on-write on shared vDSO pages causing unexpected page faults.

10.8 Edge Cases

  • rdtsc inside signal handler: rip points into signal frame; advance correctly, restore frame after injection.
  • cpuid leaf 0x80000000 and extended leaves: mask table must cover all extended leaves; unknown leaves → pass-through with warning.
  • Tracee calls arch_prctl(ARCH_SET_CPUID, 0) to disable CPUID itself: record this call; on replay reapply.
  • vDSO page at different address on replay kernel: PatchVsyscall must re-derive address from AT_SYSINFO_EHDR, not hardcode.
  • rdtscp with ecx TID check: programs use ecx to detect CPU migration; inject the recorded ecx value.

10.9 Failure Analysis

  • Untrapped rdtsc: replay diverges at first tick-dependent branch; DivergenceReport points to RdtscTrap gap. Detection: scan trace for gaps in EventId sequence in RdtscTrap category.
  • Wrong rip advance: SIGILL or decode fault in tracee on next instruction; supervisor sees unexpected signal, aborts with DivergenceReport { kind: RegMismatch }.
  • CPUID mask misapplied: feature-gated code path diverges silently; bisect via dret divergence --bisect to first CpuidTrap mismatch.
  • Recovery: all failures are fatal; no silent continuation (P6).

10.10 Operational Notes

  • dret dump --filter RdtscTrap,CpuidTrap shows all nondeterministic instruction events with values.
  • Metric: rdtsc_trap_count, cpuid_trap_count, vdso_patch_count per session.
  • Log WARN on any cpuid leaf not in the mask table.
  • On replay host CPU mismatch, emit structured log { recorded_cpu_model, replay_cpu_model, masked_leaves: [..] }.

Phase 11 — Checkpointing + Fast Seek

11.1 Objectives

  • Emit periodic Checkpoint snapshots: per-task register sets, dirty-page contents, fd table, brk value, mmap layout.
  • Implement tick-targeted seek: restore from nearest preceding checkpoint; replay-forward to requested (TaskId, Ticks).
  • Build reverse-continue and reverse-single-step on top of checkpoint + replay-forward.
  • Constraints: checkpoint I/O must not perturb tracee observable state; checkpoint interval configurable (default: every 50k events or every 10s of wall time, whichever first).

11.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

[I7: checkpoint on exact event boundary. P2: trace is immutable; checkpoint restores read from stored data only. P7: checkpoint fsync ensures replay of prefix on crash. Ref: Xu et al. 2003 (Flashback) (checkpoint-rewind tradeoff); King & Chen 2002 (COW snapshot); Laughlin 2019 (indexed checkpoint density).]

dret-mem — dirty-page capture [I7: dirty-page scan via /proc/pid/pagemap soft-dirty bits — Narayanasamy 2005 page-diff strategy; Xu 2003 checkpoint interval formula]:

  • Use /proc/pid/pagemap + /proc/pid/mem to enumerate and read dirty pages.
  • Strategy: before checkpoint, madvise(MADV_SOFT_DIRTY) to clear soft-dirty bits; after interval, scan pagemap for PM_SOFTDIRTY pages; read only those via /proc/pid/mem.
  • PageRef — index into checkpoints/ckpt-{event_id}/pages.bin.
  • Checkpoint.dirty_pages: Vec<(MemRange, PageRef)> — page-granular diffs since last checkpoint.

dret-trace — checkpoint container:

  • Directory layout: ckpt/{event_id:016x}/regs.bin, pages.bin, fds.bin, mmaps.bin.
  • regs.bin: [TaskId(u32), RecordedRegs(N bytes)] pairs, length-prefixed.
  • fds.bin: [fd: i32, flags: u32, path: [u8; 256]] array for O_CLOEXEC-safe reconstruction.
  • mmaps.bin: serialized AddressSpace (segment list with perms, file backing, offset).
  • All files CRC-32 tagged; versioned header ckpt_version: u16.

dret-record — checkpoint emission:

  • RecordSession::maybe_checkpoint(event_id, ticks): triggers when event_id - last_ckpt_event_id >= CKPT_INTERVAL or wall delta >= threshold.
  • Procedure: PTRACE_INTERRUPT all threads → read all RecordedRegs → scan dirty pages → snapshot fds → write checkpoint dir → resume.
  • Invariant I7: checkpoint falls exactly on an event boundary; the event_id field is the boundary event.

dret-replay — seek algorithm:

seek(target_tid, target_ticks):
  ckpt = trace.latest_checkpoint_before(target_ticks)
  restore_from_checkpoint(ckpt)          // restore regs, pages, fds, mmaps
  replay_forward_to(target_tid, target_ticks)  // consume events from ckpt.event_id
  • restore_from_checkpoint: write page contents via PTRACE_POKEDATA or /proc/pid/mem; set registers via PTRACE_SETREGSET; reopen fds (read-only or dup) matching recorded table; restore brk via brk(2) injection; replay mmap sequence from mmaps.bin.
  • replay_forward_to: standard replay loop from ckpt.event_id consuming TraceFrame events until current_ticks >= target_ticks.

dret-replay — reverse execution:

  • reverse_single_step(current_event_id): find checkpoint before current_event_id - 1; restore; replay to current_event_id - 1.
  • reverse_continue(until_condition): binary search over checkpoints; replay-forward probing for condition; narrow bracket; O(log N) checkpoints.
  • ReplaySession exposes fn seek(&mut self, tid: TaskId, ticks: Ticks) -> Result<()>.

dret-gdb — integration:

  • vCont;s → reverse_single_step; vCont;c backward → reverse_continue.
  • Phase 13 builds the full gdbserver stub; Phase 11 delivers the seek primitive it depends on.

dret-sched — scheduler state in checkpoint:

  • Serialize run-queue order, futex wait queues, SchedPoint position as part of Checkpoint.regs_per_task.
  • On restore, reinitialize dret-sched state from checkpoint data.

11.3 Deliverable

  • dret replay --seek <ticks> restores execution to requested tick and halts.
  • Checkpoint files present in trace dir after recording.
  • dret dump --checkpoints lists all checkpoints with event_id, ticks, size.
  • reverse_single_step and reverse_continue primitives pass unit tests.

11.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Pass condition
test_checkpoint_roundtrip restore from checkpoint + replay-forward reaches same register state as linear replay
test_seek_exact_tick seek to tick T lands at T ± 0 ticks
test_reverse_single_step 100 reverse steps correct register sequence
test_checkpoint_dirty_pages only dirty pages written; clean pages not re-snapshotted
test_multithread_checkpoint all threads' regs restored correctly
test_checkpoint_crc corrupted pages.bin detected before restore
Seek latency seek within 100k events: < 500 ms wall time

11.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
fixtures/long_run.trace 1M events, 20 checkpoints seek to each checkpoint event_id exact
fixtures/ckpt_corrupt_pages.bin bit-flip in page data CRC mismatch → abort with error
fixtures/reverse_step_seq.trace 50-event sequence reverse-step 50 times matches forward reg log reversed
fixtures/fd_reopen.trace tracee opens/closes files fd table restored; file offsets match
fixtures/multithread_seek.trace 4-thread program seek to thread-local tick; all threads restored

11.6 Pitfalls

  • Soft-dirty TLB flush latency: after clearing soft-dirty bits, a brief window exists before the kernel updates pagemap. Read pagemap after PTRACE_INTERRUPT confirms all threads stopped to avoid races.
  • Shared pages (mmap MAP_SHARED): COW snapshot captures the private view; shared-mapped file changes are not captured. Log warning; treat shared-writable mmaps as checkpoint limitations (document limitation in operational notes).
  • fd table reconstruction: O_CLOEXEC fds may not be reopenable if the file was deleted. Record inode and device; on restore, attempt to reopen by path; if unlinked, synthetic memfd with recorded content.
  • brk restoration ordering: brk must be restored before replaying mmap calls that reference heap addresses; wrong ordering causes SIGSEGV in tracee.
  • Checkpoint interval too frequent: I/O pressure causes record slowdown; adaptive interval based on dirty-page count (fewer dirty pages → allow longer interval).
  • Multiple threads at different tick positions: checkpoint captures a consistent global state only when all threads are simultaneously stopped. Ensure PTRACE_INTERRUPT all tasks before reading.

11.7 Performance Concerns

  • Dirty-page scan: pagemap read is O(address-space pages); cache last checkpoint's page set for diff.
  • Page snapshot I/O: batch /proc/pid/mem reads into 4 MB chunks; single pread per chunk.
  • Checkpoint directory fsync: defer fsync to background thread; mark checkpoint as pending until flushed; only expose to seek once stable.
  • Restore /proc/pid/mem writes: pwrite in 4 MB batches; measure restore wall time; target < 200 ms for < 1 GB heap.
  • Seek latency dominated by replay-forward distance; checkpoint interval directly controls worst-case seek time.

11.8 Edge Cases

  • Seek to tick 0: restore initial exec state from events.log start; no checkpoint required.
  • Seek past end of trace: return Err(SeekBeyondEnd { max_ticks }).
  • Checkpoint during signal delivery: defer checkpoint until signal is fully recorded (after SignalRecord emitted).
  • Concurrent fork/clone during checkpoint window: if new task created between PTRACE_INTERRUPT and register capture, re-enumerate tasks from waitpid.
  • Trace with no checkpoints (short run): seek falls back to linear replay from start.
  • MADV_SOFT_DIRTY unsupported kernel (< 3.18): fall back to full page snapshot on each checkpoint; warn at startup.

11.9 Failure Analysis

  • Checkpoint CRC mismatch: detected at restore time; abort seek with Err(CheckpointCorrupt); suggest dret pack --verify.
  • Wrong tick after restore: replay immediately diverges; DivergenceReport at event ckpt.event_id + 1. Root cause: page not restored (shared-mmap blind spot). Operator action: inspect dret dump --checkpoint <id> --pages.
  • fd restore failure: file unlinked post-record; replay aborts with FdRestoreFailed { fd, path }; mitigation: pack trace with dret pack --embed-files.
  • Blast radius: seek failure is non-destructive; replay session can retry from a different checkpoint.

11.10 Operational Notes

  • Metric: checkpoint_count, checkpoint_bytes_written, seek_duration_ms, dirty_pages_per_checkpoint.
  • dret dump --checkpoints → table: event_id | ticks | dirty_pages | size_bytes | wall_time.
  • Log INFO checkpoint emitted event_id={} ticks={} dirty_pages={} at each emission.
  • Configurable: --checkpoint-interval-events N (default 50000), --checkpoint-interval-wall-ms M (default 10000).
  • Checkpoint dir is append-only; do not delete mid-session; dret pack compresses and embeds.

Phase 12 — Divergence Detection (Self-Checking Replay)

12.1 Objectives

  • At every replay event, compare (RecordedRegs, Ticks, memory-page hashes) against recorded values.
  • Emit DivergenceReport with first-diverging EventId, kind, expected, and actual values; abort replay.
  • Provide bisect tooling to narrow divergence to a single event.
  • Invariant: any divergence is treated as a recorder bug (P6 fail-closed); never silently continue.

12.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

[P6: fail closed on divergence — any mismatch aborts. I9: RecordedRegs at event E identical in record and replay. ADR-15: xxHash3 for page hashing. Ref: O'Callahan et al. 2017 (divergence detection); Leblanc & Mellor-Crummey 1987 (invariant taxonomy for I9).]

dret-divergence — per-event check [P6: hard abort on first mismatch; I9: field-by-field RecordedRegs equality; ADR-15: xxHash3 chosen over SHA-256 for speed]:

  • fn check_event(frame: &TraceFrame, live_regs: &RecordedRegs, live_ticks: Ticks, touched_pages: &[(MemRange, [u8;32])]) -> Result<(), DivergenceReport>
  • Hash function: Blake3 of page contents (4096 bytes) → 32-byte digest stored in TraceFrame.data_ref during record via PageHash tag.
  • Register comparison: field-by-field equality of RecordedRegs (rip, rsp, rax..r15, rflags, fs_base, gs_base, orig_rax).
  • Tick comparison: live_ticks == frame.ticks per TaskId; skid tolerance of 0 (exact match enforced).

dret-divergence — DivergenceReport type:

struct DivergenceReport { event_id: EventId, tid: TaskId, ticks: Ticks, kind: DivergenceKind, expected: DivergenceValue, actual: DivergenceValue }
enum DivergenceKind { RegMismatch(RegField), TickMismatch, MemHashMismatch(MemRange), EventOrderMismatch }
enum DivergenceValue { Regs(RecordedRegs), Ticks(u64), Hash([u8;32]), EventId(u64) }

dret-record — recording page hashes: after each syscall that produces MemDelta entries, compute a Blake3 hash of each affected page and store as PageHash { range: MemRange, hash: [u8;32] } in data.log via DataRef; gated by --with-page-hashes (disabled by default for performance).

dret-replay — check loop: after injecting each event, call dret_divergence::check_event(...); on Err(report), log the structured DivergenceReport, write it to divergence.json in the trace dir, and call ReplaySession::abort(report); continue the loop only on Ok(()).

dret-divergence — bisect tooling: dret divergence --bisect --trace <dir> --event <id> binary-searches the event range [0, id], restoring the checkpoint at each midpoint and replay-forwarding to check that event, narrowing to the first diverging event; output is first_divergence_event_id, the DivergenceReport, and suggested recorder-side log lines. BisectSession wraps ReplaySession with checkpoint-seek and check callbacks.

dret-divergence — EventOrderMismatch: detected when TraceReader's next event event_id != expected_event_id (I1, I3 violation); immediately fatal, with the report including both ids.

dret-divergence — metrics integration: increments divergence_check_count/divergence_fail_count counters per session; optionally samples 1-in-N events for the page-hash check when --check-rate N is set (a full check is O(dirty pages) per event).

12.3 Deliverable

  • Every replay session runs with divergence checking enabled by default (register + tick; page-hash optional).
  • dret replay exits non-zero with structured DivergenceReport on any mismatch.
  • dret divergence --bisect narrows to single event in O(log N) replays; divergence.json written to trace dir on failure.

12.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Pass condition
test_clean_replay_no_divergence 10k-event replay exits 0; 0 divergence reports
test_inject_reg_mismatch mutate one rax in trace; RegMismatch(Rax) reported at that event
test_inject_tick_mismatch mutate ticks field; TickMismatch reported
test_inject_page_hash_mismatch flip one byte in MemDelta; MemHashMismatch reported
test_bisect_converges bisect on 1k-event trace; first-divergence event correct
test_event_order_mismatch gap in EventId sequence; EventOrderMismatch reported
Page-hash overhead --with-page-hashes adds < 30% replay wall time

12.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
fixtures/clean_10k.trace known-good 10k event trace replay exits 0
fixtures/mutated_rax_event_500.trace rax flipped at event 500 RegMismatch(Rax) at event 500
fixtures/mutated_ticks_event_200.trace ticks incremented at event 200 TickMismatch at event 200
fixtures/gap_event_id.trace EventId jumps from 100 to 102 EventOrderMismatch { expected: 101, actual: 102 }
fixtures/mem_delta_flip.trace single byte flip in data.log MemHashMismatch at affected event
fixtures/bisect_corpus/ 10 traces with single known mutation each bisect returns correct first_divergence_event_id for all 10

12.6 Pitfalls

  • RCB skid: hardware performance counters have a delivery skid; recorded tick may be off by 1–3 counts. Use exact-match enforcement only if RCB source is PERF_COUNT_HW_BRANCH_INSTRUCTIONS with precise_ip=3. Document skid budget; fail if skid > configured tolerance (default 0).
  • orig_rax clobbering: kernel may overwrite orig_rax in syscall restart scenarios; record and check orig_rax separately from rax; mismatch here indicates restart-path divergence.
  • Partial-page MemDelta: syscall writes < 4096 bytes into a page; hash covers entire 4096-byte page; surrounding bytes must match too. Ensure /proc/pid/mem read captures full page, not just delta range.
  • Blake3 hash collision probability: negligible (2^{-256}); no practical mitigation needed; document assumption.
  • Bisect with checkpoints: bisect must use checkpoints for efficiency; if trace has no checkpoints, bisect degenerates to O(N) linear replays. Warn if checkpoint count is 0.
  • Signal delivery divergence masked by reg check: async signal delivery changes rip to signal handler; register mismatch at delivery event may look like data divergence. Check EventKind before interpreting RegMismatch.

12.7 Performance Concerns

  • Register comparison: 17 fields × 8 bytes; 136 bytes compared per event; negligible.
  • Tick comparison: single u64 comparison per event; negligible.
  • Page-hash computation: Blake3 over 4096 bytes ≈ 2–5 µs per page; amortized across events that touch many pages. Gate with --with-page-hashes.
  • Bisect I/O: each bisect iteration is a full replay-forward from checkpoint; minimize by maximizing checkpoint density during recording.
  • divergence.json write: one write at failure; no steady-state I/O cost.

12.8 Edge Cases

  • Divergence at event 0 (first event): no prior checkpoint; replay from start; report immediately.
  • Divergence in signal handler frame: rip points into signal trampoline; ensure RecordedRegs includes rip at signal entry, not return address.
  • Multiple concurrent divergences (multi-thread replay): report the lowest EventId divergence; discard later ones.
  • Replay on different kernel version: kernel may write different padding bytes into RecordedRegs fields; mask padding bytes in comparison (document which fields are kernel-version-stable).
  • Trace truncated mid-event: TraceReader returns Err(UnexpectedEof); treated as EventOrderMismatch variant.
  • --check-rate N with N > 1: first-divergence event may not be detected; bisect required to find it; document limitation.

12.9 Failure Analysis

  • False divergence from skid: TickMismatch by 1–2 ticks on every event. Root cause: RCB skid. Fix: re-record with precise_ip=3; or widen skid tolerance in config.
  • Divergence from untracked nondeterminism: RegMismatch at event not associated with RdtscTrap/CpuidTrap. Indicates Phase 10 coverage gap. Bisect to event; inspect EventKind; add interception for missing source.
  • Divergence from Phase 8 scheduler mismatch: TickMismatch on thread switch event. Root cause: SchedSwitch tick boundary incorrect. Inspect dret dump --filter SchedSwitch.
  • Blast radius: divergence aborts the replay session; no state is corrupted; trace file is unmodified; operator can re-run with --check-rate 1 for full coverage.
  • Postmortem: divergence.json + dret dump --event <id> + register diff is sufficient for root-cause in > 90% of cases.

12.10 Operational Notes

  • Default mode: register + tick check at every event; page-hash check disabled.
  • Flags: --with-page-hashes (enable), --check-rate N (sample 1-in-N), --skid-tolerance N (default 0).
  • Metrics: divergence_check_count (counter), divergence_fail_count (counter), bisect_iterations (histogram).
  • Log format: ERROR divergence event_id={} tid={} kind={:?} expected={:?} actual={:?}.
  • divergence.json schema: { event_id, tid, ticks, kind, expected, actual, trace_path, replay_timestamp }.
  • CI integration: run dret replay --with-page-hashes on all conformance traces; non-zero exit fails the build.
  • Operator remediation guide: DivergenceReport → dret divergence --bisect → inspect event kind → add missing interception or file recorder bug.

Phase 13 — gdbserver + reverse execution

13.1 Objectives

  • Expose a gdbserver-compatible RSP socket over an active ReplaySession; no separate process required.
  • Map GDB c, s, n, rc, rs to forward replay-advance and checkpoint-rewind.
  • Implement reverse-step and reverse-continue via nearest-checkpoint + replay-forward bisection.
  • Hardware-style watchpoints: replay forward from checkpoint, compare MemDelta per event until write to watched range detected.
  • Scope: dret-gdb crate; depends on dret-replay, dret-divergence, dret-trace.
  • Constraint: RSP framing must be correct for GDB >= 9.x and LLDB >= 14; test with both.

13.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

[P2: no write-back to trace — GDB M packet updates replay session state only. I7: reverse-step seeks to checkpoint boundary then replays forward. ADR-29: Unix socket default transport. Ref: GDB Remote Serial Protocol §E; Laughlin 2019 (omniscient debugger query interface); mozilla/rr GdbServer.cc.]

RSP socket layer — dret-gdb::rsp [ADR-29: Unix socket default; GDB RSP §E packet syntax; mozilla/rr GdbServer.cc RSP isolation lesson]

  • TCP listener (default port 1234) + Unix socket mode (--socket flag).
  • Frame codec: $<data>#<checksum>; ACK (+) / NAK (-) retransmit; NoAckMode negotiation via QStartNoAckMode.
  • Packet dispatch table: ? (halt reason), g/G (read/write all regs), p n/P n=v (single reg), m addr,len/M addr,len:xx (memory), c/C (continue), s/S (step), vCont (multi-thread continue/step).
  • Reverse packets: bc (reverse-continue), bs (reverse-step); advertise via qSupported → ReverseContinue+;ReverseStep+.
  • Breakpoints: Z0/z0 software BP → insert into replay watchlist; Z2/z2/Z3/z3 write/read watchpoints → WatchRange table.

Replay integration — dret-gdb::session

  • GdbSession { replay: ReplaySession, checkpoints: CheckpointIndex, watch_ranges: Vec<WatchRange> }.
  • Forward-step: advance ReplaySession by one TraceFrame; extract RecordedRegs → RSP register reply.
  • Forward-continue: run replay until SW breakpoint EventId hit or watchpoint fires.
  • Watchpoint fire detection: after each event, diff MemDelta against WatchRange set; report T05 watch:addr.

Reverse execution — dret-gdb::reverse

reverse_step(target_event):
  checkpoint = checkpoints.nearest_before(target_event - 1)
  replay_forward_to(checkpoint.event_id)
  step until event == target_event - 1
  report registers
  • reverse_continue(target): replay from nearest checkpoint; stop at first matching SW BP or watchpoint hit before target_event.
  • Bisection for watchpoints: binary search checkpoint intervals; O(log N) replay passes; N = events between checkpoints.
  • CheckpointIndex: sorted Vec<(EventId, CheckpointRef)>; nearest_before(e) = upper_bound - 1.

Register/memory mapping

  • RecordedRegs → RSP g packet: layout matches GDB x86-64 register order (rax, rbx, rcx, rdx, rsi, rdi, rbp, rsp, r8-r15, rip, rflags, cs, ss, ds, es, fs, gs).
  • Memory reads via ReplaySession::read_mem(addr, len) → reads from replay tracee via ptrace pread.

Thread support

  • Multi-thread: qfThreadInfo/qsThreadInfo enumerate recorded TaskId list.
  • Hg tid / Hc tid set thread context; replay locks to single logical CPU per I6.
  • Thread-stop reply includes thread:<tid> in T packet.

13.3 Deliverable

  • dret-gdb crate compiles; dret-cli replay --gdb :1234 <trace> opens RSP listener.
  • GDB target remote :1234 connects; info registers, x/16x addr, SW breakpoints, rc/rs all function.
  • reverse-continue from a diverged state rewinds to prior breakpoint; wall-time for reverse-step ≤ checkpoint-interval replay time.

13.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Criterion
RSP framing unit tests All GDB 9.x packet types encode/decode round-trip without checksum error
Forward continue to SW BP Stops at correct EventId; registers match recorded
reverse-step N=1 Steps back exactly one event; rip matches event N-1 recorded value
reverse-continue to watchpoint Detects first write to watched range; ≤ 3 replay passes
Multi-thread enumeration info threads lists all TaskId; per-thread register read correct
LLDB compatibility process connect connect://localhost:1234 connects and single-steps

13.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected Output
fixtures/gdb/hello_bp.trace SW breakpoint on main RSP T05 at correct rip
fixtures/gdb/reverse_write.trace Watchpoint on heap write reverse-continue halts at writer event
fixtures/gdb/multithread.trace 3-thread trace All TaskId visible; per-thread regs distinct
fixtures/gdb/rsp_malformed.bin Truncated RSP packet Server sends - NAK; no panic
fixtures/gdb/no_checkpoint.trace Trace with no checkpoints Reverse-step falls back to trace-start replay

13.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal
Register order mismatch GDB shows wrong rip RSP g packet layout differs from GDB x86-64 expedited reg order Compare with gdb/regformats/i386/amd64.dat
Reverse-continue overshoot Stops one event too early Off-by-one in nearest_before + replay-forward boundary Log (checkpoint_eid, target_eid, stop_eid) triple
Watchpoint false negative Write not detected MemDelta only records kernel-injected writes; userspace direct writes missed unless page-fault trapped Enable dirty-page scan on every step in watch mode
SW breakpoint / replay divergence Breakpoint address not in recorded trace Replay inserts INT3; tracee sees different instruction stream Use EventId-based BP, not address injection
ACK stall GDB hangs after packet Server consumed packet but sent no reply Log every sent byte; check NoAckMode negotiation state
Reverse over exec reverse-continue crosses an Exec event AddressSpace invalidated at exec; checkpoint before exec is invalid Detect EventKind::Exec boundary; stop reverse at exec

13.7 Performance Concerns

  • Checkpoint granularity: coarse (rare checkpoints) → long replay passes for reverse; fine → large trace + memory overhead. Target: ≤ 100 K events between checkpoints.
  • Replay-forward cost per reverse-step: proportional to events since last checkpoint; emit dret.gdb.reverse_step_events metric.
  • Memory: each checkpoint holds dirty-page snapshot; GC old checkpoints beyond a configurable depth window.
  • RSP I/O: packet parsing is on the critical latency path; use a BufWriter/BufReader pair; avoid per-byte syscalls.
  • Watchpoint scan: O(events × watch_ranges) per replay pass; cap watch_ranges at 8 (hardware-counter analogy).

13.8 Edge Cases

  • reverse-continue from event 0: no checkpoint; must replay from trace start; emit warning, not abort.
  • Watchpoint on stack-allocated variable: address changes across stack frames; invalidate watch on SchedSwitch or frame change.
  • GDB sends vKill: tear down ReplaySession cleanly; release all ptrace attachments.
  • Large memory read (m 0,ffffffff): clamp to valid mapped ranges per AddressSpace; return E03 for unmapped.
  • RSP connection drop mid-reverse: ReplaySession left in mid-replay state; reset to last stable checkpoint on reconnect.
  • clone during replay with GDB attached: new TaskId appears mid-session; qfThreadInfo must refresh lazily.

13.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Divergence during reverse replay DivergenceReport from dret-divergence Incorrect register state reported to GDB Abort reverse; report E47 RSP error; log DivergenceReport
RSP socket bind failure EADDRINUSE on listen GDB cannot connect Retry on port+1; or --socket /tmp/dret.sock
Corrupt checkpoint used for reverse Page hash mismatch at restore Wrong memory state, silent wrong answer Validate Checkpoint CRC on load; fall back to earlier checkpoint
Replay session OOM during reverse allocator failure Server process crash Pre-reserve replay memory budget; emit metric before OOM kill

13.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: dret.gdb.reverse_step_events (histogram), dret.gdb.checkpoint_replays (counter), dret.gdb.rsp_packets (counter by type).
  • Structured log fields: {event="gdb_stop", eid, tid, rip, cause} on every halt.
  • dret dump --gdb-log <trace> replays all SW BP and watchpoint events for offline inspection without GDB.
  • RSP port configurable via --gdb-port; Unix socket via --gdb-socket; default port 1234.
  • No persistent state between GDB sessions; each target remote starts a fresh GdbSession from trace root.
  • Migration: if RSP extension set changes, bump dret-gdb crate minor version; qSupported re-negotiates per session.

Phase 14 — trace packing, hardening, conformance

14.1 Objectives

  • Produce a self-contained .dret pack archive (embed all mapped ELF files, shared libraries, and vDSO by build-id; no host-path dependencies) and compress data.log with zstd while keeping events.log uncompressed for O(1) seek.
  • Performance hardening: batch ptrace calls, syscall-result buffering in recorder, reduce stop/resume round trips; build a conformance corpus of ≥ 20 representative recorded programs (each must replay bit-exact), fuzz dret-trace's reader with cargo-fuzz/libFuzzer for zero panics/UB on malformed input, and soak-test 1000 record+replay executions of each corpus program, asserting 100% replay equivalence.

14.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

[P7: batch flush must preserve crash-replayable prefix. P8: events.log stays uncompressed for O(1) seek. ADR-26: zstd for data.log compression. ADR-21: cargo-fuzz for trace reader hardening. Ref: mozilla/rr TraceStream.cc compression design; O'Callahan 2017 (conformance corpus).]

Pack format & compression — dret-trace::pack/compress [ADR-26: zstd data.log; ADR-05: events.log uncompressed for O(1) EventId seek; P2: pack is immutable archive]. .dret is a zstd-framed container, internal layout:

[pack_header: magic 8B + version u16 + flags u32]
[manifest: JSON array of {kind, path, build_id_hex, offset, len}]
[events.log: verbatim copy]
[data.log: zstd-compressed]
[mmap.log: verbatim]
[ckpt/: checkpoint blobs verbatim]
[libs/: ELF blobs keyed by build_id]

TraceWriter::pack(out_path) iterates mmap.log for all mapped files, dedups by build-id (sha1 of .note.gnu.build-id), and embeds each once; vDSO is extracted from /proc/pid/mem at the [vdso] range during record and embedded as kind="vdso". TraceReader::open_pack(path) extracts to a temp dir on demand, or reads in-memory with offset arithmetic. data.log compresses at zstd level 3 (speed/ratio balance), streamed during record via zstd::Encoder with 1 MB chunks; DataRef(u64) remains a byte offset into the uncompressed data.log, with the pack maintaining a seek table (chunk boundaries) so a lookup decompresses only the relevant chunk. events.log stays uncompressed — fixed-size TraceFrame records, O(1) binary search by EventId/Ticks; mmap.log and checkpoints are zstd-compressed at pack time (not streaming), given their small total size.

ptrace batch hardening — dret-record::batch: replace PTRACE_PEEKDATA/PTRACE_POKEDATA with /proc/pid/mem pread/pwrite for bulk transfers (≥8 bytes); group register reads into one PTRACE_GETREGSET NT_PRSTATUS per stop instead of per-field; buffer up to 64 SyscallRecords in a ring before flushing to TraceWriter (flush on checkpoint or buffer full); use SECCOMP_RET_ERRNO as a fast path for pointer-free syscalls (gettid, getpid) to return the recorded value with no supervisor round trip (Phase 2 already classifies these); pipeline PTRACE_CONT immediately after recording completes to overlap tracee execution with trace I/O.

Conformance corpus — dret-record::conformance:

Program Category Key Nondeterminism
hello_world baseline none
ls /tmp filesystem, getdents file order
date time syscall clock_gettime
stress_threads_4 multi-thread mutex futex, SchedSwitch
signal_handler SIGUSR1 async async signal delivery tick
mmap_anon mmap/munmap address layout
rdtsc_loop rdtsc TSC values
cpuid_check cpuid feature bits
fork_exec clone/exec pid/tid assignment
socket_echo read/write socket network data
epoll_server epoll_wait event ordering
sqlite_insert file I/O heavy pread/pwrite
python3 -c ... interpreter many syscalls
openssl_hash rdrand/getrandom entropy source
stress_malloc brk/mmap heap layout
longjmp_signal SA_SIGACTION sigreturn
vdso_time vDSO gettimeofday vDSO interception
thread_sanitizer_test TSan-instrumented binary atomic ordering
large_write writev ≥ 1 MB large MemDelta
crash_sigsegv SIGSEGV sync signal

Fuzzer & soak test — dret-trace-fuzz/dret-record::soak: fuzz_trace_reader feeds arbitrary bytes as events.log, exercising TraceReader::next_frame/seek_by_event_id/seek_by_ticks; fuzz_pack_open feeds arbitrary bytes as a .dret pack, exercising manifest parse + chunk decompress; corpus seeds are truncated real traces, bit-flipped headers, wrong CRCs, overlapping DataRef offsets, aborting on any panic (panic=abort, ASAN+MSAN enabled, 1 hour minimum per CI gate). Soak: for each corpus program, dret record then dret replay --verify × 1000 iterations; equivalence metric is DivergenceReport count == 0 across all iterations; record-overhead wall_time_record/wall_time_native asserted ≤ 20×, replay-overhead wall_time_replay/wall_time_native asserted ≤ 5× (no real syscalls).

14.3 Deliverable

  • dret pack <trace-dir> -o out.dret produces a standalone archive; dret replay out.dret succeeds on a machine without original binaries; data.log compression ratio ≥ 2× on large_write corpus entry; recorder throughput ≥ 5 K syscall events/second on single-threaded corpus entries; fuzzer runs 1 hour with zero panics/UB, corpus integrated into CI; soak test passes 20 000 record+replay pairs (20 programs × 1000) with zero divergences.

14.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Criterion
pack_roundtrip Unpack + replay == original trace; EventId/Ticks identical
pack_missing_lib Replay on host without original libs succeeds using embedded ELFs
data_log_compression zstd ratio ≥ 2× for large_write; DataRef seek correct after decompress
batch_ptrace_perf stress_threads_4 recorder overhead ≤ 20× vs native
fuzz_no_panic 1 h libFuzzer run; zero panics; zero ASAN/MSAN findings
soak_equivalence 20 × 1000 replay runs; zero DivergenceReport
corpus_coverage All 20 corpus programs record + replay without error
forward_compat Trace written by version N replays under version N+1 reader

14.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal
Build-id collision Wrong ELF embedded Two distinct ELFs share build-id (hand-built binaries) Warn + embed both; key by (build_id, file_size)
zstd seek table drift Wrong bytes at DataRef after decompress Chunk boundary off-by-one in seek table Unit-test every chunk boundary with known offsets
seccomp fast-path wrong classification Syscall with pointer arg bypasses supervisor dret-syscall ABI table missing a pointer arg Audit ABI table against strace source; integration test with valgrind
Batch flush lost on recorder crash Last 64 buffered events missing from trace Ring not flushed before SIGKILL Flush on every SIGTERM; also flush when ring is half full (P7 crash-only)
Pack extraction TOCTOU Replayer uses partially extracted pack Concurrent dret replay on same pack Atomic rename of temp dir; lock file per pack extraction
Fuzz corpus not seeded Fuzzer makes no progress Empty corpus; random bytes never hit framing magic Seed from real truncated traces; verify seed inputs are valid starting points
Overcount skid under batch ptrace Ticks boundary missed Batching delays PTRACE_INTERRUPT beyond tick boundary Bound batch size by tick window; re-check ticks after every batch flush

14.7 Performance Concerns

  • /proc/pid/mem pwrite batching: single pwrite per SyscallRecord's mem_effects vector; avoid per-byte ptrace pokes.
  • TraceWriter I/O: async write via io_uring (optional feature flag); fall back to blocking BufWriter (4 MB buffer).
  • zstd streaming: encoder holds 1 MB input buffer; flush to file every 4 MB uncompressed; keep latency bounded.
  • Pack creation time: dominated by ELF embedding; dedup by build-id avoids repeated reads of libc.so.
  • Soak test wall time: parallelize across corpus programs (rayon); total soak ≤ 4 h on 8-core CI host.
  • Fuzzer throughput: target ≥ 50 K executions/second; profile TraceReader::next_frame for hot allocation paths.

14.8 Edge Cases

  • ELF mapped from tmpfs (memfd): no path on disk; embed from /proc/pid/mem at mapped range; mark kind="anon_exec".
  • Trace on read-only filesystem: pack write fails; emit Err(ReadOnlyTrace); do not corrupt original.
  • data.log > 4 GB: DataRef(u64) handles; seek table must store 64-bit chunk offsets.
  • Corpus program that forks into daemon: record must follow only the initial process tree; detach background children cleanly.
  • Partial pack write (disk full): detect via ENOSPC; leave .dret.partial file; dret pack refuses to overwrite with partial.
  • Unknown frame tag in forward-compat test: TraceReader skips field per P8; soak replay must not diverge on skipped fields.
  • Replay on different kernel version: AddressSpace layout may differ; pack must embed [vdso] image; replay uses embedded vDSO, not host's.

14.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Pack missing mapped ELF dret replay can't find library by build-id Replay fails to exec tracee Re-record on original host; or use --lib-path fallback
zstd decompress error mid-replay Err(ZstdDecompress) at DataRef Replay aborts; partial execution Re-pack from original trace dir; validate with dret pack --verify
Soak divergence detected DivergenceReport in soak run Nondeterminism bug in recorder Bisect: reduce to minimal reproducer trace; file against recorder phase
Fuzz-found panic in TraceReader CI fuzz target crashes Reader is unusable on malformed input Fix + add crash input to regression corpus; re-enable CI gate
Batch flush lost Replay ends before last events Recorder killed without flush Detect via EventId gap > 1 at trace end; mark trace incomplete; partial replay still valid up to last good frame

14.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: dret.pack.embedded_libs (gauge), dret.pack.data_log_compressed_bytes (counter), dret.record.syscall_events_per_sec (gauge), dret.soak.divergence_count (counter); structured log on pack: {event="pack_complete", path, events_count, data_log_ratio, embedded_libs_count, duration_ms}; dret dump <pack|trace> subcommand prints TraceFrame summary, compression stats, embedded lib inventory for postmortem.
  • Versioning: dret-trace crate version encoded in trace header version field; CI rejects replay of a trace written by a future minor version without explicit --allow-future-version; rollback safety: if a new pack format breaks old dret replay, the version field lets old binaries emit Err(UnsupportedVersion) cleanly, never a silent wrong answer.
  • Fuzz CI gate: runs on every PR (60-second budget per target), full 1-hour run nightly; corpus managed in fixtures/corpus/, each entry a hermetic shell script + expected sha256 of replay stdout, CI asserting hash match.

Threat Model

Trust Boundaries

Derived from the architecture diagram. Two principals:

  • Supervisor (dret-record / dret-replay, runs as user with CAP_SYS_PTRACE): trusted core, owns the trace.
  • Tracee (recorded program, arbitrarily hostile or buggy): fully untrusted; all its outputs pass through the supervisor before reaching disk or the outside world.

Secondary boundaries:

  • Trace directory on disk: writable by supervisor only; read by replay, dret-cli, dret-gdb.
  • GDB client: connects to dret-gdb via TCP/Unix socket; authenticated by file permissions or token.
  • Operator/CI: invokes dret-cli; trusted for configuration, untrusted for trace content.

Threat Table

# Threat Vector Component Mitigation Detection Telemetry Recovery
T1 Malicious tracee escapes ptrace via kernel bug Exploited CVE in ptrace/seccomp subsystem; tracee sends crafted syscall sequence dret-ptrace, dret-record Run supervisor in minimal-privilege namespace; seccomp-BPF filter on supervisor itself; keep kernel patched Unexpected supervisor signals; process tree anomalies SIGCHLD demux anomaly counter Kill tracee via EXITKILL; quarantine trace
T2 Tracee injects arbitrary data into trace via large pointer-arg buffers Syscall with huge buffer; supervisor faithfully records all bytes dret-mem, dret-trace Enforce max MemDelta size per event (configurable cap, default 64 MB); reject oversized records MemDelta size exceeds cap threshold bytes_recorded gauge per event Truncate record; mark frame TRUNCATED; continue or abort per policy
T3 Malformed / crafted trace file parsed by dret-trace reader Corrupt or adversarial events.log / data.log delivered to replay dret-trace, dret-replay CRC per frame; versioned header validation; length-prefixed DataRef bounds check; no unsafe indexing CRC mismatch; frame length overrun; out-of-bounds DataRef parse_error_total counter Abort replay; emit DivergenceReport with corpus frame index
T4 Resource exhaustion via trace amplification Program generates enormous memory-effect payloads; fills disk dret-mem, dret-trace Disk quota / ulimit on trace directory; configurable max-trace-size hard stop; page-diff dedup Disk usage rate alert; bytes_written rate gauge disk_bytes_written counter Stop record cleanly at quota; trace is valid prefix per P7
T5 Privilege escalation via PTRACE_POKEDATA into supervisor memory Tracee attempts to ptrace the supervisor dret-ptrace Supervisor never calls PTRACE_TRACEME; seccomp filter blocks ptrace() on itself; yama ptrace_scope ≥ 1 ptrace() denied events in audit log audit_denied_ptrace_total Alert; kill tracee
T6 Divergence silently swallowed; wrong replay produces misleading gdb state Replay bug masks divergence; operator debugs wrong execution dret-divergence, dret-replay P6 / I9: any divergence hard-aborts replay; no silent continue path; DivergenceReport written to trace dir Missed divergence == incorrect hash comparison divergence_events_total; abort_on_divergence boolean Abort; force operator to file bug; provide bisect tooling
T7 Perf counter skid yields wrong tick values; phantom divergence or missed event injection PMU overcount / skid causes Ticks read too late dret-time Overcount correction algorithm (Phase 4); singlestep-to-boundary after overflow signal; skid budget constant tick_correction_applied counter; correction_delta histogram Per-task tick correction log Log correction; if uncorrectable emit WARN and fail closed
T8 Trace replay side effects leak to host (file writes, network) PTRACE_SYSEMU skipped; real syscall executed on replay dret-replay I5: PTRACE_SYSEMU enforced unconditionally; seccomp SECCOMP_RET_TRACE as double fence; audit all syscall inject paths Any real I/O during replay triggers audit alert real_syscall_leaked_total (must stay zero) Hard abort replay; treat as critical bug
T9 gdbserver RSP injection via crafted GDB packets Attacker sends malformed RSP packets to dret-gdb port dret-gdb Bind to loopback only by default; optional auth token; length-prefixed packet parser with strict bounds Parse error counter; unexpected packet type rsp_parse_error_total Close connection; log source
T10 Supply-chain: malicious dependency in Cargo.lock Compromised crate publishes new version with backdoor All crates Pin exact versions in Cargo.lock; cargo-deny license + advisory checks in CI; reproducible builds cargo audit in CI; advisory DB match CI advisory_failure count Block merge; bump to safe version
T11 Checkpoint restore overwrites wrong address space Bug in dret-mem restores pages to wrong segments; corrupts tracee dret-mem, dret-replay Validate MemRange against AddressSpace map before pwrite; SIGABRT on out-of-range Address out-of-segment error page_restore_oor_total Abort replay; emit DivergenceReport
T12 EventId gap / wrap attack corrupts ordering Attacker edits trace to create EventId gaps or reuse dret-trace I1: reader enforces strictly monotone EventId; gap → parse error; u64 space precludes practical wrap EventId non-monotone counter eventid_gap_total Abort; report corruption
T13 Operator accidentally replays with wrong binary version ABI mismatch; diverges immediately or later dret-replay Embed build-id + ELF hash in trace header (Phase 14); dret-replay validates on open Build-id mismatch warning build_id_mismatch_total Warn + refuse replay; operator must supply matching binary
T14 Async signal storm DoS Flood of async signals overwhelms supervisor waitpid loop dret-record, dret-sched Rate-limit signal recording per task; maximum pending-signal queue; SIGKILL tracee on overflow signal_queue_depth gauge; overflow counter async_signal_drop_total Abort record cleanly; trace is valid prefix
