Operational Readiness

Metrics Strategy

Export via dret-cli ps --metrics (Prometheus text format) or embedded metrics server (optional feature flag).

Metric Type Labels Meaning
dret_events_total Counter kind, session_id Events recorded/replayed per kind
dret_ticks_current Gauge tid, session_id Current Ticks per task
dret_trace_bytes_written Counter session_id Bytes appended to trace
dret_syscall_duration_ns Histogram nr, phase Supervisor overhead per syscall stop
dret_tick_correction_delta Histogram tid RCB overcount correction magnitude
dret_divergence_events_total Counter kind, session_id Divergence events (must be zero in production)
dret_checkpoint_restore_duration_ms Histogram session_id Checkpoint restore latency
dret_replay_real_syscall_total Counter session_id Real syscalls during replay (must be zero; alert on > 0)
dret_parse_error_total Counter file, session_id Trace parse errors
dret_signal_queue_depth Gauge tid, session_id Pending async signals in supervisor queue

Structured Logging

  • Format: JSON lines (NDJSON), emitted to stderr by default; configurable to file.
  • Log levels: ERROR, WARN, INFO, DEBUG, TRACE (TRACE only in --debug-ptrace builds).
  • Mandatory fields: ts (ISO-8601 + ns), level, session_id, event_id (if in event context), phase (record|replay), msg.
  • Optional fields: tid, ticks, nr (syscall number), divergence (DivergenceReport fragment).
  • Never log tracee memory contents at INFO; DEBUG may log first 64 bytes with SENSITIVE tag.
  • On divergence: emit full DivergenceReport as one structured ERROR line; include expected and actual as JSON objects.

Tracing Strategy

  • OpenTelemetry spans for supervisor hot paths: ptrace_stop, syscall_intercept, mem_capture, checkpoint_write, checkpoint_restore.
  • Span attributes: session_id, event_id, ticks, tid.
  • Export to OTLP (env DRET_OTLP_ENDPOINT); disabled by default.
  • Sampling: 1-in-100 for normal record; 1-in-1 for divergence or checkpoint operations.

Dashboard Design

Recommended Grafana panels (one dashboard per session type: record / replay):

Panel Visualization Key Signals
Event throughput Time-series dret_events_total rate by kind
Trace write rate Time-series dret_trace_bytes_written rate
Syscall intercept overhead Heatmap dret_syscall_duration_ns histogram
Tick correction delta Heatmap dret_tick_correction_delta histogram
Divergence events Stat (red if > 0) dret_divergence_events_total
Real syscalls on replay Stat (red if > 0) dret_replay_real_syscall_total
Signal queue depth Gauge dret_signal_queue_depth max across tids
Checkpoint restore latency Time-series P50/P95/P99 dret_checkpoint_restore_duration_ms
Disk usage Time-series derived from dret_trace_bytes_written vs quota

Alerting Philosophy

  • Alert only on conditions requiring immediate operator action; no noisy informational alerts.
  • Severity CRITICAL: dret_divergence_events_total > 0; dret_replay_real_syscall_total > 0; supervisor exits unexpectedly.
  • Severity HIGH: disk usage > 90% of quota; dret_parse_error_total rising; checkpoint restore > 5s.
  • Severity WARN: tick correction delta > skid budget; signal queue depth > 100; perf_event_open failure.
  • All alerts include session_id and event_id for direct correlation to trace.

SLO Definitions

SLO Target Measurement Window Breach Action
Replay determinism: zero divergence on conformance corpus 100% Per release Block release
Zero real syscall side effects on replay 100% Per session Hard abort; file P0 bug
Record overhead vs native wall time ≤ 20× median Conformance corpus Perf investigation; not a blocker
Trace parse success on valid traces 100% Per release (fuzz corpus) Block release
Checkpoint restore latency P99 ≤ 2 s Production sessions Perf investigation
GDB reverse-step latency P95 ≤ 500 ms per step (checkpoint-bounded) Lab benchmarks Note in release

Incident Response

  1. Divergence alert fires: Stop all active replay sessions. Capture DivergenceReport + last 500 events from events.log. Identify first-divergence EventId. Run dret replay --bisect to isolate. File bug with trace attached. Do not re-use trace for debugging until bisect complete.
  2. Real syscall on replay: Immediately quarantine session. Audit dret_replay_real_syscall log line for syscall number and EventId. Review inject path for stop-type mismatch. Treat as severity-1 bug.
  3. Supervisor crash / SIGKILL: Verify EXITKILL removed tracee (pgrep by session ppid). Open trace prefix with dret dump --last-frame. Determine if trace prefix is replayable to last good CRC frame.
  4. Disk full during record: Trace is valid up to last complete frame. Extend storage or reduce --max-trace-size. Re-record from a checkpoint if partial trace insufficient.
  5. Perf counter failure in CI: Escalate to infra; bare-metal runners required. Do not merge until PMU available.

Runbooks

  • RB-01: Recover partial trace after supervisor crash — verify last CRC frame, open with dret replay --last-good.
  • RB-02: Diagnose and bisect a divergence — dret replay --bisect --start <eid> --end <eid>.
  • RB-03: Extend trace directory quota without data loss.
  • RB-04: Upgrade kernel version — regression test full conformance corpus; verify vDSO interception.
  • RB-05: Attach gdb to a running replay session — gdb -ex "target remote :1234".
  • RB-06: Pack and archive a trace for long-term storage — dret pack --compress --embed-binaries.
  • RB-07: Fuzz trace reader against corpus — cargo fuzz run trace_reader.
  • RB-08: Calibrate RCB skid constant for new CPU micro-architecture.
  • RB-09: Restore from checkpoint and replay forward — dret replay --seek-to-tick <ticks>.
  • RB-10: Rotate trace directory without replay disruption — replay reads are read-only; swap directory atomically.

Trace Management / Backup

  • Trace directory is append-only during record; never modify after record complete.
  • Archive: dret pack --compress lz4 --embed-binaries → single .dret archive; stores build-ids, mapped file content, compressed data.log.
  • Backup: standard filesystem snapshot or rsync of packed .dret file; no special tooling required.
  • Retention policy: operator-defined; dret pack --gc-checkpoints prunes intermediate checkpoints to reduce size while preserving full replayability.
  • Integrity check: dret pack --verify recomputes all frame CRCs and validates EventId monotonicity.

Deployment Topology

  • Standalone: dret-cli on developer workstation; no daemon; trace stored locally.
  • CI integration: dret record wraps test binary; trace archived as CI artifact; replay on failure.
  • Remote debugging: trace packed and transferred; replay on analyst workstation with --binary pointing to matching build.
  • No network-facing service in normal operation; dret-gdb binds loopback only.

Rolling Upgrade / Rollback

  • Supervisor binary and tracee are independent; no shared in-process state.
  • Upgrade: replace dret binary; new sessions use new binary; in-flight sessions complete with old binary.
  • Rollback: reinstall previous binary; old traces replay with matching binary version (build-id check).
  • No database migrations; no persistent daemon state.

Canary

  • CI conformance corpus (Phase 14) acts as canary: full record+replay of each corpus program required to pass before release.
  • Canary metric: dret_divergence_events_total == 0 across all corpus sessions.

Compatibility & Versioning

Trace Format Versioning

Trace container header (dret-trace, Phase 3) carries:

TraceHeader {
  magic: [u8; 8],        // b"DRET\x00\x00\x00\x01"
  format_version: u32,   // bumped on breaking schema change
  feature_flags: u64,    // bit-per-optional-feature
  min_reader_version: u32, // oldest reader that can parse this trace
  producer_semver: [u8; 16], // dret crate semver string, null-padded
  creation_ts: u64,      // Unix ns
  checksum: u32,         // CRC32C of header bytes preceding this field
}
  • format_version uses major.minor encoding (high 16 / low 16 bits of u32).
  • Minor bump: new optional tagged fields added; older readers skip via length-prefix (P8).
  • Major bump: breaking structural change; older readers reject with IncompatibleVersion error.
  • min_reader_version set to current major when backward compatibility is broken.

Frame-Level Forward Compatibility

  • Every TraceFrame is length-prefixed and ends with a CRC32C.
  • Unknown EventKind variants: reader must skip unknown kinds if feature_flags does not mandate them (opt-in via flag bit).
  • Unknown tagged fields within a frame: skip by length; never error on unknown tags.
  • DataRef offsets are stable for the lifetime of a trace; data.log is append-only.

Type Stability Guarantees

Type Stability Change Policy
EventId(u64) Stable; never reused (I1) Breaking to redefine; requires major version bump
Ticks(u64) Stable; monotone per task (I2) Breaking to change RCB definition; major bump + migration note
TaskId(u32) Stable across record/replay; not OS pid Breaking to change mapping; major bump
TraceFrame layout Minor-compatible via tagged fields Additive fields: minor bump; removal: major bump
RecordedRegs (x86-64 GPR set) Stable for x86-64 target New ISA requires new variant; existing x86-64 traces unaffected
SyscallRecord.args[6] Stable ABI change in kernel is a major bump
Checkpoint Minor-compatible New field → minor; field removal → major
DivergenceReport Semver-relaxed (output only, not stored in trace) Can change without version bump; not part of wire format

API Stability (dret-core public types)

  • dret-core public API follows Rust SemVer: breaking change → major crate version.
  • dret-cli subcommand interface (flags, exit codes) follows SemVer: new optional flags = minor; removed/renamed flags = major.
  • gdbserver RSP wire protocol: follows GDB Remote Serial Protocol spec; extensions under qDRET prefix; standard RSP subset stable.
  • dret-trace on-disk format version is independent of crate semver; both are recorded in the header.

Rolling-Upgrade Guarantees

  • A newer dret replay can always read traces produced by an equal or older format_version (minor bumps are backward-compatible).
  • A newer dret record never writes a format_version that an older dret replay cannot detect as incompatible (min_reader_version field enforces this).
  • Mixed-version record+replay is supported within the same major format version; tested in CI.

Deprecation Policy

  • Deprecated EventKind variants: marked with #[deprecated] in dret-core for one major release cycle; emitted with a WARN log; removed in next major.
  • Deprecated CLI flags: printed with a deprecation warning for one minor release; removed in next major.
  • Deprecated trace fields: tagged as deprecated in the schema changelog; readers continue to parse for min_reader_version period; writers stop emitting after one major.
  • No silent removal of any type or flag without a prior deprecation cycle.

Migration Strategy

  • Format major version migration: dret pack --migrate --target-version <N> rewrites events.log and data.log to new schema; original preserved unless --replace passed.
  • Migration is always one-directional (old → new); no downgrade path for major bumps.
  • Migration tool validates CRC on input and output; aborts on any error; output is written to a new directory to preserve original.
  • Kernel ABI changes (new syscall numbers, changed argument layouts): update dret-syscall ABI table; bump minor version; traces recorded on old kernel remain replayable on old kernel via --kernel-abi-version flag.

Semantic Versioning Policy Summary

Component Semver Scope Breaking Change Trigger
dret-core crate Rust SemVer Public type or trait change
Trace format Independent format_version u32 Schema structural change
dret-cli flags Semver via crate version Flag removal/rename
gdbserver RSP extension qDRET namespace Incompatible extension change
dret-gdb TCP protocol Semver via crate version Auth/framing change

Trace Portability Constraints

  • Traces are not portable across CPU architectures; RecordedRegs is x86-64 specific.
  • Traces are not portable across kernel major ABI changes (syscall numbering).
  • Traces are portable across kernel minor versions if syscall ABI is unchanged.
  • dret pack --embed-binaries embeds all mapped ELF files; replay requires only the packed archive + matching kernel ABI, not the original build environment.
  • Portability invariants documented in dret dump --portability-check output.

Testing & Verification Strategy

Unit Testing

Crate Unit Targets Method
dret-core EventId monotonicity, Ticks ordering, TraceFrame serde round-trip #[test] inline; proptest for type invariants
dret-ptrace ptrace wrapper panic boundaries, waitpid demux state machine mock via ptrace stub in test harness; no real fork
dret-syscall ABI table completeness (all Linux x86-64 syscalls ≤ kernel 6.x), arg-slot → pointer/value classification table-driven; compare against strace syscall list
dret-time RCB overcount correction formula; tick monotonicity under simulated skid synthetic counter injection; property: corrected_ticks ≤ raw_ticks
dret-mem MemDelta patch/apply round-trip; page-diff correctness proptest: random before/after page buffers; diff+apply == after
dret-sched SchedPoint queue ordering; tick-budget preemption boundary single-threaded simulation; scheduler invariant I6
dret-trace TraceWriter append → TraceReader seek by EventId/Ticks; CRC detect 1-bit flip; forward-skip unknown tags unit + fuzz
dret-divergence DivergenceReport generation; bisect index math synthetic TraceFrame pairs with injected reg/tick/mem divergences
dret-gdb RSP packet encode/decode; breakpoint address arithmetic no real gdb; feed raw RSP bytes, verify response bytes

Integration Testing

  • record-replay equivalence: record a fixed binary under dret-record; replay under dret-replay; assert zero DivergenceReport events; binaries in tests/fixtures/.
  • multi-threaded fixture: tests/fixtures/race_free_mt (N=2,4 threads, mutex-serialized); record → replay; identical exit code, stdout hash, register snapshot at exit.
  • signal delivery: fixture delivering SIGALRM at known intervals; replay delivers at identical (tid, ticks); assert I8.
  • syscall coverage matrix: fixture exercises every captured syscall family (mmap, read, write, sendmsg, recvmsg, clone, futex, etc.); assert all mem_effects replayed verbatim (I4).
  • checkpoint seek: record 10 000-event trace; seek to event 5000 via nearest checkpoint + replay-forward; assert register state == original at event 5000 (I7).
  • divergence injection: deliberately corrupt one SyscallRecord.ret in trace; assert replay aborts with DivergenceReport (P6).
  • vDSO/vsyscall patch: fixture calls gettimeofday via vDSO; replay produces identical return value; assert no real clock read (I5).
  • rdtsc/cpuid trap: fixture reads TSC; replay injects recorded value; assert bit-exact (I4, Phase 10).

Differential Testing (Reference Implementation)

Comparison Target Method Pass Criterion
Mozilla rr Record same fixture binary under rr AND dret-record; compare per-syscall (nr, ret, errno) sequence Sequences match for all non-rr-extended syscalls
strace -f output Record under dret; replay-generated syscall log vs strace output from original run Same syscall sequence, same argument classes
strace mem effect For write/read/sendmsg: compare mem delta bytes Byte-identical
  • Differential suite runs in CI on a fixed Linux kernel version (pinned via nix or Docker image hash) to avoid kernel-version drift.

Property-Based Testing (proptest / arbitrary)

Property Generator Invariant
TraceFrame serde Arbitrary TraceFrame encode → decode == original
EventId strict monotone random-length event sequences each frame's EventId == prev + 1
Ticks monotone per TaskId random SchedSwitch interleavings per-task ticks non-decreasing (I2)
MemDelta idempotent random deltas over 4 KB pages apply(apply(page, delta), delta) == apply(page, delta)
Checkpoint round-trip random AddressSpace + dirty-page sets serialize → deserialize → serialize == original

Fuzzing

  • dret-trace reader: cargo-fuzz target fuzz_trace_reader; feed arbitrary bytes as events.log + data.log; assert no panic, only Err(...).
  • RSP packet parser in dret-gdb: fuzz raw packet bytes; assert no panic.
  • syscall ABI decoder: fuzz (nr, regs) pairs; assert no OOB access, no panic.
  • Coverage-guided via libfuzzer; corpus seeded from real trace files; target: 24 h continuous fuzz in CI weekly.
  • Sanitizers: ASan + UBSan on fuzz targets; Miri on pure dret-core logic.

Chaos and Fault-Injection Testing

Fault Injection Point Expected Behavior
Trace file truncated at byte N Truncate events.log mid-frame TraceReader returns last valid frame; no panic
CRC bit-flip Corrupt single byte in events.log Reader rejects frame, returns Err(Corruption)
SIGKILL recorder mid-run kill -9 supervisor PID Tracee killed (EXITKILL); trace prefix replays to last good frame (P7)
perf_event_open fails Mock ENOSYS Recorder returns Err(NoPerfCounter); no silent fallback
Tracee calls unknown syscall Inject syscall nr not in table Recorder logs UnknownSyscall event; replay aborts if not in trace
OOM during checkpoint Limit via cgroups Partial checkpoint discarded; prior checkpoint preserved

Determinism / Replay Soak Tests

  • Idempotent replay: replay same trace 100× in sequence; assert every replay produces identical DivergenceReport (empty) and identical exit state.
  • Cross-machine replay: record on host A, replay on host B (same ISA, different kernel micro-version); assert equivalence; documents portability boundary.
  • Long-running soak: record 10-minute CPU-bound fixture; replay to completion; track DivergenceReport rate (target: 0).

Conformance Validation

  • tests/conformance/ directory: curated set of fixture binaries with pre-recorded traces (committed as binary blobs).
  • CI runs dret replay against each committed trace; asserts exit code, stdout hash, no divergence.
  • Fixtures cover: single-thread, multi-thread (N=2,4,8), signal-heavy, mmap-heavy, rdtsc-heavy, futex-heavy, vDSO-calling.
  • New fixture addition protocol: record on CI host, commit trace + expected-hash file; review diff of events.log summary.

Performance Regression Testing

  • Benchmark suite in benches/ (criterion); CI runs on every merge to main.
  • Alert on ≥ 5% regression in: record overhead ratio, replay throughput (events/s), checkpoint write latency, seek-to-event latency.
  • Baseline pinned per kernel version tag; never compare across kernel versions.

Compatibility and Regression

  • Trace forward-compatibility: generate traces with versions N-1, N-2; assert current reader successfully skips unknown tags (P8).
  • Syscall table regression: on kernel upgrade, run diff against prior ABI table; new syscalls flagged for manual classification.
  • gdb RSP regression: replay + attach gdb headlessly; run reverse-step 100 script; assert final PC matches expected.

Build & Release Engineering

Toolchain

Attribute Value
Minimum Supported Rust Version (MSRV) 1.78 (const-stabilizations needed for ABI tables)
Toolchain pin rust-toolchain.toml with channel = "1.78.0"
Cross-compile targets x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu (primary); x86_64-unknown-linux-musl (static binary)
Nightly use None in production crates; nightly only in fuzz targets (feature-gated)
Clippy cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings; custom lint list in .clippy.toml
Rustfmt cargo fmt --check in CI; rustfmt.toml pinned edition = 2021
Miri Weekly CI run on dret-core and dret-trace with Miri; flags UB in unsafe blocks

Reproducible Builds

  • Cargo.lock committed; no [patch] redirections in release builds.
  • Docker build image pinned by digest (FROM rust:1.78.0@sha256:<digest>).
  • SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH set from git commit timestamp; passed to rustc via RUSTFLAGS.
  • Two independent CI workers build the same commit; assert byte-identical binaries for x86_64-unknown-linux-musl target.
  • No build.rs generating non-deterministic output; all generated files (ABI tables) checked into repo.

CI/CD Topology

PR open   → lint + fmt + test(unit+integration) + fuzz(60s) + bench(quick)
Merge main→ full test suite + bench(full) + conformance + differential + coverage report
Nightly   → 24h fuzz + soak + Miri + cross-machine replay (if infra available)
Tag vX.Y.Z→ reproducible-build check + SBOM gen + artifact sign + release upload
  • CI provider: GitHub Actions; pinned action versions by commit hash, not tag.
  • Kernel version matrix: Ubuntu 22.04 (5.15), Ubuntu 24.04 (6.8); tests must pass on both.
  • cargo-deny runs on every PR: license audit, vulnerability check, banned crate list.
  • cargo-audit runs nightly against RustSec advisory DB.

Artifact Signing and Provenance

Artifact Mechanism
Release binaries cosign sign-blob with OIDC-backed keyless signing (Sigstore)
Container images cosign sign image digest
SBOM cargo cyclonedx generates CycloneDX JSON; attached to GitHub release
Build provenance SLSA Level 2 via GitHub Actions provenance attestation (sigstore/gh-action-sigstore-python equivalent for Rust)
Checksum file SHA-256 of each artifact; signed with same key

Version Stamping

  • Version: MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH in Cargo.toml workspace; single source.
  • Git commit hash embedded at build time via build.rs reading VERGEN_GIT_SHA; exposed as dret --version.
  • Trace format version (u16 in header) incremented independently; semver-tracked in TRACE_FORMAT_VERSION constant in dret-trace.
  • API stability: dret-core types follow semver; internal crates (dret-ptrace, dret-syscall) are pub(crate) and not semver-stable.

Release Channels

Channel Cadence Stability
stable Monthly Full conformance suite pass; no open P0 bugs
beta Weekly CI green; fuzz clean; may have known minor regressions
nightly Per-commit No stability guarantee; for development use

Dependency Auditing and Supply-Chain

  • cargo-deny policy: no GPL-incompatible licenses; no yanked crates; allow list for non-Rust deps.
  • All build.rs scripts audited for network access; banned if found (deny.toml [build-scripts]).
  • Vendored vendor/ directory for offline CI builds; synced via cargo vendor on each Cargo.lock change.
  • cargo-vet for supply-chain audits of direct dependencies; audit records committed to supply-chain/.

Performance Engineering

Benchmarking Strategy

Benchmark Tool Metric Target
Syscall interception round-trip criterion ns/syscall stop (entry→exit→record) ≤ 8 µs per syscall event
Record overhead ratio wall-clock ratio vs untraced run slowdown factor 2–20× (P1); alert if > 20×
Replay throughput events replayed per second events/s ≥ 50 000 events/s for syscall-dense traces
Checkpoint write criterion ms per checkpoint ≤ 200 ms for 512 MB dirty-page set
Seek latency (checkpoint + replay-forward) criterion ms to reach target event ≤ 2 s for 100 000-event seek distance
Trace reader seek by EventId criterion µs per seek ≤ 100 µs with index
Memory snapshot delta criterion MB/s ≥ 2 GB/s for page diff on 4 KB pages

Benchmarks run on a dedicated bare-metal CI node (no VM); CPU frequency pinned; turbo off; ASLR off for stability.

Profiling Methodology

  • flamegraph workflow: cargo flamegraph --bin dret -- record <fixture> → SVG; Inferno for diff-flamegraph between versions.
  • perf stat: perf stat -e cycles,instructions,branch-misses,cache-misses on record and replay binaries; tracked per release.
  • perf record -g: on high-overhead paths; identify hot ptrace calls, waitpid spin, /proc/pid/mem reads.
  • strace on the supervisor: measure supervisor's own syscall overhead (meta-level); optimize batch ptrace.

Tracing Methodology

  • tracing crate (tokio/tracing) with tracing-subscriber; spans around: supervisor loop iteration, per-event record, checkpoint write, replay injection.
  • Log levels: ERROR (divergence, corruption), WARN (skid correction applied, unknown syscall), INFO (session start/stop, checkpoint), DEBUG (per-event), TRACE (per-ptrace-stop).
  • Structured fields: event_id, tid, ticks, nr on every span; allows post-hoc log analysis with jq.
  • Performance-trace mode: DRET_PERF_TRACE=1 enables histogram output for supervisor-loop latency; no external dependency.

Allocator Analysis

  • dhat (Valgrind) on record session: identify allocation hot spots in TraceWriter, MemDelta construction.
  • Peak heap target for recorder: ≤ 2× tracee RSS (dominated by COW page snapshots and MemDelta buffers).
  • dret-core types: all owned data via Vec/Bytes; no arena allocator by default; arena feature-flag for checkpoint bulk alloc.
  • cargo-heaptrack weekly CI job on a 500 MB-RSS fixture; alert on > 10% increase in peak allocator bytes.

Syscall Analysis (Supervisor's Own Syscalls)

  • Supervisor's own syscall cost is a first-order overhead source; profile with perf trace -p <supervisor_pid>.
  • Hot path: ptrace(PTRACE_GETREGS), ptrace(PTRACE_SETREGS), waitpid(-1), /proc/pid/mem pread; minimize per-event invocations.
  • Batch ptrace (Phase 14): buffer multiple PTRACE_POKEUSR / PTRACE_POKEDATA writes into one process_vm_writev; reduces per-event ptrace round-trips.
  • process_vm_readv / process_vm_writev preferred over repeated PTRACE_PEEKDATA for multi-word reads.

Lock Analysis

  • dret-sched serializes all threads; no concurrent lock contention in the critical record path by design (P4).
  • TraceWriter: single-writer; internal Mutex only for data.log offset counter; contention negligible.
  • perf_event_open fd per task: no cross-task locking on counter reads.
  • parking_lot::Mutex preferred over std::sync::Mutex for lower overhead in supervisor hot path.

Cache Analysis

  • TraceWriter write path: events.log is append-only; sequential writes hit L1/L2 write buffer; no cache thrashing.
  • Replay hot path: TraceReader event scan is sequential; prefetch events.log into OS page cache at session start.
  • RecordedRegs (≈ 160 bytes) fits in 3 cache lines; avoid false sharing by aligning per-task structs to 64-byte boundary.
  • MemDelta bytes buffers: allocate contiguous Vec<u8>; avoid pointer chasing per byte.

Throughput and Tail-Latency Targets

ID Metric Target Phase Gate
M1 Record overhead (general, §4.1) 2–20× wall-clock slowdown Phase 6 (bit-exact replay, 100 runs)
M2 Record overhead (syscall-heavy) ≤ 10× wall-clock Phase 2, 6
M3 Record overhead (compute-bound) ≤ 3× wall-clock Phase 4
M4 Replay overhead (general, §4.1) 5–30× wall-clock vs. native Phase 6
M5 Replay throughput ≥ 50 000 events/s Phase 6
M6 Replay-forward rate, no divergence check ≥ 200 000 events/s Phase 12
M7 Trace size / memory written (§4.1) ≤ 10× bytes written by program Phase 14 (soak + compression)
M8 Divergence detection overhead (§4.1) ≤ 5% of replay time Phase 12
M9 Checkpoint write P99 ≤ 500 ms Phase 11
M10 Seek P99 (10k-event distance) ≤ 500 ms Phase 11
M11 Skid correction budget (§4.1) ≤ 50 RCB ticks overcount Phase 4
M12 GDB reverse-step latency P50 ≤ 100 ms Phase 13

Memory profiling: weekly heaptrack + valgrind --tool=massif on a 1 GB-RSS fixture; report RSS at peak checkpoint, at session end.

Scalability Analysis

Dominant Scaling Pressures

Pressure Source Growth Rate Ceiling
Trace file size events.log + data.log O(syscall count × avg MemDelta size) Disk / compression ratio
Checkpoint size dirty-page snapshot per checkpoint O(tracee RSS × dirty-page fraction) RAM + disk I/O
Replay event fan-out per-event reg+hash comparison O(total events) CPU throughput
Thread count TaskId + per-task tick counters + register sets O(threads × events_per_thread) ptrace overhead per-task
Address-space complexity AddressSpace segment table O(mmap calls) mmap.log size; linear scan on restore

Bottleneck Map

Record path bottlenecks (in order):
  1. ptrace round-trip latency  — one stop = 2 context switches (tracee→kernel→supervisor→kernel→tracee)
  2. /proc/pid/mem pread latency — per syscall with pointer args
  3. Checkpoint dirty-page scan  — proportional to tracee RSS
  4. events.log write throughput — sequential; disk I/O bound at high event rate

Replay path bottlenecks:
  1. PTRACE_SYSEMU + inject sequence — same 2-context-switch cost as record
  2. TraceReader deserialization   — sequential read; CPU-bound at high event density
  3. Divergence hash computation   — per-event SHA over touched pages; proportional to MemDelta size
  4. Checkpoint restore I/O        — random writes to tracee pages via process_vm_writev

Horizontal Scaling

  • Record is inherently single-machine and single-supervisor; no horizontal record parallelism.
  • Replay is embarrassingly parallel across independent sessions: N replay sessions over N identical trace copies; used for parallel gdb reverse-debug clients.
  • Conformance and regression test suites: parallel replay jobs on CI; each fixture independent.
  • Trace analysis tools (dret dump, dret pack): parallelizable over events.log segments via rayon.

Vertical Scaling Limits

Resource Limit Mitigation
RAM (supervisor) Checkpoint dirty-page buffer; peak = tracee RSS × 2 Incremental checkpoint: stream pages to disk, limit in-memory buffer
Disk (trace dir) events.log + data.log grow unboundedly dret pack compression (Phase 14); configurable checkpoint interval
CPU (supervisor) Single-threaded hot path (by design, P4); scales with single-core IPC Batch ptrace calls; minimize syscall round-trips per event
File descriptors One perf_event_open fd per task; one /proc/pid/mem fd per task Limit thread count; close and reopen on task exit

Thread Count Scaling

  • Per-thread overhead: one perf_event_open fd, one ptrace-tracked task, one register set in checkpoint.
  • Tested thread counts: 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32; alert if record overhead grows super-linearly (expected: O(N) context switches per SchedSwitch).
  • Deterministic serialization (I6) means per-event cost is constant; total cost scales O(N × events) not O(N²).
  • Practical ceiling: 128 threads; beyond that, ptrace waitpid demux and fd table become measurable bottlenecks.

Trace Size Growth

  • Linear in event count; no metadata explosion.
  • Data.log growth proportional to avg MemDelta size × syscall rate; write(2) to a 4 KB buffer = 4 KB delta per event.
  • Compression (Phase 14): data.log compresses 4–10× with zstd for typical workloads (repeated page patterns).
  • Checkpoint interval tuning: longer interval → smaller trace; longer seek latency; configurable via --checkpoint-interval=N_events.
  • Index file (events.idx): one 16-byte record (EventId, file-offset) per event; 1 M events = 16 MB index; negligible.

Scheduler and Storage Growth

  • dret-sched run-queue: bounded by thread count; O(N) per SchedSwitch; not a scaling concern up to N=128.
  • mmap.log: one record per mmap/munmap/mremap/brk; typical programs issue < 10 000 mmap events per run; O(1) storage concern.
  • TraceReader index: built lazily on first seek; rebuilt from events.log in O(events) if lost; cached in memory for session duration.
  • Checkpoint directory (ckpt/): one subdirectory per checkpoint; number of checkpoints = total_events / checkpoint_interval; 1 M events at interval 10 000 = 100 checkpoints; manageable.

Metadata Scaling

  • AddressSpace segment table: segment count bounded by kernel vm.max_map_count (default 65 530); linear scan on restore acceptable.
  • TaskId namespace: u32; supports 4 × 10⁹ tasks per trace; not a ceiling in practice.
  • EventId namespace: u64; supports 1.8 × 10¹⁹ events; effectively unbounded.
  • SyscallRecord mem_effects: Vec<MemDelta>: bounded by pointer-arg count per syscall (≤ 6 args); no growth concern.

Coordination Overhead

  • Zero cross-task coordination during replay (I6): single logical CPU eliminates all lock/barrier overhead.
  • Supervisor → tracee coordination: pure ptrace; no shared memory, no futex in supervisor hot path.
  • dret-gdb session: one gdb client per replay session; no multi-client coordination; multiple clients require multiple independent replay sessions.
  • Trace writer coordination: single writer; no reader/writer lock; readers operate only on completed sessions (read-only after record ends, per P2).

Mental Models & First Principles

MM1. The tracee is a deterministic function of its inputs. A program's execution is fully determined by: (1) its initial memory image, (2) the sequence of syscall return values, (3) signal delivery order, (4) hardware instruction trap results (rdtsc/CPUID). Record exactly these four categories and replay is exact. The bug class you write without this model: attempting to record every instruction executed, which is unnecessary and impractical. Resolving §: §6 (replay engine enforces this — only syscall results + signals + trap values are injected; no instruction-level logging needed).

MM2. Retired conditional branches are the only portable, per-task logical clock. Wall time varies with frequency scaling; rdtsc varies with core migration; cycle counts vary with cache misses. Retired conditional branches (RCB) are determined purely by the program's control flow, which is itself determined by MM1's four inputs. Two recordings of the same deterministic program produce identical RCB sequences. Without this model: using wall-clock preemption, which makes SchedSwitch events non-reproducible. Resolving §: Phase 4 (PerfCounter design), P5.

MM3. Serialization is cheaper than synchronization logging. Recording the exact memory access order of N concurrent threads requires O(N²) synchronization events. Running at most one thread at a time (single logical CPU) makes all atomics and memory operations sequentially consistent by construction, requiring zero synchronization recording. The tradeoff: replay does not preserve multi-core throughput. Without this model: attempting to record all memory ordering barriers, which is the approach of Dunlap et al. 2008 at VM level and is impractical in user space. Resolving §: §P4, Phase 7 (scheduler design), Phase 8 (atomics argument).

MM4. A checkpoint is the derivative, not the integral, of state. A full memory snapshot at every event is O(RSS) per event. A dirty-page delta since the last checkpoint is O(working-set churn). Checkpoints should store only what changed. Without this model: taking full snapshots leads to O(RSS × events) storage, which is infeasible for any workload beyond a few seconds. Resolving §: Phase 5 (page-diff delta), Phase 11 (checkpoint design), Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model.

MM5. The trace is the log; replay is the query. An append-only trace with CRC-framed, EventId-indexed records behaves exactly like a write-ahead log: writes are sequential (fast), reads are random-access by EventId (fast via index), and crash leaves a replayable prefix. Without this model: designing the trace as a mutable database (random writes) eliminates crash-safety and replay-from-prefix guarantees. Resolving §: §P7, §P8, Phase 3 (trace container design).

MM6. Divergence is a recorder bug, not a replayer failure. If replay produces a state different from record, the replayer faithfully executed the injected trace — the trace simply does not match what the original program did. This means divergence always points to a gap in the recorder's coverage of nondeterminism sources. Without this model: treating divergence as a replay engine bug leads to adding workarounds in the replayer that mask real recorder gaps. Resolving §: §P6, Phase 12 (divergence detection, bisect tooling).

MM7. The observer effect is the enemy; hardware counters are the antidote. Software instrumentation (LD_PRELOAD hooks, binary rewriting, in-process timers) changes what the program executes, making the recording itself nondeterministic. Hardware PMU counters and ptrace operate outside the tracee's instruction stream. Without this model: instrumenting the tracee to count its own branches introduces new branches, invalidating the count. Resolving §: §P3, ADR-10, ADR-13, Phase 4 (perf_event_open design).

MM8. Reverse execution is forward execution from the nearest earlier checkpoint. There is no instruction-reverse hardware. "Reverse-step" means: find the last checkpoint before the target event, replay forward to (target − 1). The cost is O(distance from checkpoint), not O(distance from trace start). Without this model: attempting to un-apply syscall side effects in reverse, which is impossible for I/O syscalls. Resolving §: Phase 11 (seek algorithm), Phase 13 (reverse-step/continue design), §8.9 (checkpoint model).

MM9. The TCB size determines the attack surface. Every line of code that runs with elevated privilege or accesses the tracee's memory is part of the trusted computing base. dret-ptrace + dret-mem form the TCB; every other crate can be untrusted. Minimizing the TCB means a tracee exploit can corrupt only what crosses the TCB boundary. Without this model: growing the TCB with convenience features (e.g., parsing trace files in the supervisor) creates unnecessary attack surface. Resolving §: §P9, Threat Model (T1, T5, T9).

MM10. Forward compatibility is a write-time contract, not a read-time feature. A trace written today must be readable by a reader built next year. This is only possible if the writer commits to never changing the meaning of existing frame tags, and the reader commits to skipping unknown tags by length. Both sides of the contract must hold. Without this model: adding fields to existing frame types causes older readers to misparse subsequent fields, breaking all existing traces. Resolving §: §P8, Phase 3 (tag-skip design), Compatibility & Versioning.

Comparative Architecture Table

Dimension dret (this system) rr (Mozilla) Pernosco gVisor Antithesis WinDbg TTD (Microsoft) Undo LiveRecorder
Recording mechanism ptrace + perf_event_open RCB ptrace + perf_event_open RCB rr-based Kernel syscall interception (Sentry) Deterministic hypervisor (custom VMM) Windows kernel driver (ring-0 ETW) ptrace + hardware breakpoints
Nondeterminism sources captured syscalls, signals, rdtsc/CPUID, futex order syscalls, signals, rdtsc/CPUID, futex order same as rr syscalls only (kernel boundary) all hardware nondeterminism below VM all (instruction grain) syscalls, signals, shared memory
Recording overhead 2–20× 1.5–2× (production) ~2× via rr ~10–50× (full syscall interception) near-zero (deterministic by construction) 2–5× (kernel driver) 2–10×
Replay fidelity bit-exact (x86-64 Linux) bit-exact (x86-64 Linux) bit-exact not designed for replay bit-exact across arbitrary hardware bit-exact (Windows only) bit-exact (Linux)
Checkpointing periodic dirty-page delta (Phase 11) periodic COW snapshot dense indexed (cloud storage) not a replay system continuous (VM snapshot) periodic (ETW log segments) periodic + on-demand
Multicore handling single logical CPU (serialization) single logical CPU same as rr native multicore (no replay guarantee) deterministic virtual multicore full multicore replay single-thread serialization (optional SMP)
Platform Linux x86-64 (user-space only) Linux x86-64, ARM Cloud (Linux) Linux (any arch via Sentry) Any OS/arch via hypervisor Windows only Linux x86-64, ARM
Reverse execution checkpoint + replay-forward (Phase 13) checkpoint + replay-forward omniscient query (indexed) not supported full reverse supported checkpoint + replay-forward checkpoint + replay-forward
Open source yes (Apache/MIT, this design) yes (MPL-2.0) no (commercial) yes (Apache-2.0) no (commercial) no (proprietary) no (commercial)

rr (Mozilla): The direct ancestor of dret. rr made the critical insight that retiring one logical CPU during replay eliminates the need to record memory ordering — all concurrent accesses become sequentially consistent by construction. rr uses the same RCB clock mechanism. Where rr wins: it is production-hardened over a decade, supports a wider syscall surface, and has a larger conformance corpus. dret's niche over rr: it is designed to be read and extended, uses Rust's type system to enforce invariants at compile time, and has a layered crate architecture that makes subsystem replacement straightforward.

Pernosco: Built on rr, Pernosco adds a cloud-hosted omniscient debugger: rather than replaying forward on demand, it indexes the entire execution into a queryable database. Where Pernosco wins: O(1) queries to "what called this function" or "when was this variable last written" without replay-forward latency. The trade-off: it requires uploading the trace to a proprietary cloud service, making it unsuitable for air-gapped security research or proprietary codebases. dret's niche: local-first, open, no cloud dependency.

gVisor (Google): gVisor intercepts Linux syscalls at the kernel boundary via a user-space kernel (Sentry), achieving strong isolation. It captures all nondeterminism at the syscall layer. Where gVisor wins: native multicore workloads, strong sandboxing, no ptrace overhead per-stop. The trade-off: gVisor is a sandboxing tool, not a replay system; it does not record futex ordering or signal delivery ticks, making deterministic replay impossible for multi-threaded programs. dret's niche: full multi-thread replay fidelity.

Antithesis: Antithesis runs the entire system under a custom deterministic hypervisor, making all hardware nondeterminism disappear by construction. Where Antithesis wins: zero recording overhead (the system is always deterministic), full multicore replay, and the ability to test distributed systems across multiple VMs. The trade-off: it requires running inside its custom VMM, which is not generally available and cannot trace arbitrary Linux processes on a developer workstation. dret's niche: records ordinary processes on any Linux x86-64 machine without infrastructure changes.

WinDbg Time Travel Debugging (Microsoft): TTD uses a Windows kernel driver (ETW + iDNA) to record at instruction grain, not syscall grain. Where TTD wins: it captures intra-syscall nondeterminism (e.g., shared memory written by another process mid-syscall) and supports full Windows kernel-mode debugging. The trade-off: kernel driver requirement means privileged installation; Windows-only; not open source; the Bhansali 2006 paper documents the original iDNA approach that TTD builds on. dret's niche: user-space only, no privileged installation, Linux.

Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model

All numbers derived from the System Constraints table (§4.1) and a baseline workload: 1 M syscalls, 100 MB total data written, 256 MB tracee RSS, 50 checkpoints, 4 threads.

1. events.log bandwidth:

events.log frame size = 192 B (§4.1)
syscall rate = 100 000 syscalls/s (I/O-intensive workload assumption)
events.log write rate = 192 B × 100 000 /s = 19.2 MB/s
For 1 M syscall events: 192 B × 1 000 000 = 192 MB

2. data.log bandwidth (mem effects):

average mem effect per syscall = 100 MB / 1 000 000 = 100 B/syscall
data.log write rate = 100 B × 100 000 /s = 10 MB/s
For 1 M syscalls: 100 MB total

3. Checkpoint dirty-page storage:

checkpoint interval = 10 M RCB ticks ≈ 50 000 events (§4.1 default)
checkpoints = 1 000 000 / 50 000 = 20 checkpoints
dirty-page fraction per interval = 10% of RSS (typical write-intensive workload)
dirty bytes per checkpoint = 256 MB × 0.10 = 25.6 MB
total checkpoint storage = 20 × 25.6 MB = 512 MB

4. Total trace storage:

events.log    = 192 MB
data.log      = 100 MB
mmap.log      ≈ 1 MB  (few thousand mmap events × 128 B)
checkpoints   = 512 MB
Total         = 805 MB
Storage amplification = 805 MB / 100 MB = 8× (within ≤10× target in §4.1)

5. Checkpoint write time:

dirty bytes per checkpoint = 25.6 MB
/proc/pid/mem pread throughput ≈ 1 GB/s (§4.1 benchmark)
checkpoint write time = 25.6 MB / 1 GB/s = 25.6 ms  (well within 200 ms target)

6. Seek latency (reverse-continue worst case):

checkpoint interval = 50 000 events
replay throughput = 50 000 events/s (§4.1 replay overhead target)
seek forward distance = 50 000 events
replay-forward time = 50 000 / 50 000 = 1.0 s
checkpoint restore time = 25.6 ms
total seek latency = 1.0 s + 0.026 s ≈ 1.03 s  (within ≤2 s target in §4.1)

7. Supervisor RSS ceiling:

In-flight MemDelta buffer = 64 KB (max, one large read syscall buffer)
Per-task state = 1 KB × 4 threads = 4 KB
Checkpoint page buffer = 25.6 MB peak (streamed to disk, not retained)
Index (event.idx) = 1 000 000 × 16 B = 16 MB
Peak supervisor RSS ≈ 16 MB + 25.6 MB + small buffers ≈ 45 MB  (well within 256 MB target)

8. perf_event_open fd consumption:

perf fds = 1 per task = 4 fds (4 threads)
/proc/pid/mem fds = 1 per task = 4 fds
ptrace = no fd
Total extra fds = 8 (trivial vs. default limit of 1024)

9. ptrace round-trip overhead:

ptrace round-trip latency = 1–3 µs/syscall (§4.1)
at 100 000 syscalls/s: overhead = 100 000 × 3 µs = 300 ms/s = 30% of wall time
plus mem capture: 100 000 × 2 µs = 200 ms/s
Total instrumentation overhead ≈ 50% of CPU → 2× wall-clock (lower bound)
Combined with single-step + scheduler: 2–20× (§4.1 recorded target range)

10. Divergence hash overhead:

pages touched per syscall = 1 (average for simple read/write)
Blake3 hash rate = ~10 GB/s (AVX-512 SIMD; Phase 12 uses xxHash3 at similar speed)
4 KB page hash time = 4 096 B / 10 GB/s = 0.4 µs per page
at 100 000 events/s with page hashing: 0.4 µs × 100 000 = 40 ms/s overhead = 4%
Well within ≤5% divergence detection overhead target (§4.1)

Hard Questions (Self-Examination)

Each question requires synthesis of ≥2 sections. Resolving § cited for each.

  1. Why does single-CPU serialization (P4, Phase 7) make futex-based atomics deterministic without recording memory barriers? What invariant guarantees this, and where would it break? (Phase 8, §7 I6, MM3)

  2. If the RCB counter has a skid of ±5 branches on a specific CPU model, what is the maximum error in delivery_ticks (Phase 9) for an async signal? How does dret handle this, and what happens if the skid exceeds the configured SKID_THRESHOLD? (Phase 4, Phase 9, §4.1)

  3. A trace records a program that calls rdtsc in a tight loop. Phase 10 injects recorded TSC values. The program uses the TSC difference to busywait 100 ns. Why might the replay tracee hang forever, and what does dret do about it? (Phase 10, §10.6 pitfalls, MM2)

  4. Explain the exact sequence of events when dret-replay calls reverse-continue and a watchpoint is on a heap address that was written by a different thread than the one currently being debugged. How many checkpoint restore + replay-forward cycles may be required? (Phase 11, Phase 13, §8.9, §8.14)

  5. A conformance corpus test fails: replay of a 4-thread program produces DivergenceReport{kind: TickMismatch} at event 50,000. List the five most likely root causes in order of probability, and for each, name the diagnostic tool and the specific trace section that would rule it out or confirm it. (Phase 12, Phase 4, Phase 7, Failure Modes & Recovery F5, F6)

  6. Why must the trace container (Phase 3) keep events.log uncompressed but may compress data.log? What would break if events.log were zstd-compressed, and what would break if data.log were not compressed at all? (Phase 3, Phase 14, ADR-26, Scalability Analysis)

  7. An operator records a program that forks 500 short-lived threads, each doing 1 syscall then exiting. The record session runs out of file descriptors. Explain exactly which fds are consumed, what the theoretical ceiling is, and how Phase 8 mitigates this. (Phase 8, §4.1 fd budget, §8.7 perf fd leak pitfall)

  8. Pernosco (Comparative Architecture Table) achieves O(1) query latency to "when was this variable last written" while dret requires O(distance) replay-forward. What architectural change would be required to make dret approach Pernosco's query latency, and what would the storage cost be? (Phase 11, Phase 13, Comparative Architecture Table, Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model)

  9. Why is P3 (observe, do not perturb) violated by injecting a trampoline into tracee .text for branch counting, and why is it NOT violated by patching the vDSO in Phase 10? What specific property distinguishes the two cases? (§P3, Phase 10, §10.6 pitfalls, Threat Model T1)

  10. The Bhansali 2006 (iDNA/TTD) approach records at instruction grain while dret records at syscall grain. For a program that does 1 M iterations of a tight CPU-bound loop with no syscalls, what does dret's trace contain? What does TTD's trace contain? Which is correct for replay and why? (Phase 2, Comparative Architecture Table, MM1)

  11. Explain why TraceFrame.data_ref == DataRef(0) for a syscall like getpid() but not for read(). Walk through the exact sequence of dret-syscall decisions that produces this difference, citing the specific invariant that would be violated if getpid() did emit a MemDelta. (Phase 2, §11.5, §8.6, I4)

  12. A developer uses dret-gdb to reverse-continue to find who wrote to address 0x7fff1000. The watchpoint fires at event 42,000. The checkpoint nearest before 42,000 is at event 40,000. The developer then issues another reverse-continue to find the previous write. Explain the full algorithmic sequence including number of replay passes and why the bisection terminates. (Phase 11, Phase 13, §8.9, §8.14)

  13. dret-sched records SchedSwitch events with (from_tid, to_tid, at_ticks). On replay, what happens if the replay tracee's RCB counter reads 999 when the recorded SchedSwitch.at_ticks == 1000? Name the exact invariant that governs this, the detection mechanism, and the recovery path. (Phase 7, §7 I6, Phase 12, MM2)

  14. Explain why mmap events are stored in mmap.log (Phase 3) rather than being recorded as normal SyscallRecord entries in events.log. What seeking operation requires mmap.log to be a separate, independently-seekable structure? (Phase 3, Phase 11, §8.4, §8.10)

  15. A user's trace contains a SIGKILL delivered to the tracee. What is in the trace at that point? What does dret replay do differently for SIGKILL vs. SIGINT? Why is there no single-step-to-delivery-tick needed for SIGKILL? (Phase 9, §8.7, §9.6, §9.8)

  16. Walk through the Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model calculation for a program with 10 M syscalls, 1 GB total memory writes, and a checkpoint interval of 100 000 events. Would the total trace exceed the ≤10× storage amplification target? Show the arithmetic. (§4.1, Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model, Scalability Analysis)

  17. A new nondeterminism source is discovered: the Linux kernel sometimes returns different errno values for EINTR vs. ERESTARTSYS for the same syscall restart. What existing dret mechanism captures this, what invariant would detect a gap, and what ADR decision would change if it could not be captured via ptrace? (Phase 2, §2.6 pitfalls, I4, ADR-12)

  18. Why does dret store raw 128-byte siginfo_t bytes rather than parsing the struct? What ABI guarantee does this rely on, and under what condition would a cross-kernel-version replay fail despite this strategy? (Phase 9, §9.7 perf concerns, Compatibility & Versioning)

  19. The Scalability Analysis shows replay throughput is bounded by PTRACE_SYSEMU round-trips. If you wanted to achieve 10× higher replay throughput (500 000 events/s), name two fundamentally different architectural changes you could make, which back-matter section predicts the tradeoffs, and which ADR would need to be revisited. (Scalability Analysis, Alternative Architectures (Roads Not Taken), ADR-12)

  20. A Checkpoint (Phase 11) contains a fd_table entry for a pipe fd that the tracee had open. On restore, the supervisor must recreate this pipe. Describe the exact mechanism, what information is stored in FdRecord, and why a socket fd (AF_INET) cannot be restored the same way. (Phase 11, §8.9, §11.6, §11.8)

Alternative Architectures (Roads Not Taken)

Alt-1: Hardware-Assisted Recording (Intel Processor Trace)

Tracee process
    │
    │ CPU PT hardware collects compressed
    │ branch trace at near-zero overhead
    ▼
Intel PT ring buffer (kernel-managed)
    │
    ▼
dret-pt-decoder (libipt-based):
  branch trace → instruction stream reconstruction
  → syscall positions derived from branch sequence
    │
    ▼
Trace store (instruction-grain, not syscall-grain)

Regime where it wins: Programs that are CPU-bound with very few syscalls but much intra-loop nondeterminism (e.g., a program that reads from a hardware-mapped memory region, uses inline assembly, or relies on CPU feature detection inside tight loops). Intel PT captures every branch taken, not just syscall boundaries, so it can replay arbitrary control-flow divergence without per-syscall stops.

Real system: Intel PT-based replay is prototyped in academic systems (e.g., PinPlay, and partially in rr's --chaos mode). QEMU's record/replay uses a similar instruction-grain approach via the TCG translator.

Why rejected for dret: Intel PT requires post-processing the compressed PT stream to decode branch traces, which is complex and requires libipt (C library). More importantly, PT data does not capture syscall return values — it captures which branches were taken, not what the kernel returned. A separate syscall-capture mechanism is still required, making it more complex than the ptrace approach without eliminating the need for ptrace. Additionally, PT ring buffers overflow quickly for long recordings (typical ring = 4–64 MB), requiring frequent snapshot + decode cycles that add their own complexity.

Alt-2: VM-Level Deterministic Execution (Hypervisor Record/Replay)

Physical hardware
    │
    ▼
Deterministic hypervisor (e.g., QEMU TCG, Antithesis VMM)
  - intercepts all I/O, interrupts, memory DMA
  - records all hardware nondeterminism at VM boundary
    │
    ▼
Guest OS + tracee process
  (unmodified; no ptrace; no PMU configuration)
    │
    ▼
Replay: re-execute VMM with recorded hardware events

Regime where it wins: Full-system replay of multi-machine distributed systems, kernel-mode bugs, driver interactions, and DMA-triggered nondeterminism. Antithesis uses this approach to test distributed databases and infrastructure software where the nondeterminism crosses multiple processes, kernel subsystems, and network stacks simultaneously. QEMU's built-in record/replay (enabled with -icount mode) uses the TCG translator to make execution deterministic at instruction grain.

Real system: Antithesis (commercial VMM), QEMU record/replay (-icount det,shift=N -record/-replay), ReVirt (King & Chen 2002).

Why rejected for dret: Requires either a custom hypervisor (high engineering cost; not deployable on arbitrary machines) or a software interpreter like TCG (10–100× overhead, negating the performance advantage of hardware execution). dret targets developer workstations with hardware-native execution speed; hypervisor-level recording is only feasible in controlled lab or cloud environments. Additionally, VM-level replay produces traces tied to a specific VM image, not a portable per-process trace.

Alt-3: Deterministic-Scheduler-Only (No Syscall Capture)

Tracee process (multiple threads)
    │
    ▼
Scheduler interceptor:
  - forces deterministic thread interleaving via sched_setaffinity
  - records SchedSwitch sequence
  - does NOT record syscall return values
    │
    ▼
Replay: re-execute with identical SchedSwitch sequence
  (real syscalls execute against the live kernel)

Regime where it wins: Programs whose nondeterminism is entirely due to thread interleaving (data races, lock ordering) and not due to syscall return values varying across runs. A researcher investigating a Heisenbug caused purely by a specific thread-scheduling sequence can use this approach to stabilize the interleaving without capturing any syscall data. This is the approach taken by tools like CHESS (Microsoft Research) for lock-order testing.

Real system: CHESS (Microsoft Research, Musuvathi & Qadeer 2008), partial implementations in sanitizer-mode runtimes (TSan's schedule stabilization).

Why rejected for dret: Syscall return values are a primary source of nondeterminism for I/O-bound programs (read returns different bytes, gettimeofday returns wall time, etc.). A scheduler-only approach produces replay-instability for any program that reads from files, sockets, or the clock. dret's design requirement is bit-exact replay across machines and time, which is impossible without capturing syscall return values. Scheduler-only replay is useful as a debugging aid but not as a trace-and-replay archival system.

Alt-4: Binary Instrumentation (DynamoRIO / Pin)

Tracee binary (.elf)
    │
    ▼
Dynamic binary translator (DynamoRIO / Pin):
  - recompiles basic blocks on-the-fly
  - inserts instrumentation callbacks at syscall sites,
    rdtsc instructions, CPUID, memory accesses
    │
    ▼
Instrumentation tool records:
  - syscall nr, args, ret
  - rdtsc values
  - memory writes (optional)
    │
    ▼
Trace + replay via re-instrumented execution

Regime where it wins: Cross-platform (Linux and macOS and Windows), no kernel-specific interfaces required, fine-grained control over exactly what is instrumented (e.g., only instrument memory writes to a specific address range). The iDNA/TTD approach (Bhansali 2006) is essentially this model deployed with a Windows kernel driver for performance. Pin-based record/replay tools like PinPlay (Intel) use this architecture.

Real system: PinPlay (Intel), iDNA (Microsoft, Bhansali 2006), Dr. Memory (DynamoRIO-based).

Why rejected for dret: DynamoRIO and Pin introduce nondeterminism of their own: the JIT recompiler's behavior may vary between record and replay (different code cache layout, different JIT decisions for hot paths), which is P3 violation. More critically, binary translation adds 5–50× overhead even without any instrumentation, making the combined overhead prohibitive. ptrace + perf_event_open achieves the same syscall interception at lower overhead because it is handled at the OS boundary, not by recompiling the binary. dret explicitly chooses the observe-not-perturb path (P3), which rules out binary instrumentation.

Deferred Features Register

Feature Anchor (PhaseDeferred ref) Building Phase Un-defer Trigger
Kernel-assisted recording via eBPF ring buffer §1.10 PhaseDeferred("Phase 14") Phase 14 (hardening) eBPF CO-RE support for per-task syscall ring buffers is stable in kernel ≥ 6.0 and perf overhead is measured < 10% vs. ptrace path; requires port of dret-record to eBPF consumer mode.
Multi-machine distributed trace correlation §1.10 PhaseDeferred("Phase 14") Phase 14 (hardening) A distributed tracing layer (OpenTelemetry span propagation) is added that correlates per-machine dret traces by shared request ID; requires dret-trace format extension for cross-machine EventId namespacing.
ARM64 / RISC-V architecture support §1.10 PhaseDeferred("Phase 14") Phase 14 (hardening) x86-64 conformance corpus achieves zero divergence across 50 programs; dret-core types are ISA-agnostic; dret-ptrace and dret-time require new backends for ARM64 PMU (ARMV8_PMUV3_PERFCTR_BR_RETIRED) and RISC-V Zihpm.
Automatic source-line mapping in divergence reports (DWARF) §1.10 PhaseDeferred("Phase 13") Phase 13 (gdbserver) Phase 13 gdbserver stub is complete; DWARF v5 unwinding (§5 RFCs: DWARF v5 §6.4) is added to dret-divergence to map diverging EventId → source file:line for human-readable reports.
JIT-compiled language support (V8, JVM) §1.10 PhaseDeferred("Phase 14") Phase 14 (hardening) A JIT-pinning mechanism is defined: JIT must be configured to use a deterministic code generation seed and the JIT code cache must be snapshotted at Checkpoint; requires cooperation from the JIT runtime (not possible without runtime modifications).

Glossary

Alphabetical. Each term defined at implementation precision. References to crates, invariants, and principles are parenthetical. Terms covering implementation behavior are marked with the crate that owns them.

Core Execution & Tracing Terms

Term Definition
AddressSpace Recorded snapshot of /proc/pid/maps: segment list (addr, len, perms, offset, dev, ino, path), mmap provenance (file-backed / anonymous / stack / vdso / vsyscall), current brk value. Must be identical at event E in record and replay (I10). Managed by dret-mem/maps.rs; updated on every mmap/munmap/mremap/brk intercept.
arch_prctl Linux syscall (ARCH_SET_FS / ARCH_SET_GS) that sets fs_base/gs_base for TLS. Intercepted by dret-record; recorded as a SyscallRecord; replayed by dret-replay/injector to restore TLS layout.
async signal Signal delivered by the kernel at an arbitrary execution point, unrelated to the tracee's current instruction (e.g., SIGINT, SIGALRM, SIGCHLD). Delivery tick is recorded in SignalRecord.delivery_ticks; on replay the signal is injected at that exact tick via PTRACE_SETSIGINFO (I8). Contrast: sync signal.
brk Program break: lowest address above the heap data segment, controlled by brk(2)/sbrk(3). Tracked as part of AddressSpace. Changes intercepted and replayed deterministically; divergence in brk produces an I10 violation.
build-id ELF .note.gnu.build-id section: a hash uniquely identifying a binary. Recorded in the trace pack (Phase 14) to verify that replay uses the same binary as was recorded; mismatch is a fatal error before replay starts.
Checkpoint Self-consistent snapshot anchored at a precise (EventId, Ticks) boundary (I7): per-task RecordedRegs, dirty-page table (Vec<(MemRange, PageRef)>), fd table, mmap table, brk. Written to ckpt/ by checkpoint_store.rs. Enables fast seek in Phase 11; enables reverse execution in Phase 13.
COW snapshot Copy-on-write page snapshot: pages read from /proc/pid/mem at checkpoint time. Only pages modified since the prior checkpoint are stored as MemDelta entries. Full baseline snapshot is taken at trace start.
cpuid x86 CPUID instruction; returns CPU feature flags and topology that vary across machines. Trapped by seccomp-BPF (SECCOMP_RET_TRACE) or CR4.TSD; recorded value injected on replay to ensure portability across CPU generations (Phase 10, P3).
DataRef(u64) Byte offset into data.log pointing to the variable-length payload for a given TraceFrame: mem_effects bytes, si_info, raw page data. Enables constant-size TraceFrame records with arbitrarily large associated data.
divergence Any mismatch between replay and recorded state at a given EventId: register values (RegMismatch), tick count (TickMismatch), or memory-page hash (MemHashMismatch). Any divergence triggers a DivergenceReport and immediate abort (P6, I9). Treated as a recorder or replayer bug, never silently continued.
DivergenceKind Enum: RegMismatch (specific register differs), TickMismatch (RCB count differs), MemHashMismatch (page content differs at expected address). Embedded in DivergenceReport for triage.
DivergenceReport { event_id: EventId, tid: TaskId, kind: DivergenceKind, expected: u64, actual: u64 }. First divergence wins; printed to stderr; replay session returns Err.
EXITKILL PTRACE_O_EXITKILL option: if the tracer dies, all traced children are immediately killed. Ensures no tracee escapes unsupervised; mandatory for P7 and P9. Set during Phase 1 supervisor initialization.
EventId(u64) Monotonically increasing sequence number assigned to every TraceFrame, starting at 1. Gaps indicate corruption or truncation (I1). Primary key for TraceReader::seek_by_event_id.
EventKind Enum discriminant on TraceFrame: Syscall, Signal(async|sync), SchedSwitch, RdtscTrap, CpuidTrap, PatchVsyscall, Exec, Exit, Checkpoint. Determines which record type is stored in data.log at DataRef.
fd table Per-process table of open file descriptors (number → kind, flags, path/pipe-id/socket-id). Captured in every Checkpoint; used during replay seek to restore file descriptor state so subsequent syscalls observe correct fds.
frame tag A 16-bit type field in each events.log frame header enabling forward-compatible extension (P8). Unknown tags are skipped by older readers without error; new fields are appended within an existing tag's payload.
fs_base / gs_base x86-64 segment base registers encoding thread-local storage (TLS) pointer (Linux uses fs_base). Captured in RecordedRegs at every stop; restored on context switch during replay via PTRACE_SETREGSET REGSET_NT_X86_XSTATE.
futex Fast user-space mutex; kernel wait/wake via futex(2). Ordering of futex_wake and futex_wait across threads is non-deterministic on SMP hardware; serialized by dret-sched to one logical CPU during record, producing deterministic SchedSwitch ordering replayed exactly (Phase 8).
Heisenbug A bug that disappears or changes behavior when observed. Avoided by P3: the recorder uses hardware RCB counters rather than software instrumentation, so the tracee runs the same instruction stream regardless of recording.
logical CPU The single serialized execution context enforced by dret-sched. sched_setaffinity pins the tracee to one physical CPU; only one TaskId has ptrace-continue permission at any instant (I6, P4). Eliminates true concurrency nondeterminism without requiring VM-level virtualization.
MemDelta { range: MemRange, bytes_ref: DataRef } — the bytes a syscall wrote into tracee address space as a side effect (e.g., read(2) filling a buffer, recvmsg(2) writing data + cmsg). Replayed by pwriting the exact bytes at the exact address via /proc/pid/mem (I4).
MemRange { addr: u64, len: u64 } — a contiguous half-open address interval [addr, addr+len) in tracee virtual address space. Used in MemDelta, Checkpoint dirty-page list, and AddressSpace segment descriptors.
orig_rax The original syscall number placed in rax before kernel entry, preserved in pt_regs.orig_ax. After syscall return, rax holds the return value and orig_rax holds the number. Read via PTRACE_GETREGSET at syscall-exit stop; required to correlate entry and exit stops.
overcount / skid PMU hardware fires the overflow interrupt a small number of branches after the exact counter threshold due to instruction pipeline depth. The skid is bounded (typically 1–8 branches on modern Intel); dret-time corrects by single-stepping backward to the precise tick boundary.
page-diff delta Between two consecutive checkpoints, only pages dirtied by the tracee change. dret-mem detects dirty pages via /proc/pid/pagemap soft-dirty bits (or userfaultfd) and records only those, keeping checkpoint size proportional to working-set churn rather than total heap size.
PageRef Offset into ckpt/pages.bin for the raw 4096-byte page contents stored in a Checkpoint. Analogous to DataRef for data.log; avoids embedding page data inline in the checkpoint record.
perf_event_open(2) Linux kernel interface for hardware performance counters. dret-time opens PERF_COUNT_HW_BRANCH_INSTRUCTIONS (Intel: retired conditional branches; AMD: equivalent PMU event) per tracked task. Configured with pinned=1, exclude_kernel=1, exclude_hv=1 to count only user-space branches.
PTRACE_SYSEMU ptrace request that stops the tracee at syscall entry and does NOT execute the syscall in the kernel. Used exclusively by dret-replay to suppress all real syscall side-effects (I5). The replayer injects the recorded return value and writes recorded mem_effects into tracee memory.
RCB (retired conditional branch) The hardware event used as the deterministic logical clock (Ticks). Conditional branches retire at the same count for a given deterministic instruction sequence regardless of wall time, CPU frequency, or cache state — making counts reproducible across replay runs on the same ISA.
RecordedRegs Canonical x86-64 register snapshot: { rax, rbx, rcx, rdx, rsi, rdi, rbp, rsp, r8–r15, rip, rflags, orig_rax, fs_base, gs_base }. Stored in every TraceFrame. Compared verbatim at each replay event (I9); any byte difference triggers DivergenceKind::RegMismatch.
RecordSession Top-level handle for a recording run. Owns: Tracer (dret-ptrace), TraceWriter (dret-trace), one TracedTask per thread, perf_event fds, CheckpointTrigger. Drives the supervisor stop-dispatch loop until all tasks exit.
ReplaySession Top-level handle for a replay run. Owns: TraceReader, ptrace handles for re-executed tracee, DivergenceChecker, optional GdbServer. Drives the replay stop-dispatch loop in recorded EventId order.
RSP (gdb Remote Serial Protocol) Wire protocol spoken by gdbserver over TCP (typically port 1234). dret-gdb implements: g/G (read/write all regs), m/M (read/write mem), c/s (continue/step), bc/bs (reverse-continue/reverse-step), Z0/z0 (breakpoint insert/remove), Z2/z2 (write watchpoint insert/remove).
SchedPoint { tid: TaskId, ticks: Ticks } — a deterministic preemption boundary recorded in the trace. The scheduler preempts the running task when its RCB count reaches the next SchedPoint.ticks for another task. Replayed by dret-sched to produce identical context-switch sequence.
SchedSwitch EventKind::SchedSwitch variant. Data: { from_tid, to_tid, from_ticks, to_ticks }. Written to events.log on every context switch during record; consumed by dret-sched during replay to gate which task is allowed to proceed.
seccomp-BPF Secure computing mode with BPF filter attached to the tracee. Used to intercept rdtsc/rdtscp/cpuid and deny dangerous syscalls with SECCOMP_RET_TRACE, delivering a ptrace-stop to the supervisor without requiring per-instruction PTRACE_SYSCALL overhead. Installed by dret-record before tracee exec.
si_code / si_info siginfo_t fields: si_code distinguishes signal origin (SI_USER, SI_KERNEL, SI_TKILL, SEGV_MAPERR, FPE_INTDIV, …). Entire siginfo_t (128 bytes) recorded in SignalRecord.si_info; replayed via PTRACE_SETSIGINFO so the tracee's signal handler sees the exact same siginfo as during recording.
SignalRecord { signo: i32, si_code: i32, deterministic: bool, delivery_ticks: Ticks, si_info: [u8; 128] }. deterministic=true for sync signals (fault-induced); false for async. Stored in data.log at DataRef.
skid — see overcount / skid
supervisor The tracer process running dret-record or dret-replay. The trusted computing base (P9). Observes and controls the tracee exclusively via ptrace and /proc/pid/mem. Owns all recorded state; arbitrates all nondeterminism. Must not crash after trace start (P7).
sync signal Signal caused directly by the tracee's current instruction: SIGSEGV (invalid memory access), SIGFPE (arithmetic fault), SIGBUS (alignment / bus error), SIGTRAP (breakpoint/watchpoint). Deterministic because the same instruction sequence produces the same fault; recorded for completeness and si_info fidelity.
SyscallRecord { nr: u32, args: [u64; 6], ret: i64, errno: i32, mem_effects: Vec<MemDelta> }. The complete record of one syscall execution: number, all arguments, return value, errno, and every byte the kernel wrote into tracee memory as a side effect.
TaskId(u32) Stable task identity assigned at clone(2) interception, starting at 1 for the initial thread. Invariant across record and replay; never equals the OS pid/tid which may differ between runs. Used as the primary per-task index in the trace.
Ticks(u64) RCB count for a given TaskId at a given TraceFrame. Monotonic non-decreasing per task across the whole trace (I2). The sole addressing mechanism for positions within a task's execution. Ticks are per-task; they are not globally comparable across tasks without a SchedPoint anchor.
TracedTask Per-thread supervisor state: { task_id: TaskId, os_tid: pid_t, ticks: Ticks, regs: RecordedRegs, stop_state: PtraceStop, perf_fd: RawFd }. Created on clone interception; destroyed on exit.
TraceFrame The fundamental unit of the trace: { event_id: EventId, tid: TaskId, ticks: Ticks, kind: EventKind, regs: RecordedRegs, data_ref: DataRef }. Fixed-size header in events.log; variable payload in data.log at data_ref.
TraceReader Seekable reader over a trace directory. Supports: sequential iteration by EventId, seek_by_event_id(EventId), seek_by_ticks(TaskId, Ticks). Validates CRC per frame; skips unknown tags (P8).
TraceWriter Append-only writer over a trace directory. Assigns EventId monotonically; writes TraceFrame to events.log; appends variable payload to data.log; flushes CRC; fsync at checkpoint boundaries (P7).
TLS (thread-local storage) Per-thread data accessed via fs_base on x86-64 Linux ABI. fs_base is captured in RecordedRegs at every stop; set via arch_prctl or PTRACE_SETREGSET on context-switch during replay to restore TLS pointer exactly.
vDSO / vsyscall Kernel-mapped code pages providing fast user-space implementations of time-related syscalls (gettimeofday, clock_gettime, time, getcpu). Return wall-time or CPU values that are nondeterministic. dret-record patches the tracee's vDSO mappings (recorded in mmap.log as PatchVsyscall events) to trampoline through the supervisor, which injects recorded values (Phase 10).
waitpid demux The supervisor's main event loop: calls waitpid(-1, WNOHANG | __WALL) to collect ptrace stops from any child; dispatches by (pid, stop-reason bitmask) to the correct TracedTask handler. Implemented in dret-ptrace/tracer.rs.

Signal & Scheduling Mechanics

Term Definition
SIGCHLD Signal sent to parent when a child process changes state (exits, stops, continues). The supervisor must filter its own SIGCHLD from the tracee's; handled in dret-ptrace waitpid demux.
SA_RESTORER Signal handler restorer trampoline address in sigaction. Recorded as part of SignalRecord context; replayed to allow the tracee's signal frame to unwind correctly without calling the real restorer side-effects.
PTRACE_SYSCALL ptrace request to resume a tracee and stop at the next syscall entry or exit boundary. Used in record mode; generates SyscallEntry / SyscallExit PtraceStop events consumed by the supervisor. Costlier than PTRACE_CONT; incurred for every syscall.
PTRACE_CONT Resume tracee without stopping at syscall boundaries. Used during replay for non-syscall stretches between SchedPoints; cheaper than PTRACE_SYSCALL.
PTRACE_SETOPTIONS Batch-sets tracer options in one call. dret-ptrace installs: PTRACE_O_EXITKILL, PTRACE_O_TRACESYSGOOD, PTRACE_O_TRACECLONE, PTRACE_O_TRACEFORK, PTRACE_O_TRACEEXEC, PTRACE_O_TRACEVFORK.
**SIGTRAP PTRACE_EVENT_***
sched_setaffinity Linux syscall to pin a process/thread to a CPU set. Applied by dret-sched to force the entire tracee process tree onto one logical CPU (P4, I6). Reversed on trace completion to avoid leaving tracee pinned if it outlives the session.
logical clock divergence A replay run where Ticks(RCB) at a given EventId differs from the recorded value. Caused by: different binary (wrong build-id), undeterministically executed instruction, or cpuid/rdtsc injection failure. Detected by dret-divergence; abort on first occurrence.
seccomp SECCOMP_RET_TRACE A seccomp filter action that forwards the syscall to the ptracer rather than executing or blocking it. Used to intercept rdtsc/cpuid with lower overhead than PTRACE_SYSCALL polling. Requires the tracer to have PTRACE_SEIZE or be the parent.
perf_event overflow PMU counter overflow at the programmed sample period triggers a SIGIO or SIGPERF to the tracer, used as the tick-boundary interrupt. Must be re-armed after each use; dret-time manages fd lifecycle and re-arm in a race-free window.
PTRACE_PEEKDATA / PTRACE_POKEDATA Word-at-a-time tracee memory read/write. Used for inserting software breakpoints (Phase 13) and reading small values. For bulk memory transfer, /proc/pid/mem pread/pwrite is preferred (dret-mem).
exec stop A ptrace stop triggered when the tracee calls execve(2) successfully; PTRACE_EVENT_EXEC. The new address space is live. dret-record must re-read /proc/pid/maps, reset AddressSpace, and restart tick counters for the task.
group-stop A ptrace stop that applies to all threads of a thread group simultaneously (SIGSTOP, SIGTSTP, SIGTTIN, SIGTTOU). Must be distinguished from signal-delivery stops by examining PTRACE_GETSIGINFO si_code.
ptrace-stop Any state in which a tracee is stopped and the tracer must call ptrace/waitpid to observe it. Five kinds: syscall-entry, syscall-exit, signal-delivery, group-stop, ptrace-event. Each demands a different handler in dret-ptrace/stop.rs.

Trace Storage Terms

Term Definition
events.log The primary trace log: sequence of fixed-size (64-byte) TraceFrame records in EventId order. Framed with CRC32. Seekable by byte offset via the in-memory index.
data.log Variable-length payload store. SyscallRecord mem_effects, SignalRecord si_info, and raw page bytes are appended here; their offsets become DataRef values stored in TraceFrame.data_ref. Append-only; never rewritten.
mmap.log Address-space event log: one record per mmap/munmap/mremap/brk/mprotect/exec syscall; used to reconstruct AddressSpace at any EventId during replay seek (Phase 11).
ckpt/ Checkpoint subdirectory: one index file (ckpt/index.bin mapping EventId → byte offset in ckpt/data.bin) and one page slab file (ckpt/pages.bin). Written by dret-trace/checkpoint_store.rs.
header.json Human-readable trace metadata: recorder version, kernel version, build-id list, record timestamp, tracee argv, CPU model. Validated at replay open.
pack file Output of dret pack: a single archive containing the trace directory plus all binary images referenced by build-id. Enables replay on a machine without the original binaries installed.
frame CRC CRC32C computed over the frame header + payload; appended as the last 4 bytes of each events.log frame. TraceReader validates on read; rejects corrupt frames before returning to caller.
forward-compatible tag A 16-bit type field in a frame header that allows a newer writer to add frame kinds an older reader does not understand; the older reader skips the entire frame payload using the stored length. Ensures P8 at the wire level.

Reverse Debugging Terms

Term Definition
reverse-step RSP bs packet: move execution one instruction backward. Implemented by: find nearest prior Checkpoint before current EventId; replay-forward to (current_event_id - 1). Returns register state at that event.
reverse-continue RSP bc packet: run backward until a breakpoint or watchpoint condition is true. Implemented by binary-search over checkpoints: replay-forward from each until the condition fires or the current position is reached.
watchpoint Triggers when a memory address is written. dret-gdb implements via replay-forward with per-event MemDelta scan: replay proceeds until a MemDelta covering the watched address appears, then stops.
checkpoint granularity The interval (in events or wall seconds) between written Checkpoints. Smaller granularity = faster seek / reverse execution; larger granularity = smaller trace. Default: every 50k events or every 5 seconds of record time, whichever first.
replay-forward Resume a ReplaySession from a given Checkpoint state, consuming TraceFrames in EventId order, until a target EventId or Ticks is reached. The primitive underlying seek, reverse-step, and reverse-continue.
session fork Creating a second ReplaySession from a Checkpoint snapshot without re-running from trace start. Used internally by dret-gdb reverse execution to explore multiple paths efficiently.

Every leaf file in the repository tree. Paths relative to repo root. Consistent with section 10 layout. Ordered by crate, then supporting infrastructure, then tests and tooling.
